### Z.

Sorcerers, who are enchanted by the wine and spirit of the outer visible <term>Principle|In Behmenist philosophy, a "Principle" refers to one of the three fundamental realms of existence: the dark fire-world, the light-world, and the outer visible world</term>, and who in turn enchant others. 139. The witch of Endor is the outer visible world, the Babylon outside of us, which we should no longer touch. <margin>153</margin>

Destruction; the destruction or dissolution of our spirit and soul must precede before the renewal can follow. 114. According to the testimony of Scripture, it is unavoidably necessary. 114. It does not happen through elemental or hellish means, but through the Love-Fire, which alone consumes evil, sin, and selfhood and drives out the evil spirits. 115. That this service of destruction happens through John the Baptist, to prepare the way for the birth or reappearance of Jesus in the spirit. 119. That this work of destruction is attributed to the Holy Trinity of Wisdom alone. 126. And although such a work is indeed attributed to the Name of Jesus in the New Testament, the Spirit of Jesus and the Spirit of Wisdom are one Spirit. 128. Wisdom’s fiery service of destruction is a preparatory service for the coming of Jesus in the Spirit, just as the service of Elijah or John the Baptist was before His coming in the flesh. 129/134. Wisdom’s day of destruction brings with it the total eradication of all earthly, devilish, and hellish spirits in man. 832 <note>Original: 832; likely a typographical error for 132 or 140</note>/140. On this day of destruction of Wisdom, everything built by man—and not by her Spirit—upon the foundation of Jesus in us must be consumed in the fire, according to 1 Corinthians 3:13. <margin>143</margin>

Coming; that the coming of Jesus in the flesh happened in weakness; but the coming in Spirit and power is yet to happen. 119. What the sign of His coming in the flesh was. 141/142. Because the coming of Jesus in the Spirit, and of the Holy Spirit in our impure spirit and soul, cannot happen [without preparation], the day of Wisdom must precede to purify them and prepare the way for them. <margin>163/171</margin>

# Four Small Treatises
### By the Blessed
# John Pordage
### Doctor of Medicine
### Left behind in manuscripts:
### And now translated for the pleasure of those who love the truth:

->The First<-
### I. Of the Outer Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.

->The Second<-
### II. Of the Mystical and Inward Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ, or His birth in us, and our birth in Him. Wherein the nature of Rebirth is opened from its foundation.

->The Third<-
### III. Of the Spirit of Faith, and of the different degrees and kinds of Faith.

->The Fourth<-
### IV. Experimental Discoveries of the Union of Natures, Essences, <term>Tinctures|A mystical term referring to the spiritual life-force or "color" of a substance or soul</term>, Bodies, Persons, and Spirits: to which are prefixed several dogmas concerning the outer and inner man.

> Matthew 24:5, 26
> Many will come in My name and say, "I am Christ," and will deceive many. Therefore if they say to you: "Look, He is in the inner rooms," do not believe it. For as the lightning comes from the east and shines even to the west, so also will the coming of the Son of Man be. For wherever the carcass is, there the eagles will be gathered together.

Printed in the Year of Salvation of Jesus Christ, 1704. And to be found in Amsterdam at the shop of Rudolph and Gerhard Wetstein, in Calf Street.

# PREFACE
### From the Translator to the Lovers of the Truth contained in these Writings.

Kind Reader.

Just as it once happened with the restoration of the external, figurative Temple—which, on account of the abominable apostasy and sin of the people, and especially of the blind priests, was ravaged, torn down, and destroyed by wild beasts in the wrath of God—where the souls released from Babylon <note>The translator uses "Babylon" or "Babel" to refer both to the biblical exile and to a state of spiritual confusion and false religious systems.</note> certainly possessed a good will, yet lacked sufficient wisdom, ability, and strength; and moreover, they lacked the true cement with which they might firmly join and lock the stones together after they had been well-hewn and prepared; and beyond all this, they were surrounded on all sides by enemies, and out of fear of them, they had to build (so to speak) with only one hand, while wielding the sword [which today is solely Love and Prayer] in the other: just so, and perhaps even more miserably, does it now occur; after the infinite Love of God has taken merciful pity on the abominable desolation of His Temple in the hearts and souls of men, and has shown how it may be built up again and brought to its initial glory, such as was held in the Apostles and Saints.

Thus, one may truly say that things proceed poorly and are troubled on all sides (especially because there is "no king in Israel" <note>A reference to the Book of Judges (21:25), implying a state of spiritual anarchy where everyone follows their own will.</note>; and everyone fancies themselves to have already departed from Babylon and to be free before they even know or <term>experimentally|In this context, "experimentally" (Latin: experimentaliter) refers to knowledge gained through direct spiritual experience or "living through" a trial, rather than mere book-learning.</term> recognize what Babylon is; and thus they glory in being resurrected before they have ever truly felt the sufferings of Christ—to say nothing of having completely died to their <term>selfhood|The original German term "Eigenheit" refers to the ego, self-will, or the fallen nature that seeks its own interest rather than God's.</term> with Him). For not only do Sanballat, Tobias, and Geshem <note>These figures were the historical enemies of Nehemiah who opposed the rebuilding of Jerusalem's walls through mockery and conspiracy (Nehemiah 2:19).</note> want to help build—for they say they also seek the true God just as Nehemiah does—but there are also those who seek to force their way into this construction who...

<summary>The translator likens the spiritual renewal of the human soul to the historical rebuilding of the Jerusalem Temple, noting that modern seekers often lack the necessary spiritual "cement" and face many internal and external enemies.</summary>
<keywords>Temple restoration, Babylon, Nehemiah, Selfhood, spiritual experience, Love and Prayer, internal temple</keywords>

# P R E F A C E.

...to draw the precious grace of Jesus Christ toward the most frivolous wantonness and toward such godless, fleshly lusts, the likes of which have never been heard of in any century <note>original: "Seculo"</note>. Now, to point out their horrific error in the Light of God to these frivolous and seductive blind spirits—and especially to the poor souls led astray by them—one would have gladly released these four little tracts into public print sooner, had one not been hindered and forced to wait until God Himself opened the door for it and thereby appointed a publisher. Such publishers are now quite rare and difficult to find for the sake of impartial truth; for this same reason, the author’s work *The Angelic World* <note>original: "Englische Welt." In this era, "Englisch" often meant "Angelic" (Engelisch), referring to the heavenly or angelic realm.</note>, which has been ready for the press for a long time and for which the seekers of truth were given hope some time ago, still remains unpublished.

I do not wish to hope that anyone shall have reason to complain about any harsh expressions, partiality, darkness, or lack of clarity within these pages, as is commonly done when their feeble eye of reason is unable to perceive the mysteries of the Spirit of God, nor can they understand His language. Those who might do so would certainly betray themselves as still lacking the Spirit that arises from rebirth—according to the testimony of the Epistle of Jude, verse 19—and as still living merely from their soulish <term>soulish|Refers to the Greek "psychikos," describing the natural human mind or ego as opposed to the "pneumatikos" or spiritual person.</term> or natural powers and abilities; thus, they will find the dangerous state in which they stand clearly depicted in these little tracts. May God grant it for their actual improvement! To Him I most faithfully commit them and all of us, and I remain, as always, so long as I shall be here:

->The loving and servant-minded friend<-
->of all sincere souls.<-

Given on June 8th, 1704, being the 3rd Sunday after Trinity—when, indeed, much is preached concerning the lost sheep and the lost coin [which signify the Divine Image within us], but little (alas!) concern is shown for how they might be found again!

### The First Little Tract

## On the External Birth and
## Incarnation of Jesus Christ.

->The Introduction.<-

In the teaching of the Gospel, or the New Testament, Jesus Christ is presented as the adequate object <note>original: "adæquatum Objectum"</note>, or the perfect aim of a Christian’s faith; and this is shown in these six particular circumstances:

I. In His Birth.
II. In His Life.
III. In His Death and Suffering.
IV. In His Resurrection.
V. In His Ascension.
VI. In His Return.

Here it is to be noted and considered that between these six particular circumstances there is an inseparable dependence or hanging of one upon the other, whether we move forward or backward.

For His Birth was a preparation for His holy and sinless life in this world. His miraculous life here was a preparation for His death on the cross; and this was a preparation for His resurrection. This resurrection cleared a path for His actual ascension to His state of glorification; and this in turn for His return. And so it is also in reverse: Jesus Christ, I say, could never have been able to descend again unless He had first ascended; nor could He have ascended unless He had first actually [been present] with His soul...

The text then begins the first treatise, outlining the six essential stages of Christ's existence from birth to his eventual return.</summary>

# The First Little Treatise of the
<meta>catchword: First</meta>

...soul from hell / and with His body from the grave / He had risen. He could never have risen / if He had not first truly died and suffered death on the cross / and been buried in the grave beforehand. Nor could He have died / had He not previously lived in the world. So also, He could not have lived in the world / if He had not first been born into this world from the body of the Virgin Mary. And in this way it now clearly appears / that each one of these circumstances refers to the other and depends inseparably upon the other.

It is therefore every Christian's bounden duty / to make Jesus Christ the object <term>object|German: "Vorwurffe," here meaning the focus or subject matter of faith.</term> of his faith for eternal life and salvation, to trust in Him and to rest upon Him; and indeed not only in His birth / life and death: but also in His actual resurrection from death / in His actual ascension into heaven and finally also in His return. For all of these together unite / and constitute a complete adequate object <note>original: "adæquatum Objectum"</note> or perfect object of a believer's faith / in the person of Jesus Christ.

Now let us endeavor to prove the truth of each of these circumstances separately from the New Testament.

I. The First Circumstance / that Jesus Christ was actually born / and truly or essentially came in the flesh; so that He is now not to come / but has already come.

And this is proven in this way: Namely from Matthew 1:23: Behold, a virgin / shall be with child / and shall bear a son. This virgin is / as is evidently clear to see / none other / than Mary / the betrothed wife of Joseph / who is mentioned in verse 20. As also appears at the first glance of verse 21: And she shall bear a son / and you shall call His name Jesus: compared with verse 25: She bore her first

But in what this favor consists, we read in detail in the words following immediately: Behold / he says further / you will be with child / and bear a son / and call His name Jesus. These words are indeed only a prophecy of the angel to the blessed virgin; which we find exactly and specifically fulfilled in the very next chapter: for there in Luke 2:10, 11, 12 the angel's message to the shepherds reads thus: Fear not; for behold I bring you tidings of great joy / which shall be to all people: for unto you is born this day in the city of David a Savior / who is Christ the Lord. What could be clearer / than this account / along with its circumstances / to testify to and prove the birth of Jesus Christ which actually occurred? For in the preceding part of this chapter (of which these words are but a result /) we have the stated time of His birth described / that it happened under Augustus, the Roman Emperor / verse 1. when Herod / the Great / Tetrarch <note>original: "Vierfürst"</note> or King in Judea / and Cyrenius [Quirinus] was Governor in Syria verse 2. compared with Matthew 2:1 <note>The original text cites "Matth. 21," likely a printing error for the birth narrative in Matthew 2:1.</note>. And the heavenly hosts; verse 13. So that / if we were to actively and intentionally deny the actual birth of Jesus Christ / we would have to roundly deny / that the history of the Gospel was not the true history.

Again also Romans 1:3. Of His Son Jesus Christ / our Lord / who was born—

# The First Little Treatise of the

...born <note>The text continues from the previous page: "...as is evident, that He was born..."</note> of the seed of David / according to the flesh: This is likewise a clear testimony / that His birth according to the flesh truly occurred and is now a past event.

Finally also Galatians 4:4: But when the fullness of the time was come / God sent His Son / born of a woman / that is / born of the seed of a woman. And that this is mentioned as having happened / when the time was fulfilled / clearly indicates this much / namely / that just as Jesus Christ was truly promised by God Himself / as the promised seed / several thousand years before His coming / that He should come; as we read in Genesis 3:15 / that the seed of the woman should crush the serpent's head: so the Gospel reveals to us / that Jesus Christ (who is that very same promised seed / of whom all the prophets spoke in their prophecies / saying that He should come) finally truly came / and was actually born into the world. And this may hopefully be enough for a clear proof of the birth of Jesus Christ / that it truly and essentially took place.

However, so that this highly important truth / upon which so much depends / may be rightly applied and used by all true Christians / one must earnestly weigh and consider three special circumstances.

1. That Jesus Christ was conceived and born with two natures; namely with His Heavenly / and with His Human nature. For thus the Holy John teaches us in his Gospel, Chapter 1:14: And the Word became flesh. The Word indicates His Deity or Divine nature: And flesh / His Human nature or humanity: as we intend to testify more clearly under the third of these circumstances. But what we should understand by these words / *is become flesh* / must be the work of the second circumstance.

Also, to say nothing of others / Holy Paul teaches us in 1 Timothy 3:16: That God was revealed in the flesh; where God and flesh again clearly signify two

# Outer Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.

two natures; and as clearly / as the Creator and the creature are distinct. But what is meant and to be understood by the revelation of the one in the other / I now come to consider in my

2. Circumstance / which is / that these two natures of Jesus Christ were clothed in flesh in close union with one another in the body of His mother / and He brought them from thence / so inseparably and indissolubly united / with Himself into the visible world.

This close union of these two natures is a secret and hidden union / which is not so easily explained with words / nor depicted through parables / nor even to be grasped and understood through common reasons or knowledge. The teachers of the Scripture might well call it / as they indeed do / a most exact union / an altogether inseparable and most indissoluble union. And yet, as secret and inexplicable as this union is / so seemingly simple and common is the language of Scripture regarding it and when the occasion arises. The Word was flesh / says Holy John; and Holy Paul: God is revealed in the flesh. For by "flesh" in both places is understood the Human nature. And by "the Word" / in Holy John must (more properly) be understood the second person in the Godhead <note>A reference to the Trinity; the "Word" or *Logos* is the Son.</note> / of whom it is said for the sake of distinction / that He was (in the beginning) with God, John 1:1. But if that is so; how and in what sense could this blessed Word then become flesh? To which I answer / precisely through this: that it was united and became one with the same: and indeed not / that it thereby changed its nature / for that was impossible: but through a life-union / which indicates such a change just as little as the state of marriage / whereby the man and the wife become one flesh, Genesis 2:24. Or we may say / it became flesh through and because of its indwelling in the same / according to John 1:14; where the original word <note>Original Greek: "eskēnōsen" (ἐσκήνωσεν), meaning to pitch a tent or tabernacle.</note> / which the translators give: dwelt among

# The First Little Treatise of the

...testified <note>original catchword: "ten", likely completing "bezeuget" (testified)</note> among us / actually means: He raised up His Tabernacle or booth within us; just as He had previously done in the Jewish Temple. Accordingly, just as God was revealed through His indwelling in the same under the Law as an example; so also under the Gospel through the assumption of our fleshly nature / as a booth or house / to dwell therein: Also 1 Timothy 3:16 testifies of Him / that He was revealed in the flesh. And thus the Godhead of Christ is revealed / so that It might be in union with His humanity; and consequently also His humanity in union with the Godhead. Both natures are so distinctly and inseparably bound together by the law of this union / that one cannot exist without the other; and what God has thus joined together stands not in the power of any created being to divide.

3. Circumstance. These two natures of **Jesus Christ** / His Divinity and His Humanity / are / despite this close union in themselves / nevertheless distinct from one another. For although the Word indeed became flesh / yet it nevertheless remained the Word / and the flesh still flesh. The difference is essential / and consequently unchangeable. And thus again / although God was indeed revealed in the flesh; yet neither was the flesh God / nor God the flesh / but only united therewith. That is, in a word: Christ’s Divine nature is not His Human nature / nor His Human nature His Divine: but they both remained and remain in His conception / birth and life both here / as well as how they are now in heaven / in themselves without change / mixing or confusion / distinct: as the texts cited above clearly prove and demonstrate to an impartial mind.

From the manner and way of the birth of **Jesus Christ** into this world arise these following Six Mysteries.

1. Mystery. The first / that offers itself for our consideration

<column-break/>

## outer Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.

...itself <note>original catchword: "heim-", likely continuing "Geheimnus" (Mystery) from the previous page</note> is the Mystery of His Divine nature / how it is in itself distinct and divided from His Human nature.

And if one should ask what It is? I can only answer / that the Divine nature of **Jesus Christ** (as It is in Its bare, pure essence) is the Godhead itself flowing out from the center of the Holy Trinity. But the center of the Holy Trinity is the middle or second number <note>The author refers to the second person of the Trinity</note> / and is called the Word of God or God / the Word / and / for the sake of an even fuller distinction / the Son of God. That we give Him these titles / Holy Scripture will not only justify us; but also give us guidance to assert even more expressly / that He is God / true God / and God Himself / co-eternal with the Father / co-essential and equal to Him / Who was with Him in the beginning / through whom / with and in the Father / all things were made / and without whom nothing was made / that is made / John 1:2, 3, 4. Yes, we may go even further / and add / that He is even more properly the essential light / the essential life / the essential wisdom / the essential love / the essential righteousness / the essential power of God the Father / and in a word / the Godhead of **Jesus Christ** / according to His order and dignity in the Holy Trinity / called the bright radiance of the Father's glory / and the express image of His person. So high and well-qualified is He (with the Father and the Holy Spirit) that He is the Creator / Protector and Sustainer of all things / as the sense and content of Holy Scripture represents Him throughout. For according to the Divine nature He is the very same Yesterday / Today and Forever / having been with the patriarchs / prophets and holy men at all times / and in all generations / as is clearly evident from His union and co-eternal existence or essentiality with the Father / before all visible and invisible creations.

<meta>This page continues the theological distinction between the two natures of Christ. The previous page established that while the Divine and Human natures are inseparably united, they remain distinct. This page warns against "confusing" the two (an old heresy known as Eutychianism) and begins to break down the specific components of Christ's humanity—Spirit, Soul, and Body—to show He was fully human in every faculty.</meta>

# The First Little Treatise of the
# Outer Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ

mystery <note>original: "heimnüsses"; the catchword "heit" from the previous page likely completed a word like "Christianity" or "Humanity", and this page begins mid-sentence regarding the "mystery."</note> / and in the manner of an addition or consequence of what has already been said / we cannot say of this Divine nature—viewed as separate from the humanity—that it is, strictly speaking, **Jesus Christ**. That is, the Divine nature is not **Jesus Christ** in His whole and complete personhood; rather, it is only one part of His person, though an essential part. Anyone who wished to claim otherwise would become the author of a fundamental error and harmful consequence; for this would be to claim that the humanity was swallowed up by the divinity and mixed together with it into confusion and disorder. Indeed, it would give cause and occasion for a paradox or strange opinion which is an obvious contradiction or impossibility in both theology and even in philosophy.

**2. Mystery.** The Second Mystery (which is indeed lower in degree than the preceding one) is the mystery of His Humanity, or Human Nature. Regarding this, and for a better explanation of this circumstance, I will first say in general that Human Nature is composed of and consists of these three essential parts: namely, **Spirit, Soul, and Body**. I then establish and maintain that **Jesus Christ** in particular possessed each of these: namely, an eternal Spirit, an immortal Soul, and these dwelt together in a body of flesh as if in a house or dwelling place. And this is nothing other than what the Holy Scripture teaches, as we wish to show more clearly in the following circumstances; such as:

1. **Jesus Christ**, as He was the Son of Man, had His own inherent Spirit within Himself, together with the essential instrumental faculties belonging to Him; such as His own understanding and His own will, which were distinct from His Heavenly ones. And indeed, 1. regarding His understanding <note>or "intellect"</note>, we read in **Luke 2:40**: *And the child grew and became strong in spirit, filled with wisdom*

<column-break/>

<page-num>9</page-num>
# Outer Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.

&c. These words, I say, teach us that **Jesus Christ** had His own particular Spirit belonging to Him as a man; and that this Spirit of His humanity was a spirit distinct from the Spirit of His divinity. Also, that this intellectual or rational Spirit which He had as a man was filled with wisdom, and grew and increased in all knowledge from degree to degree. The grace of His Divine nature was always with His human Spirit to sanctify, purify, and enlighten it with His own Divine and Essential Light, filling it with wisdom and knowledge. 

2. And then, that He also had an Eternal Will-Spirit, which again was distinct from His Divine Will as God, is evident even more immediately and clearly from all those passages that speak of His yieldedness <note>original: "Gelassenheit", a term for total surrender to God's will</note> or self-denial and submission under His Heavenly Father, and the seeking of the Father's will in contrast to His own will. As for example in **Matthew 26:39**: *Yet not as I will, but as You will.* And again in **John 5:30**: *Because I seek not my own will, but the will of the Father who has sent Me.* As also in **John 4:34**: *My food is that I do the will of Him who sent Me.* In all these places, two wills that are set over against one another are expressly mentioned: The Human Will—that is, the will of the man **Christ Jesus**, which He calls "His will" or "His own will"—and then the Divine Will, which He expresses through the words: "Your will," or "the Father's will," and "the will of Him who sent Me." This Divine Will, or the Will of the Father, is essentially one and the same will as the Will of Christ Himself insofar as He is **GOD**. And therefore, in view of this Divine Will, it would be an obvious contradiction for the Son to have said to the Father: *not as I will, but as You will.*

<summary>This page explains that Jesus Christ is a union of both Divine and Human natures, warning against the error of thinking His divinity "swallowed" His humanity. It argues that as a true man, Christ possessed a distinct human spirit, intellect, and will, which grew in wisdom and surrendered to the Divine will.</summary>

...wills, etc., because / as they are of one and the same Essence / they can also have only one and the same will; and as they are equal to one another / the will of the one / with any reasonable use of language / cannot be said to subject and surrender itself to the other and to be yielded <note>original: "gelassen"; a reference to *Gelassenheit*, a central concept in German mysticism meaning a state of selfless surrender to God's will.</note>. Therefore, the will / which **Christ** here denies / kills / and surrenders to His Father / is a will / which He held as His own for Himself / in regard to which He is said to be smaller or lower than the Father / because He was only a part of that nature / through which He / at His **First Coming** into the world / was made a little lower or lesser / than the angels. Had **Jesus Christ** been without this Eternal Spirit / without this understanding and without this will / He would not have been a true human / as we are / nor could He have called us brothers. But if someone should say (as some indeed have actually done) that the Divine nature of **Christ** could have replaced the lack or the place of His eternal spirit / of which we have just spoken / and also the office of its essential instrumental powers, understanding, and will; then the answer is already all too clear; namely, that the dispute here is not about the expansion of Almighty power / whether God could have raised up a body of flesh / that could have seen / recognized / willed / and / in a word / done and performed everything / that is characteristic of Human Nature / without the principles / powers and instrumental faculties of the same: but this is finally the unavoidable consequence; that if God had been revealed in the flesh of **Jesus Christ** in no other way / He would not have taken on our nature.

2. The Second Essential Part of **Christ** / as a human / is His **Eternal Soul** / which likewise / like His eternal spirit / is distinct from His Divinity: This is also abundantly clear from Scripture, Matthew 26 v. 38: *My soul is exceedingly sorrowful even unto death.* So, therefore, **Jesus**

**Christ** did not only have 1. a soul / in general; *My soul* etc. But 2. a Human soul of the same passions and affections / or movements and inclinations of the heart and soul / as our own: for it is *exceedingly sorrowful* etc. And likewise John 12 v. 27: *Now is My soul troubled.* What can His joy / His sadness / death-struggle and the like indicate and make known other than / that He had all passions and affections just as we have: all of which are but so many branches of the Human soul. Here again is exactly the same reason for the assertion (against the <term>Eutychians</term> <note>Followers of Eutyches (c. 380–456 AD), who argued that Christ's human nature was absorbed into his divine nature, leaving him with only one nature.</note>) that He had a Human soul as we have / as was said before / that He had an eternal spirit: for how else could He have been made equal to us? Otherwise He would have been more like a ghost / than like Human Nature. The other texts / which I have already cited above: such as Luke 1 v. 31, Romans 1 v. 3, Galatians 4 v. 4, compared with Genesis 3 v. 15, further confirm our statement abundantly: For by the word / **Flesh** / and the **Seed of the woman** / the whole Human Nature is understood in Scripture: Unless / we could assume / that **God** would enter into a covenant with a purely mute, unfeeling, and irrational being / and require from the same / through public and express ordinances of rewards and punishments / to obey the Moral or Ethical laws / and to hold them in honor: Which in truth are thoughts / that indicate or make known neither a serious / nor a healthy mind. In a word / because He (excepting only sin) had all the imperfections / yes, even the weaknesses of our nature / but not sin / because it in no way belongs to the essence of that nature; it follows / that He lacked nothing which could make Him both a perfect human / and **God** / and consequently also [possessed] a Human soul.

# The First Little Treatise of the

...humanity of Christ belongs His **fleshly body**. For since He was of the **seed of David** <note>original: "Saamen Davids"; a biblical reference to Jesus's royal and human lineage from King David.</note> according to the flesh (Romans 1:3); since He took the **form of a servant** and became like men (Philippians 2:7); since He, just like the children (according to the conclusion of the same Apostle in Hebrews 2:14), became a partaker of flesh and blood; and even more specifically, did not take on the nature of angels, but the **seed of Abraham** (v. 16); since He, I say, had to become like His brothers in all things (v. 17)—then He must indeed have had a body like ours. 

The teaching of the Scripture is as clear and distinct as it can possibly be: that **Jesus Christ** had a **natural and earthly body**, a body consisting of **flesh, blood, and bones, etc.**, a body provided with all **instrumental limbs** <note>The author uses "instrumental" to describe the body parts as tools through which the soul acts in the physical world.</note> according to its outward form, shape, and figure, in the very likeness of our coarse and visible bodies. This coarse, visible, and tangible body was the **casing** or the **house** in which His soul and spirit dwelt invisibly. 

When I say that He had a body in the likeness of our body, I mean by this that He had all and the very same essential properties, as well as His own correct measure, qualities, and the like; that He also had all the common weaknesses of all other bodies clinging to Him and similar inseparable accompanying properties; and that He was subject to hunger, thirst, toil, sleep, and the similar inseparable properties of an **elementary body** <note>A body made of the physical elements of the earth.</note>. For it was in view of the sensation of all these weaknesses that it was said of **Jesus Christ** by the Apostle (Hebrews 4:15) that He was **tempted in all points just as we are, yet without sin**. Tempted indeed in truth, as the entire history of His life sufficiently reveals; yet not overcome, and thus consequently without sin.

In order that we may briefly summarize everything we have said, there are three essential or form-and-essence-

<meta>catchword: giving</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>13</page-num>
<header>outer Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.</header>

-giving parts that belong to the completion of **Human Nature**, namely: **Spirit, Soul, and Body**, each of which is distinct from the others. **Jesus Christ** had each and every one of these; and was, consequently, essentially distinct in nothing from the rest of the children of men; and therefore became a human being in the most proper sense.

**3rd Mystery.** And this, briefly, is the exact union of these two natures in the person of **Jesus Christ**, so much so that they are never to be separated for all eternity. But I have already said something of this, and will mention it here only for the sake of the intended method of teaching and order: for my intention is to explain and express the matter to be handled as precisely as I can, even though I might be accused of speaking in tautologies <note>Saying the same thing twice in different words.</note> or of always expressing one and the same thing with other words. 

Therefore, so that I do not delay the reader with the learned phrase **hypostatic union** <note>original: "hypostatica unio"; the theological term for the union of Christ's divine and human natures in one individual existence.</note>, or **personal union**, I will here only add to what has already been said: that Christ's **Divine nature** penetrated into the **instrumental powers** of the **Human nature**, such as the understanding, will, etc., thereby to infuse them with His own Divine power; and these same instrumental powers in turn (because they are penetrating powers) penetrate into the Divine nature to receive its influence or power. Thus, in the manner of an infusion, they are, as it were, spiritually **incorporated** into one another. The union is in truth so secret and hidden that words are not able to reach it; most words that one has give only an **unequal or incomplete and dissimilar sense** <note>original: "inadæquatum & analogicum sensum"; meaning our language can only provide metaphors or analogies that fall short of the divine reality.</note>.

**4th Mystery.** From this, however, a fourth mystery sprouts forth. For these two natures remain, despite this exact union, nonetheless distinct from one another within the person of Christ. They remain, I say, distinct, without change; the **essences** of things are unchangeable; and

and therefore a change of one into the other is impossible. 2. **Without confusion or disorder**; because for the very same reason it is likewise impossible that one should be lost in the other, or swallowed up by the other. 3. **Without blending or mixing**; because all mixing conflicts with the essential union of both natures, and is ultimately nothing but a childish fiction. But because we have already spoken somewhat of this main point before, I will only add this here to be noted: that among **theologians** <term>theologians: scholars who study the nature of God and religious belief</term> (especially those of the **mystical order** <note>The "mystical order" refers to a tradition of Christian thought focusing on the direct, internal experience of God's presence.</note>), the first great mystery among all is the **Holy Trinity in Unity**; but the mystery of the **Divinity in Humanity**, and the **Humanity in Divinity**, is the second. And so I proceed to the

5. **Mystery.** Namely, that the two natures of **Jesus Christ**, united with one another in such a way and yet distinct in this union, constitute His own **Personality**, as He is the **God-Man**. He is the lovely **offspring** <note>original: "Sprößling"; here meaning the result or fruit of the union.</note> of both thus united, and He is not solely the Divine nor solely the Human nature in their separate existence. He is, as it were, the **Effect**, and they are the **Cause** of His Person; and therefore they, as such, must have existed before Him, at least in the order of **conception** <term>conception: the moment when Jesus was conceived in the womb of the Virgin Mary</term>. But what does the Scripture say of this? One may read Luke 1:35: *Therefore also that Holy thing which shall be born of thee &c.* What kind of "Holy thing" is this? It cannot be the Divine nature, for that is not capable of being born; nor can it be the Human nature, for this (to say nothing of the fact that it cannot be "the Holy thing" itself) cannot be called **God’s Son**, as this Holy thing was called. Therefore, it must be a **Third** <note>The author argues that the Person of Christ is a "third" entity arising from the union, rather than being just one nature or the other.</note> in which both come together and are united with one another. And again, Matthew 1:20: *For that which is conceived in her is of the Holy Spirit.* The same questions and answers previously made will also occur here again, which may therefore be both laid as a foundation and repeated.

<sig>B 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: migh-</meta>

6. **Mystery.** Let, therefore, the unity of this **Personality** in Jesus Christ—which arises from the union of two such distinct natures—be the **Sixth Mystery**. For He is only **one Person**. If the Human nature in Jesus Christ were sufficient in itself to constitute a personality, then His Divine nature would be even more so; and consequently, there would be two persons, and not just one. This is also evident from the Scripture, which in all those places where it speaks of Jesus Christ, speaks in the **singular number**, which otherwise would have had to happen in the plural number.

7. **Mystery.** But even more specifically: this one Person of Jesus Christ is a **Divine Person** in Himself, distinct from all other men; this is merely a consequence of what preceded. For it is the **essence** of every individual (such as every person is) to be distinct, or to have an existence for oneself separate from all others. The Scripture is also quite clear regarding this piece. One may read Luke 2:12, Matthew 2:14, 16, 2 Timothy 2:5. But why do I need to heap many texts together? It is spoken of Him throughout the entire history of the **New Testament** as a particular person, or one distinct from others, just as of Matthew, Mark, Luke, John, Peter, James, &c. Did not **Judas** betray Him with a kiss, which he did not give to a whole group as a sign, so that they should not fail to find Him, or take one of His disciples for Him? Did not the message that **Pilate** received from his wife testify—that he should have nothing to do with *that just man*—and his own public confession, bright and clear, that He is only **One**? Therefore, to say it is a truth not expressed in Scripture that Jesus Christ is a person distinct from all others in and for Himself, is, to speak plainly, vain and useless chatter that proves nothing. And I wish that those who think otherwise would consider it once more and better.

might [desire]! From this finally arises another, which is the:

8. **Mystery.** From the **numerical difference of the Person of Christ** from all other persons, many **mystery-filled names** and mystical or secret **titles** arise, which are given to Him in the Holy Scripture.

**1. Title.** Firstly, He is called **Θεάνθρωπος** <note>original: "Θεάνθρωπος" (Theanthropos)</note>, **God-Man** or **Man-God**, which names express both natures in union as far as words can do.

**2. Title.** And just so does His other [name], namely **Immanuel**, the name given to Him by the angel in **Matthew 1:23**. Which, translated or explained, is **God in and with us**; that is, in the **human**, not **angelic**, nature (**Hebrews 2:16**).

**3. Title.** When His name **JESUS** was given to Him in **Matthew 1:21**, a reason was immediately added; namely, that by this name was indicated the **office** <term>office: the specific divine role or duty</term> assigned to Him following His **incarnation** <term>incarnation: the act of God taking on a human body and nature</term> or becoming flesh; which was that He should be a **Savior**: For He would save His people from their sins.

**4. Title.** Again, He is also called the **Son of God** in **Matthew 3:17** and **Luke 1:35**, and—to pass over others—also in **John 1:14**. "And we saw His glory, the glory as of the **Only Begotten Son of the Father**." There, the Person being spoken of is the **Word** that had become flesh, and the title given to Him, as being thus clothed in flesh, is the **One begotten by the Father**.

**5. Title.** Just as He is the Son of God, so is He finally also called the **Son of Man** in **John 1:51**. And so frequently elsewhere.

But so that we may consider what has been said even more precisely, we will form this question from it: What then was **JESUS Christ** when He was born from the body of the Virgin, His mother, or into

<column-break/>

## outer Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ.

into this world? Whereupon the full answer is briefly this: that He was a **God-Man**, one Person consisting of two distinct natures—the **Divine** and the **Human**—which were joined together in such a **living union** that they can nevermore be separated. This is the **Middle Way** of Divine Truth; the two side-paths on either side are erroneous.

For example, to say that He was the **Life** and **Light** of the world, the **Wisdom**, **Power**, and **Might** of the Father; and to heap together all His other attributes that belong to Him as God, is indeed to obviously confirm His **Deity**, but to either overlook or deny His **Humanity**. The question was not about the Divine nature of JESUS Christ—what it was. For if it were, the answer would be correct. But rather, what was JESUS Christ? What was the whole and perfect **Person** understood by this name? And in this way, this answer is deficient, and either a careless oversight without any forethought, or, where it was intended as a precise answer, it is obviously **heretical** <term>heretical: a belief that contradicts the established core doctrines of the faith</term>. For it leads us quite clearly away from the Person of JESUS Christ and silently denies His **Human nature**, since He is not the Deity alone, but the **God-Man**.

And so, conversely, on the other side, to give a troublesome answer to the effect that He was a **true man**, had taken our nature upon Himself, had become flesh, etc. And as such had eaten, drunk, slept, associated with people in the world, and traveled from one place to another, and other such things; is nothing other than confirming His **mere humanity**, without any regard for His **Deity**; and therefore, if it were intended to serve as a complete answer, it is the other **heretical** side-path.

Accordingly, we deny, with the **Arians** <note>The Arians were followers of Arius (c. 250–336 AD), who taught that Jesus was a created being and not of the same essence as God the Father.</note>...

-># The First Little Treatise of the<-

Nestorians, Cerinthians, and Socinians <note>These were various theological groups throughout history accused of denying the full divinity of Christ or viewing Him as a "mere man."</note>, [we] do not [deny] the divinity of Christ, or claim that He was not God, nor do we make Him into a mere man. On the other hand, we also do not overstep the goal on the other side with the Eutychians <note>Followers of Eutyches, who believed Christ's human nature was absorbed into His divinity, essentially leaving Him with only one nature.</note> and their followers, so that we—under the pretext of confirming His divinity—should abolish or destroy His humanity.

This last point is blamed upon the Anabaptists <term>Anabaptists: A radical Reformation group; the author here uses the term "Täuffer" (Baptizers).</term> and the Quakers; namely, that they make Jesus Christ to be God alone and not man. If this accusation is true, as some indeed wish to claim, it is certainly their duty to turn away this offense. And since they are eager to have it cleared out of the way, it can (if they are willing to humble themselves to that extent) be done with few words. If they are unwilling, however, it is nonetheless indisputable that it is their duty to give the world satisfaction in one way or another. If they are now rightly accused of this, I have only two things to recommend for their earnest consideration: Namely (1) that they thereby actually deny the entire history of the Gospel to be a true history; and that it is consequently their duty to prove that it is false—which I certainly doubt can be done in this day and age. (2) That not to confess that Jesus Christ has come in the flesh is a clear sign (if we base ourselves on the judgment of St. John the Theologian, 1 Epistle chapter 4 verse 3) of the Antichrist. But should they think they want to save the matter by answering that they speak of Christ in the **mystery**, and not in the **history**; in the **spirit**, and not in the **letter**. To this I answer: that if they wish to rightly maintain and defend the **mystery**, they must necessarily also admit the **history**. The reason appears at the first glance: for the history is the only true foundation upon which the mystery can be built, and without which everything built upon it must collapse of its own accord. In the mirror of the **letter**, they both look upon each other, as it were, face

<meta>catchword: face</meta>

<column-break/>

to face: they point to one another, and one leads to the other; they are defended, strengthened, and confirmed by one another in a perfect union and complete harmony. So that ultimately, unless they wish to contradict themselves and maintain their proposition or opinion with even a single tolerable appearance of reason, they must necessarily either admit and confess both, or flatly deny both. But this is said as if in passing, to return to the:

9. **Mystery.** The ninth and last mystery, which I will add here as the end and conclusion of all others, is the **wonderfulness** of this person of **Jesus Christ**. Among His other titles given to Him by the Prophet **Isaiah 9:6**, the title **Wonderful** is truly one of them; and quite rightly and justly so: for He is such a person that before Him there was absolutely none like Him, nor will there ever be after Him. He was wonderful, 1. because He was chosen, ordained, and foreseen by God the Father in His foreknowledge. For He is that person, and no other, who was named in the purpose and decree of the Spirit of Eternity in the council of the **Holy Trinity**, that He should be the Restorer of the lost human race; the **Second Adam**, or the **Universal Person**, who should thoroughly heal and make good again the great breach of the **First Adam**. **Isaiah 51:1** <note>The author likely intended Isaiah 42:1, which says "Behold my servant... my chosen," as Isaiah 51:1 traditionally refers to pursuing righteousness.</note>: *See my chosen one,* etc. Who is this chosen one of whom it is said here? None but the perfect person of **Jesus Christ**, for which choice His divine nature is certainly incapable <note>The author suggests that the Divine Nature cannot be "chosen" in the same way, as it is the Elector itself, whereas the human/unified person is the object of the decree.</note> and His human nature of itself is quite unsuitable. And where was He thus chosen? In the decree of the Spirit of Eternity; in the foreknowledge of the Holy Trinity, which sees all things beforehand in the eye of eternity as in a mirror—things that have ever been, are present, or are yet to be—and that simultaneously and all at once in a single act. And finally, for what was He chosen?

chosen? Namely, that He should heal the breach which the first Adam had made, and clear the footpath again, so that we might walk within it.

II. Because He is the Seed of Promise, who should crush the head of the serpent (Genesis 3:15). This is such a high privilege <note>original: "Privilegium"</note> that, besides Him, no one has ever presumed to claim it; indeed, even the greatest names among all the Patriarchs, as well as Prophets—such as Enoch, Noah, Abraham, David, etc.—were content to foreshadow Him as the one to whom it rightfully belonged, even if only obscurely and from a distance.

In this promise <term>Verheissung: A central theological term referring to God's pledge of a Savior after the Fall of Man.</term>, we may note these circumstances: 

1. Who made it? And that is, briefly, the Holy Trinity: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit.

2. The persons to whom it was given: Adam and Eve personally, and in them, all their descendants.

3. The time when it happened: Namely, immediately after their fall and disobedience.

4. The characteristics of this promise that follow from it, which are as follows:

**1st Characteristic.** It is certainly the first that was ever given to fallen man. I say to *fallen* man: for if no Fall had occurred, there would have been no need for such a promise. It is the first and greatest promise of the Gospel; the foundation of all subsequent promises, covenants, and prophecies. And it may therefore, in a certain sense, be called the short sum and substance of both the Law and the Gospel.

**2nd Characteristic.** It is accordingly the very oldest of all God's promises; because it occurred so much earlier than any others; long before the Great Flood, and about a thousand years before God’s covenant with Abraham.

**3rd Characteristic.** It is a Universal Promise, for it includes all people, and does not 

---

<page-num>21</page-num>
<header>Outer Birth and Humanity of JESUS Christ.</header>
<sig>B 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: complete-</meta>

exclude a single one. Therefore, we may well call or name it the Great Christian Privilege.

**4th Characteristic.** It is an entirely free and perfect promise. It exists without any convenience or worthiness on the part of Adam and Eve; and can therefore depend alone on the truthfulness, faithfulness, all-sufficiency, and omnipotence of Him who made it. And for this reason, and upon this ground, it may also be called the Evangelical Promise.

**5th Characteristic.** And just as it is an entirely free promise, so is it also a most gracious promise. For there was nothing in Adam, nor in Eve, nothing in man, that could have moved God to it; but rather the opposite, they were in a state of apostasy, of sin, and of eternal death: so that we must conclude that this free grace alone was His essential love and mercy, His own eternal goodness, which moved His eternal, essential Heart of Love to have compassion and mercy upon them in this lost state of misery. Which is immediately a further reason why it is called an Evangelical Promise.

**6th Characteristic.** It is also a future promise; a promise of a thing that was not yet present, but was still to come, and indeed in the fullness of time <note>A biblical phrase (Galatians 4:4) referring to the moment in history when God decided the world was ready for the birth of Christ.</note>.

**7th Characteristic.** It is, finally, an obscure and secret promise. And the reasons why it was described and handed down in such an obscure and mysterious way are as follows: 

**1st Reason:** That neither the dragon himself, nor all other devils together, through all their cunning—even if they should unite with one another ever so secretly—should nevertheless not grasp the full intention and purpose, nor fathom the depths of it even unto this day.

**2nd Reason:** That Adam and Eve themselves should also not complete- <note>The text cuts off here at the catchword "gänz-" (gänzlich), which will continue on the next page as "completely understand."</note>

might understand it completely. They knew in general, to be sure, that it concerned their restoration <term>Wiederbringung: A term central to radical Pietism and mystical theology, referring to the eventual return of all things—specifically fallen humanity—to their original divine state.</term> and that of their fallen descendants, even though the manner and way in which it should happen, and other circumstances, were hidden from them. This is evident from their misunderstanding, in that they applied and interpreted the promise as a fleshly birth, though it was spoken of as a spiritual birth. For when Adam knew his wife, so that she conceived and bore Cain, she immediately claimed: *I have born a man from the LORD.* A man—that is, *the* man, by way of excellence, the promised Seed, the LORD (as the original text reads <note>The author refers to the Hebrew text of Genesis 4:1, which can be read as "I have gotten a man: the LORD," leading some to believe Eve thought her firstborn was the promised Messiah.</note>); who should restore us and redeem us from our present captivity. But when he became a murderer, they also saw their error.

**3rd Reason.** That none of the godless, fleshly, and ungodly might see its full final purpose.

**4th Reason.** That even the wise and clever, with all their rich treasures of learning and knowledge, might not understand it. *I thank Thee, Father* (says our Lord Himself, on just such an occasion, Matthew 11:25), *Lord of heaven and earth, that Thou hast hidden these things from the wise and clever, and hast revealed them unto babes!* For it is indeed the way of Divine Providence, on every occasion, to put the wisdom of this world to shame.

**5th Reason.** That no one but newborn children in Jesus Christ should understand the hidden mystery of the same; that no one but the children of Wisdom should grasp the height, breadth, length, and depth thereof. For these belong among those dark sayings or parables and riddles (Proverbs 1:6) whose explanation or interpretation no one shall have but Her <note>The author likely refers to "Wisdom" (Sophia) as a feminine personification.</note> disciples, and those who stand in Her special grace.

**6th Reason.** That the full and perfect sense and meaning of the same might be revealed from one degree to the next—

<meta>catchword: next</meta>

<page-num>23</page-num>
<header>## Outer Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ</header>

—might be revealed. It opened itself to the age before the Great Flood only quite obscurely; after the flood, it revealed itself to Abraham a little more clearly; and even more fully to the Prophets. However, by comparison, it shone forth incomparably brighter during the birth and ministry of the Son in the New Testament. Yet, throughout this entire time, it has not been revealed in its full glory. For although the work is truly and fully finished in the person of Christ Himself, sin and "selfhood" <note>original: "Selbheit" - a mystical term for the ego or the fallen nature that resists God.</note> and the purpose of the Devil are not yet fully broken in the children of God. Thus, the depth and breadth of this promise is still reserved as a dark word and hidden mystery, until the Day of the Ministry of the Holy Spirit appears and is revealed on earth. At that time, and in that same hour, these things may be unlocked, just as the "height" of the promise has now been revealed.

Hitherto we have considered the promise with regard to: 1. The person who made the promise. 2. To whom it was made. 3. The time when it happened. 4. Its characteristics. Now there remain for us to consider: 5. The final purposes for why it actually happened, and of these there are two:

**1. With regard to mankind:** namely, to comfort and raise up Adam and Eve, so that neither they themselves nor their descendants might utterly despair, even though they had fallen.

**2. With regard to the Devil:** for he had in truth tempted the woman in the form of the serpent, and through the woman also the man, which succeeded for him just as he had wished. But regardless of all this, the promise was made: *The Seed of the woman shall crush the serpent's head.* This Seed tramples everything, and consequently also the Oppressor <note>original: "Untertretter"</note> himself underfoot. These words put his cunning to shame, struck down his pride, and set all of Hell in fear and terror; because they could well imagine that their purpose might not, in the end, achieve its effect. This restrained (at least for that time) their rage and—

6. But let us now also consider for a little while the nature of this promise. **The Seed of the woman shall bruise the head of the serpent**; and to do this all the better, I will expand this sixth circumstance into four others, namely:

**1st Circumstance.** First, it may be asked: who is this crusher or treader of the serpent’s head? Why is He called the "Seed of the woman"? To which the answer is briefly: **JESUS Christ**; not indeed in a divided sense, either as God or man alone, but in His complete personality as Both.

**2nd Circumstance.** The second thing for us to consider is the work or the deed itself. It is a work of destruction, as the words clearly imply, and especially when they are compared with 1 John 3:8 and other similar passages: *For this purpose the Son of God was manifested, that He might destroy the works of the devil.* For nothing else or nothing less can be a fitting preparation for the victory and triumph of the Son of God. He alone is the one of whom it can be said that He can tread upon, break, crush, and destroy the head of the serpent.

**3rd Circumstance.** By the serpent, we are here to understand the devil himself, who tempted humanity and received judgment in the form and shape of the serpent. And therefore he is called the serpent—indeed, the old serpent—as is evident from 2 Corinthians 11:3 compared with 1 John 3:8 and Revelation 12:9 and 20:2.

**4th Circumstance.** From this, then, by the "head" of this serpent (to answer generally) the head of the devil must be meant. But this too must be understood in a figurative sense. For this

<column-break/>

<page-num>25</page-num>

this head stands for the godless plot that was devised therein and spun out against Adam and his descendants.

But here a very significant question arises for me, which I must not pass over; namely: what was this secret, godless, malicious, and cursed plot that Hell and its inhabitants hatched against the human race?

To which I answer: 1. That it was, to put it briefly, this: how they might bring them into disobedience, and consequently into a state of sin and into <term>Angst: In this mystical context, "Angst" (anguish or anxiety) refers to the contraction and inner turmoil of a soul separated from God's light.</term> within themselves. This, they knew, would cause the heavenly and Divine life to withdraw from them; and consequently, that they would and must be cast out of Paradise, just as they themselves had been cast out of Heaven into a state of eternal misery. They further knew, however, that this plot of theirs could achieve no effect except through the actual cooperation of [humanity's] own free will. So that they might bring them to the point of submitting their free will to their temptation, the whole and entire plot was first hatched and devised in the "head" of the serpent. After this had happened, all the devils in Hell went out from that head from that hour forth to put the same into action. They also succeeded in their temptations, and they actually saw that they had become the executioners and hangmen of the human race. They saw that the heavenly form and <term>Ebenbild GOttes: The Image of God; the original spiritual state of humanity before the Fall.</term> was transformed into an earthly one. And they now read to the lost couple the penal law <margin>(a) Destiny, Fate, the influences of the outer heavens and stars. <note>original: "Distinatio, Fatum, die Einflüsse der äussern Himmel und Gestirne."</note></margin> that they should be cast out of the physical paradise. And they paid close attention to everything they did or did not do, because they were now living in the <term>Principio: The beginning or foundation of this material world.</term> of this world; where they will not have failed to observe their free entrance and exit in a more precise way, so that they might [infect] spirit, soul, and body with their hellish poison and fire of anguish <meta>the text cuts off here, continuing with "infect" on the next page.</meta>

"Is it even possible or believable," they said, "that You Yourself should be able to help them? And now, well then, set them right again, if You can rescue them from our hands. And on the other hand" (for it is easy to imagine that they were not idle on such an occasion), "Look here now at your New Angelic <note>original: "Englische." In this context, it refers to "Engel" (angel), not the English nation.</note> Creation, the purer angels, who were intentionally created for the end that they might possess our vacant kingdoms, and thus also rule over us and our very thrones! Provided there is still a power and capacity in their worthy Divine properties to transform themselves once again, according to their pleasure, into their first angelic image—or if there is still a power remaining in their Sovereign Creators to reinstate them into the glory they initially had? —anew, and may therefore with all modesty presume that our imagined successors will not have such a free power either; it therefore lies upon Your [as their Creator's] own perfect Sovereign free power whether to rescue them. Let us now see, then, how far your Almightinesses extend, and let your wondrous wisdoms now also unite for their restoration and help them! Let us see what You are capable of. But it is impossible! In this way, Hell held its opposition against Heaven and rejoiced over the fall of a sinner! But in the midst of this false, boastful rejoicing, which rose to this almost unbelievable height of mockery and exaltation against God, the Spirit of Eternity, and against the blessed Trinity; (during all which things Adam and Eve certainly stood before the gates of Hell for the scorn, mockery, and laughter of the fallen angels) in the midst (I say) of this joy, boasting, and imagined triumph, and in the same moment, this Decree (of the Promise) went out from **GOD** the Father. This appeared to Adam as a soft and still word or voice of mercy, but in the ears of the devils, it rang out like a new clap of thunder, the likes of which they had never heard before. It extinguished their joy, dampened their defiance, stilled their vain boasting, and instantly checked all their scorn, mockery, and laughter. In a word, it tormented their malice and agonized their envy. And in this way, we may form an imperfect understanding of the wonderful depth, power, and effect of this mysterious promise.

2. Through this "head of the serpent," sin is also understood. The devils knew well that a single act of disobedience would give birth to Original Sin; that it would introduce itself into the spiritual, instrumental powers and faculties of Adam and Eve, and incorporate itself into all the properties and forms of their souls. Thus, sin would then live, move, and operate within them, mixing with their eternal mind, will, and senses, where it would <term>qualify</term><note>The original term is *qualificiren*, a technical term in Jakob Böhme’s mysticism meaning to interact with, manifest through, or impart a specific quality to a substance or spirit.</note> and work, and consequently penetrate, occupy, and corrupt the entire human being. And once their spirits were clothed in a sinful life, there would be an eternal dividing wall between **God** and them—a conclusion they drew from their own sad experience. In the confidence of such a well-devised plot, they then made a fierce assault, though it was likewise very hidden and just as little subject to any suspicion, and so they succeeded. For upon that single act of disobedience, in that they ate from the forbidden tree, the lovely **Image of God** in both was destroyed; the heavenly life, which nourished and preserved that same image, departed from them; and Original Sin flowed and broke into them like a rushing, mighty flood, and "qualified" and worked in and with all the instrumental faculties and powers of their spirit, soul, and body. And so far, the devils found their conclusions to be truly correct. But as for their further deduction—namely, that once sin was thus implanted in their natures (as they vainly imagined), it could never again be rooted out—this was merely a groundless arrogance and false presumption, even though it raised their unrestrained joy and triumph even higher in their foresight of things. But I could also wish that this delusion and opinion had been contained, and not spread as far into the world as has happened. For how many are there among us who violently insist and maintain that sin is such an

3. A third head of the serpent—or rather, a third treacherous plot, or a part of the devil's scheme in the serpent that occurred simultaneously—is that they introduced the **animal life** into the essential faculties, powers, and properties of man. For I must first establish here that Adam and Eve, in their state of innocence, stood solely in the knowledge and sensation of a Heavenly and Divine life, the life of righteousness and (as the Scripture calls it in some places) the **Life of God**, or a life from God; without any sensation of that which was earthly. Thus, the first and most cunningly deceptive invention of the Hells (which was intended to pave the way for all others) was to introduce the earthly life into their nature, which stands in opposition and contrariety to the Divine, Heavenly, and Paradisiacal life.

Following this, for the better and more complete discovery of the dark and confused labyrinths of the serpent, I will make known their entire dealings in this matter by providing a report of the same,

same, and how it came to pass, in the following historical manner:

**Lucifer**, the great Prince of Darkness, called a gathering of his entire fallen kingdom to consult and act together upon this important matter in his hellish court, situated in the deep abyss of darkness, or the center of their own principle. <note>The "center of the principle" is a term used in Böhmean theosophy to describe the foundational spiritual state or "source-realm" of a being—in this case, the dark world of the fallen angels.</note> After the summons had been issued and the council had assembled, the great angel of the bottomless abyss, standing under the great red dragon, began to deliver the address to that end, namely: "My lords and noble counselors of the realm; Princes, Thrones, Mages, Powers, Authorities, Dominions, and Sovereigns; I present here to your mature consideration a pair of brightly shining angels, namely **Adam** and **Eve**, with their future descendants. See how the lovely image of the blessed Trinity is implanted in them. Observe their sparkling life, how it is nourished and maintained by the daily and fresh assistance of the constant outflows and inflows of the blessed Trinity; such as we once were ourselves—I will not say even higher. But I would not have mentioned this (nor can I yet humble myself enough to envy them) were it not that these angelic creatures and their descendants were awakened by **God** for the purpose of tormenting and plaguing us, and being as thistles and thorns in our spirits. It seems they must stand there to present themselves to our eyes and sight as if in a crystalline mirror, to remind us at all times (as if we could forget it) of what we once were and what we truly are now; how lovely we were then, and how misshapen and hideous we are now. And so that I may say everything plainly: they and their descendants are intended to possess our realms and rule over us, and to fill our still-vacant thrones. ...are the unrest of my spirit and the wounding of my soul. It is, therefore, high time to make a new attempt and to secure for ourselves once more our old rights and privileges; for if we neglect this opportunity, we shall find ourselves—before we know it—troubled and tormented by new heirs who are rising up and flourishing against us, and who will incessantly burden us to prove our title and rights. <note>Lucifer speaks here like a nobleman or a lawyer, concerned that humanity—the "new heirs"—will legally displace the fallen angels from their former status.</note>

It was with this intention, therefore, that I had to summon you publicly, disturbing you and falling upon you as a burden, so that we might collectively consider, reflect, and deliberate upon what means should be used to suppress these "young plants" <note>A metaphorical reference to Adam and Eve.</note> who aspire to our kingdom in their first budding, and to darken the **Divine Image** <note>The *Imago Dei*, or the reflection of God's nature within humanity.</note> within them—to cut off and take away all moisture and help from above, and, in a word, to truly destroy them. To this end, your deeper thoughts are earnestly sought and desired, just as they are now more convenient and necessary given the current state of affairs.

Whereupon all the dark powers answered and confessed that the importance of the matter required it. They declared themselves willing and ready, promising their collective assistance and stating they would do their utmost in the common cause and interest of Hell. From that hour, a **secret council** was held for this purpose, and the conclusion reached therein consisted of the following resolutions and decrees, which were, however, put into effect only slowly and by one degree after another. Namely:

**1. Resolution.** That the Divine life in Adam and Eve cannot be extinguished except through the introduction of another life in its place, the nature of which must be contrary and opposed to it.

**2. Resolution.** That no such new life can be introduced into them except through an act of their own **free will**.

But here, a great difficulty arose regarding...

<meta>catchword: regarding</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>33</page-num>
<header>Outer Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ.</header>

...regarding this contrary life, and what it should ultimately be. And there was a very great consternation over it; for the entire assembly of the dark council stood without counsel, and their wisdom was at an end. Their cunning and craftiness could not discover it, so that they all stood there in horror and confusion, looking at one another—yet without speaking a word.

At this unusual sight, **Lucifer** himself finally stood up and addressed them a second time with these words: "Powerful potentates of my dark realm, why do you stand so dismayed and look at one another? The difficulty is indeed great in truth, because we make it so great; but in itself, it could never necessitate such silence and consternation from us. Let us only consider the great love and heartfelt care which the **Holy Trinity** has for our new rivals in the Kingdom, in that He forbade them to eat of the fruit of the tree in the middle of the garden. What was this tree? Was it not distinct from all others? Distinct in its nature, in its properties, qualities, and effects? And consequently, its fruit is likewise distinct from the fruit of all other trees? This tree, my nobles—this **forbidden tree**—is an earthly tree, and its fruit is also earthly. Eating it will awaken an **earthly life** in them, and thus transform a heavenly creature into an earthly one."

Following this, it was finally resolved, without anyone's contradiction, that the introduction of the earthly life into Adam and Eve would indeed accomplish the task. Whereupon, from that hour, orders went out to put everything into effect, which then occurred. For all the devils, in the form of serpents, made a violent and unanimous assault, using all their powers to awaken and stir up in Adam and Eve a violent lust and desire to know the nature of the earthly life (of which they were then still ignorant) and to eat from the forbidden tree.

# The First Little Treatise of the
<header>The First Little Treatise of the</header>

One must not think here as if we were departing from the main discourse by interrupting the historical narrative; rather, we do so that we might consider for a moment what providence God provided to preserve man against this fierce attack <note>The "attack" refers to the devil's plot described on the previous pages.</note>. For certainly the words of the prophet Zechariah 2:8 were just as true before as after the Fall: *He who touches man, touches the apple of God’s eye.* And in truth, God’s gracious care for him—His love, grace, mercy, and eternal goodness toward him—is sufficiently clear from the fact that He warned him of the danger and harmful consequences that would follow from eating from that same tree. For first, we read in Genesis 2:16: *And the LORD God commanded the man*, that is, the man Adam; but what kind of man was the man Adam? Not an earthly one, but an **Angelic**, **Paradisal man**, in the form of an angel <note>The author is using the term "Englischer Mensch" (Angelic Man), a common concept in 17th-century German mysticism (like that of Jacob Böhme), which suggests that before the Fall, humans possessed a spiritual body similar to the angels rather than a physical, "animal" body.</note>. For, as already reminded, man was not originally created in the animal form into which we have now come since the Fall. 

But this is only mentioned in passing; to proceed further to this **Angelic man**, this firstborn of men through creation, his Sovereign Creator gave this commandment, namely: *Of every tree of the garden you may freely eat*; which, in view of his nature, was indeed no legalistic commandment, no commandment of works; but an unconditional, free, and absolute commandment of love and goodness from the perfect Sovereign Majesty of the Creator to His creature. For he is commanded only to eat, without any restriction, neither in this nor in another way, and to do so freely and for nothing. The ground and cause of this commandment lay in his nature itself, as he was a creature: for no created being can exist without its own nourishment and sustenance; indeed, not even the angels themselves, according to the testimony of the Psalmist in Psalm 78:25: *He gave them the bread of angels.* The Angelic man

<meta>catchword: outward</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>35</page-num>
<header>outward Commandment and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.</header>

now should, through the eating of this fruit destined for him here, be sustained, and his eternal spirit become ever stronger and stronger, his heavenly and Divine life flourish, grow, and increase within him, and the beloved **Image of God** <note>original: "Ebenbild GOttes"</note>, which was imprinted into his spirit, soul, and body, be thus preserved, and he thereby be confirmed and strengthened in his paradisal state.

But if someone should interrupt me here and ask what kind of trees and fruits those were of which we have spoken? Then I must indeed confess and admit to him that the literal letter of Scripture reports nothing of it; yet I would say further that Adam, at the very least, knew all their natures; indeed, I would even assure you that whatever kind they were, they must have been in accord with the **Angelic man** in his paradisal state; and that consequently both the trees and fruits were of an eternal, Divine, spiritual, and heavenly nature—or, in a word, the fruit (and thus also the trees) was a living kind of fruit, or **fruit of life**; the life of eternity flowed within them as their own sap and power.

And so much for the first commandment (or, if you prefer, the first part of the commandment), from which it is already clear that God had already therein made provision against temptation, and that he [man] had no need to bow under it out of any lack or necessity. But there also follows another commandment for man's further assurance, namely verse 17: *But of the tree of the knowledge of good and evil, you shall not eat.* The cause of this prohibition was not a purely arbitrary thing in God; so too could God begrudge His creature nothing that might have contributed to his happiness or salvation: and therefore God immediately adds the dangerous consequences to be expected therefrom as the sole primary cause of it, when He says: *For on the day that you eat of it, you shall surely die.*

It will cause the eternal death of the souls and spirits of both your inner and outer man. Through eating the fruit of this tree, you will come to know yourself in a mixed state of good and evil. You will lose your **Paradisal Kingdom** and **Principle** <note>In the mystical tradition of Jacob Böhme, a "Principle" (Principium) refers to one of the three fundamental realms of existence: the dark fire-world, the light world of God, or the outer material world.</note> for another, and exchange your **Heavenly Life** and **Image** for such a one that neither came down from above, nor can ever fly back up there. Therefore, remember my law and my commandment, that you keep it, and my order, that you obey it. And although this entire explanation is not found so explicitly in the literal letter or history of the Holy Scripture; yet Adam's spirit was powerfully reminded of this and much more, and his **Angelic** <note>original: "Englischer"—this refers to the state of man before the Fall, which the author believes was spiritual and angel-like rather than physical.</note> understanding grasped it all.

In all of this, for my part, I can see no (*) legalistic commandment, as if from Mount Sinai <note>Referring to the Ten Commandments and the Law of Moses, which the author distinguishes from the "Covenant of Grace."</note>, no Covenant of Works, nothing conditional or based on terms; but rather a perfect and free **Covenant of Grace**, as well as mercy and goodness from Mount Zion: namely, that if Adam were to eat of the Tree of Life, he should live eternally in the Divine Image; and if not, but rather if he should eat of the Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil, he would then recognize a mixed state of good and evil. To which I may add, as if in passing, that therefore God did not want or desire man’s Fall, much less decree it. Nor can I imagine (nor, I believe, any other rational being) how God could have expressed and testified more than He actually did, that He absolutely did not want the fall of man.

<margin>(*) The author understands here that the sin did not take the cause of killing Adam and Eve from the prohibition; which would have necessarily followed if such a commandment had not been given by God in such a way, as when a father commands a child and says: see to it, do not go closer to this fire, so that you do not fall into it; for if you do, you will certainly be burned.</margin>
