But it might be asked here (as indeed happens with some): why was it necessary to give such a prohibition, that he should not eat of it? For if there had been no commandment, there would surely have been no transgression of it, etc.

To which question (although I cannot see that any answer is necessary in this case, or that it can serve anything other than to show how blind and dull Reason is in and of itself in all Divine matters, and especially how irrational and foolish it can be in its unregenerate state) I first answer by way of a confession: that, if no warning had come from God to Adam not to transgress the commandment, and the danger had not been revealed, one might say with sufficient appearance that God had willed and decreed the Fall. But then one might also add, secondly, that the mere revelation of the danger of eating, etc., could not cause the Fall as an efficient cause. For whether it had been revealed or not, the effect would have necessarily followed in the course of nature. The only difference would be this: namely, that since Adam’s transgression would then have been out of ignorance, in this way he sinned against his conscience and against the knowledge of the commandment given to him by God.

To return again to my main discourse of the history begun. So the devils (who swiftly carry out the resolutions and orders of the dark counsel and do not delay with useless, vain things concerning offices, positions, dignities, and the like) rushed in at once and, without making any further ceremonies, fell to the work. And because they saw Eve as the "weaker vessel," they first cast their eyes on her and attacked her with all their power and might to excite in her (if possible) a burning, lustful desire to eat from the forbidden tree. # The First Little Treatise of the

History has described this transaction quite perfectly; therefore, for my present purpose, it shall suffice to note how the devils sought to creep into their favor in three ways, which are recorded to be seen in Genesis 3:5 <note>original: "Gen. 3 v 5"</note> in these words: **God knows that on the day you eat of it, your eyes will be opened: and you will be as gods, and know good and evil.** Upon this part of the temptation, I say, I will restrict myself; wherein it is easy to notice that the truth is so cunningly interwoven and worked through with deceit that at first glance (especially when the **affects and passions** <note>In early modern psychology, "affects and passions" were the emotions and physical urges that could cloud a person's reason and spiritual discernment.</note> are confused) it is somewhat difficult to perceive the fraud.

For example, everything is introduced through this undeniable truth: *God knows* etc. is true enough! For God knows all things. But even Eve herself could have told the tempter as much. So this was only an enticement to bring forth the temptation which was still held back; *Then your eyes will be opened*; which is, in and of itself, another truth; although it was also a lie—yes, it is something more, and may therefore pass for a second **insinuation** <note>The author uses "insinuation" to mean a creeping, subtle influence or a "worming in" of a false idea.</note> or sneaking-in. The third is hidden in these words: *and you will know good and evil.* And this may claim the name of truth with a better title than the two preceding ones; as it is, according to appearance and outward show, a more manifest and clearer fact—which manifest clarity only elevates the insinuation or makes it greater, and makes the temptation truly more seductive. It did indeed awaken her desire for knowledge (which was also obtained through her consent); but it did not make the nature of that knowledge known to her. One thing more I must not pass over here: and that is the final conclusion and the deceit by which Eve was captured: *You shall then be as gods.* Just as if he had said: You shall then be like gods; yet also adding this: you are indeed already **little gods, lower eternities** <note>term: "Lower Eternities" (Untere Ewigkeiten). In Christian mysticism, this refers to human souls being immortal and divine in origin, but subordinate to the "Great Eternity" of God.</note>, under the high 
**Power**

<page-num>39</page-num>

Power and government of the Holy Trinity; however, if anything could be added to your divine nature and properties, or if they could be elevated, then eating the fruit of this tree would certainly bring it about. This at least is certain: it would assure you of the **perpetuity** <note>original: "Immerwierigkeit"</note> of all those magnificent privileges that belong to you as gods. Or else we may understand it thus: that it was spoken by the devil in a mocking manner; namely: *then you will see what kind of gods you are* etc.

After these preparatory insinuations, the devil continues to present the fruit itself (just as if he did not desire that one should believe him on his own word, but rather points them to experience itself) so that she might consider it impartially. The Holy History in Genesis 3:6 <note>original: "Gen. 3 v 6"</note> reads thus: **And when the woman saw that the tree was good for food, and that it was pleasant to the eyes to look upon, and a tree to be desired because it should make one wise** etc. Which words clearly indicate and give us to understand that, after the devils had awakened a fiery-lusting desire in Eve's spirit, understanding, will, and senses, they did not fail to apply all their **false magic** <note>In this context, "magic" refers to the manipulation of appearances or the creation of illusions to make something look more attractive than its true nature.</note> so that they might make the tree appear even more beautiful and delightful than it was in its own nature: and they stood thus awaiting the success of their enchantments. They did not stand waiting long before Eve stretched out her hand, suddenly and thoughtlessly broke off the fruit, and gave some to her husband, who (whether through a fatality of love, or whatever else the cause may have been) truly ate of it also, Gen. 3 v. 6. And from that moment on, a new and joyful period began in the **chronicles** <note>original: "Jahrzeit-Bücher"—literally "year-time books" or annals.</note> of the dark kingdom. For this was the deadly bite, the cruel mouthful, which introduced the earthly life, and this in turn the life of sin: just as this finally brought in the **anguished fire-life of hell** <note>term: "Fire-life" (Feuer-Leben). A concept from Jacob Böhme describing the soul's state when it is separated from God's light and left in its own burning, hungry, and restless spiritual nature.</note>, together with its eternal curse, death, and damnation.

brought it about, and indeed in Adam and Eve first; and after them in all their descendants. Whether this fruit (as is commonly said) was an apple or another kind matters little; it was, in truth, a shameful speech that a certain prelate made regarding a reason granted in this case through a prayerful request. It may be enough for us to know that it was an earthly fruit in which there was earthly life, which—because it stands in **contrariety** <note>Opposition or conflict.</note> against the Divine life—rooted it out and set itself in its place. The tree itself may not unfairly be called a figurative tree, which represented the **spirit of this world** <note>In the mystical tradition of Jacob Böhme, the "spirit of this world" (spiritus mundi) refers to the temporal, astral, and elemental forces that govern material existence.</note>, which to this day is still an earthly spirit: therefore its fruits must also be such, as well as those who eat of them and live by them. The hand which reached out to seize the fruit represents the hotly-lusting desire which drove Eve's yearning **imagination** <note>In this context, "imagination" is not merely fantasy, but a creative spiritual force that "images" or pulls an object into the soul.</note> to reach out; so that, because she longed for the fruit in her imagination, she stretched out her hand or arm for it, and through this outward deed made known what had already occurred within her. Also, the mouth which tested and tasted it was another figure; namely, of the Lustful Will, which inwardly longed and reached for it, and the eating of the earthly fruit was first in the spirit; but at last, it also ended in the physical deed.

The spectators of this whole work were diverse and innumerable. But how the devils rejoiced over it may be gathered from the imperfect expression of their triumph which I have already given: for they intended to keep this day as an eternal holiday and everlasting feast in the dark world. The spirit of this world also rejoiced exceedingly; especially as he well knew that his temporal wishes would now certainly come to a revelation,

But our **Reason**, which deems itself wise, here steps forward and thinks this (not to say more) is a very necessary question: Whether God could not have immediately forgiven this single misstep, since it was only one deed and the first error, and consequently at most a minor offense and a simple sin?

Yes, dear Reason, you have concluded wisely! I will even grant you the conclusion this time, but I answer that God had freely remitted and forgiven it as soon as it was committed. God took pity on man’s misery; His mercy was great toward him; and His wisdom consulted with His essential Love on how his **restoration** <note>original: "Wiederbringung" – the process of returning man to his original divine state.</note> might be brought about. I should not admit that the transgression of our first parents was a small sin, or only the first departure and crossing-over into the sinful state, were it not that my denial of the same might obligate me to make a long digression. I will only note here that the question only assumes it to be so, and indeed only based on a purely outward appearance of the matter.

But Reason (because it does not consider that the effect is so visible in its workings) will ask further: where then is the great terror and threat of this bite? Answer: If the damage is so small, why then did the Majesty of Heaven graciously descend and warn beforehand of the danger and forbid the fruit? Does it matter equally, or is there no difference, whether the Spirit of God or the spirit of this world is the sole ruler in man? Since this latter spirit once succeeded in **qualifying** <note>To "qualify" here means to imbue with specific qualities or to interact chemically/spiritually with the soul's essence.</note> itself with the earthly power and juice of the earthly fruit in the spirit and soul of man, did it not actually and essentially stir, seethe, or boil up in his mind, will, and senses, and work in him so long until it made him earthly? <page-num>42</page-num>
# The First Little Treatise of the
<header>The First Little Treatise of the</header>
<meta>catchword: grasp</meta>

the terror <margin>(a) 2 Cor. 5 v 11: Since we know the terror of the Lord, we persuade men to faith.</margin> or the horror of that same knowledge; namely, that the natural magic of the spirit of this world worked so powerfully upon man that it transformed him in an instant from a heavenly to an earthly creature, from an angelic to a bestial shape. Indeed, he was changed from a virginal (resurrection) body into one that bore the bestial marks of distinction—the designations of male and female. In a word, he was changed from a nature that gave him the qualities and characteristics of a free inhabitant of the paradisiacal kingdom into one that gave him the title and the right to have a share among the beasts of the earth.

The wisdom of the ancients described this transformation of shape in the manner of a fable, in this way: namely, that **Circe**, the beautiful sorceress <note>In Greek mythology, Circe was a sorceress known for transforming her enemies into animals. Here, she represents the seductive "spirit of this world."</note> (standing for the spirit of this world), had a magnificent palace in a delightful forest [namely, in this outer world or **outbirth** <term>Outbirth (Ausgeburt): A term used in Jacob Böhme’s philosophy to describe the physical, material world as a secondary manifestation or "birth" coming out of the internal, spiritual realm.</term>]. She received all travelers who were invited to her gate very kindly; but as soon as her guests had eaten of her delicacies and drunk of her potion (which was handed to them from a golden cup), their minds were so altered that they completely forgot their natural fatherland. Even their bodies suffered a similar change, for they immediately grunted in the shape of swine; and then she fed them, as such, with a kind of fodder more suited to them. The application of this story is easy to make. However, it is also remarkable that the beautiful sorceress nevertheless had no power except over those who desired to taste her delicacies—as can be seen at length in the 10th book of **Homer’s Odyssey**.

Finally, for the further explanation of this earthly life, I wish to conclude this main point with a brief indication of what it contains or **grasps**...

---

<page-num>43</page-num>

<meta>catchword: only</meta>

...**grasps**. For within it, the animal, the sensual, and even the rational life are centered. The **animal life** looks to the life of flesh and blood, according to the life of outer nature. The **sensual life** stands in the pleasures of the outer senses, in the sensory objects of this world. And the **rational life**, or the life of reason, stands in the knowledge of all the wonders of God in this outer world or outbirth, from the heights of the starry heavens above to the lowest depths of the earth. So it is that each of these lives is **only** a different limb or branch that has sprouted from the earthly life and stands rooted within it. Man would never have known such a life if he had not turned his mind, will, and senses into the spirit of this world, and thereby actually turned himself out of Paradise. 

But now (thanks be to God for it), this partition wall, or the separation between God and us, has been just as effectively abolished through **Jesus Christ**. For now, the earthly is once again swallowed up by the heavenly; the mortal by the immortal. Through the death of the earthly, sensual, animal, and rational life, the heavenly and paradisiacal life of Divine Wisdom rises anew. When this life is again "qualified" <note>Originally "qualificiret," meaning to be restored to its proper quality or spiritual state.</note> in our souls and spirits, mixing with and penetrating them, it gradually regains its original, first dominion. It works such a perfect transformation in us that we become completely **New Creatures**, as a second masterpiece from the hands of Wisdom. Once this has perfectly occurred and worked itself out within him, the heavenly-minded saint cannot keep from rejoicing and triumphing in the spirit, and crying out externally with exceeding joy: "O Earthliness, where is your sting? In your daily, stinging, miserable cares, which are the thistles and thorns of your life and station? But they are burned away. O Grave, where is..." <note>A paraphrase of 1 Corinthians 15:55, adapted to contrast the "earthly" state with the "resurrected" spiritual state.</note>

4. A fourth head of the serpent, which the promised seed should crush, is (if I may call it so) **selfhood** and **self-will**, which stands in opposition to the will of God. The devils well knew that the heavenly life in Paradise was governed by the power and might of the Holy Trinity and was ordered in each and every one of its movements by the Spirit of Divine Wisdom, subject to Them and according to Their will; so that the life of Wisdom alone stirred and moved in their minds, wills, desires, imaginations, or thoughts and senses; in and through all the instrumental faculties, properties, and powers of the spirit, soul, and body. Thus, a conflict or deliberation arose among those rulers <note>Referring to the spiritual forces or fallen angels who sought to influence the direction of human nature.</note> who stood in opposition to him and yet strove after him, concerning where it should be placed once the earthly life was introduced. And it was decided by them after mature deliberation that it must happen in the spirit of this world, and under that, in the spirit of **Reason** as its steward. For they well perceived that the life of self-will, under the guidance and government of Reason, would immediately—without any further care from them—exalt itself and maintain the upper hand. This was never such a creature of God as the angels were who fell, but was merely, in and through their apostasy from the Light, a kind of misshapen monster <note>original: "Mißgebuhrt" (mis-birth or monstrosity).</note>...

# The First Little Treatise of the

birth generates: it would also certainly have had no existence at all, except as it was conceived and brought forth in the head of Lucifer, whereby it also became lower and subject to him. Its nature is nothing other than a harsh, severe, and fierce fire-spirit—a magical fire-spirit—who rules and reigns in and over the devils (as they now dwell in its principle <note>original: "Principio" (Latin). In Jacob Böhme’s thought, a "principle" refers to a fundamental realm of spiritual existence, such as the dark world or the light world.</note>) as their God, King, and Tormentor. So the description given of him in Revelation 12:3 is very beautiful; yet it is not to be understood so literally, as if his actual form were nothing other than a red dragon or a seven-headed monster and beast. No, he is only a sulfurous power-spirit in himself, indeed an anxious power; and because he is an anxious, painful spirit, he is compared to a dragon—and specifically a red dragon, to indicate his fiery, sulfurous, and anxious property; and again, a great red dragon, to show the power and strength of its fiery and consuming nature. And therefore his seven heads must also signify his seven divided, active properties, which belong and are proper to his fierce and hellish nature. These seven properties, in turn, when viewed as being within one another, constitute the single head of the dragon; but if viewed as divided, then they signify as was mentioned before. Now I will consider them as distinct and name them as such.

**1st Property.** And this is his shrewdness and cunning. It is this which gives an almost irresistibly powerful emphasis to lies and flattering concessions, through which he deceives all eternal souls and spirits, except for those who have the Spirit of Wisdom born within them, and possess the single eye <note>A reference to Matthew 6:22, meaning a focused, pure spiritual intention.</note> and true childlike simplicity. For this reason, he is called a seducer, a deceiver, and a liar from the beginning.

**2nd Property.** This second head is Pride. It is this which so exalts and puffs him up that he flies above the heart of God and despises the gates of Paradise, the angelic thrones, and, in a word, the entire kingdom of love and glory; so that, because he is not capable in himself of reaching the Still Eternity, he falls back down into his own center of eternal darkness.

**3rd Property.** His third head or property is his power and strength. This appears or manifests itself in the chains of darkness mentioned in Scripture, used to keep and preserve the fallen angels in captivity. They are all vassals of this dragon and, because they cannot break their chains, are forced to live under him and be subject to him.

**4th Property.** The fourth head is Greed. He seeks to seize and devour the entire creation of spirits. His hungry palate, throat, or magical hunger is insatiably greedy and eager to draw everyone (if possible) into the deep abyss of his own anxiety. Also, no one can resist the magic or the insatiable hunger of his attractive desire, except for those few blessed ones who cling to the voice of Wisdom and use her as their sole guide.

**5th Property.** Envy and Malice constitute the fifth head; this is constantly directed against all the children of men and, in truth, against everything that has ever actually borne the divine impression or mark of the image of God, or even merely resembled it. Therefore, one can all the more easily understand and measure the fierceness of the attack that occurred against Adam and Eve.

**6th Property.** But the sixth head is his anxious fire or fiery anguish. In this property, he takes his pleasure and delight in tormenting and agonizing all those who are under his power and dominion. He is in truth the most cruel

...most cruel and unmerciful: His eye pities no one; nor can his hand spare anyone: instead, he delights in the misery of all those who lie in his pit, which burns with fire and sulfur.

**7th Property.** The seventh and final head of all this corruption. It is clearly evident from everything said so far how well-qualified he is to destroy; and consequently, he must necessarily be a destroying and consuming spirit. Therefore, his name in the Scripture is **Apollyon** <note>original: "Apollion" (Greek: *Apolluon*), meaning "The Destroyer."</note> or a Destroyer: and the names therein are always given with a special intention or purpose and meaning. And according to this name, his character or mark is also drawn and imprinted upon him; as when he is compared to a **fire-red dragon**; as well as to a **roaring lion**: who goes about, or walks up and down, and actively seeks whom he may devour. No one ever comes to him unexpectedly or by themselves; for he draws everyone toward him.

The wisdom of the ancients compared these seven heads to the **seven planets**, which belong to the fierce nature of the dark world. The number itself indeed signifies perfection, whether in good or in evil. So the dragon is not merely and alone an ungodly, envious, and malicious spirit, but is malice in the highest degree and the perfection of ungodliness itself. His horns were understood by the ancients to be the dark hellish constellations; and his seven crowns signify his universal victory over men and angels. For he rules as a perfect sovereign over as many of them as fall. But this may now be enough concerning the crushing of the serpent's head.

But also to completely finish this other circumstance at once; it is evident from everything in general that these two titles: **The Woman's Seed** and **Crusher of the Serpent's Head** (both of which were given to the person of Christ immediately by the Holy Trinity, in the promise

promise given to Adam and Eve) are alone enough to call Him wonderful; especially when we consider that He was never to set them aside: no, not even when He would be seated upon the throne of His Glory in the highest heaven.

III. But He is furthermore also wonderful when He is considered in a third respect, namely as the **Seed of the Covenant**. *I will establish my covenant* (says God in Genesis 17:7 to Abraham) *between Me and you, and your seed after you* etc. Here we may note: **1.** That God renewed His previous covenant (made with our first parents before the Deluge <note>The Great Flood.</note>) with Abraham and his seed, and in the same way with all the children of men. **2.** The mention of the two parties between whom the covenant is made: namely, the Holy Trinity on one side, and Abraham and his seed on the other. **3.** That the seed spoken of here is none other than Jesus Christ; who, as is clearly evident from His genealogy in Matthew 1:1, was the son of David; and thus also the son of Abraham. See also Hebrews 2:16. And also regarding the fact that He is given as a covenant in Isaiah 42:6 and 49:8. For which reason I again conclude: that as Jesus Christ held this privilege as uniquely His own, and no one ever beside Him, neither before nor after Him; He is also a wonderful person in this regard.

IV. And such a one was He also, as He was a **figurative seed** or person. I say *in Christ*, and in Christ alone, just as the Apostle proves in Galatians 3:16; He does not speak of seeds, as of many, but as of one; *and to your Seed*, which is Christ.

Before I proceed further / I will / for the sake of greater ease and better order / make some observations on the Angel’s Message <note>original: "Bothschafft"</note> itself. And it is as follows; namely Luke 1 v. 28: **And the Angel Gabriel said: Hail be with thee / thou who art highly favored / the LORD is with thee / blessed art thou among women.**

Upon these words an interpretation has been made by the Protestants / and another / quite different from it / by the Papists <note>A historical term for Roman Catholics, common in 17th and 18th-century theological writing.</note>: therefore I wish to say something in regard to both.

What concerns the first, it is easy to note: 1. That the Messenger or Angel sent here is no angel of a lower order; rather, his name Gabriel (Luke 1 v. 26) indicates much more that He was a High Throne-Angel. 2. That He did not come out of mere own will / but was sent by God. 3. That the person / to whom He was thus sent / was the Virgin Mary / who was betrothed to a man / whose name was Joseph / of the house of David; the Virgin's name, however, was Mary (v. 27). 4. The manner and way of the Message / which occurred through the greeting or congratulation: **Hail be with thee** / or **Greetings to thee, Mary** (v. 28). 5. That the Message itself consists of two parts: of which the first is, as it were, the original ground or cause of the other / expressed in these words: **thou / who art highly favored** / but the other follows in these words: **The LORD is with thee / blessed art thou among women.** 6. All signs of the difference between man and woman are there swallowed up: for in Christ they are all but one; which, however, will not prevent one from recognizing and distinguishing the other. III. But how and on what ground do they prove that worshipful honor—yes, even Divine Adoration—is due to her from the Saints here on earth? She is now (which is true) an angelic creature <note>original: "Englische Creatur"; in this context 'Englisch' refers to 'Engel' (angel), not the English nation</note> and a Princess of the Throne—[because she has the feminine tincture <term>tincture: in alchemical and mystical thought, a spiritual essence or "inner quality" that colors one's nature</term> as the dominant force within her]—but she is nevertheless still a creature. There is also so very little love in this superfluous work itself, and one is thus not bound to it! For certainly no conclusion can be drawn from any comparison between the earthly relationship of the Son and Mother. The event with our Savior is just as extraordinary as His person: both are beyond the course of nature. If Christ were nothing more than a "reborn person," then the comparison might hold; but this, I believe, they dare not say. Nor can they say that Mary shared in both natures—namely the Divine and Human—in the same sense and understanding as Christ. The conclusion is therefore easy to make: namely, that more is due to the One than to the other. And if this is an error, then the Holy Scripture itself compels us to make it. For the same teaches us clearly and plainly in John 5:22 that the Father has committed and delivered all judgment to the Son; and he who does not honor the Son, does not honor the Father (v. 23). But to pursue this opinion a little further: for what end and for what cause should we send up our prayers and supplications to the blessed Virgin, other than that she might pray for us and perform intercession? <meta>This page continues the author’s theological critique of the Roman Catholic practice of praying to the Virgin Mary and the Saints. He argues that even if saints are in heaven, they do not have the capacity to hear earthly prayers unless specifically sent by God, and he begins to transition into his specific mystical definition of Mary’s virginity.</meta>

# The First Little Treatise on the

<meta>catchword: to</meta>

...the Apostles (not to mention the Holy Spirit Himself) would seem to be guilty of it; regarding which I may boldly appeal to anyone who has even the slightest understanding.

I have already shown that in the present case, making a comparison from the natural relationship between a son and mother contributes nothing to the matter here. But our adversaries are so zealous to seek new excuses for their conclusion that they cannot refrain from making another attempt. The blessed Virgin and all other various Saints (they say) are—according to the very case we have granted them—namely, that they are in heaven—in favor and grace with their Redeemer; which is true enough. The Papists <note>A historical term for Roman Catholics, often used in a polemical or critical context during the Reformation and subsequent centuries.</note> however, add: and therefore He grants them everything they ask and desire, and can in truth deny them nothing. To which, instead of a final and direct answer, I will only add these following remarks:

1. That all such prayers or intercessions made contain too much of this world within them; indeed, they are self-interested, partisan, and therefore purely fleshly. Consequently, I cannot admit (even upon the advice of a hypothetical case) that they have any place in heaven.

2. That prayers thus made must be done either for themselves or for others. Now, however, they cannot occur for themselves, for they have need of nothing—or else the reports given to us in the Scripture concerning heaven must be entirely erroneous. If the latter be the case, then I answer: that the Saints to whom we pray, or whom we invoke, must first—before they can pray for us—know our distress and what we lack. But then I ask again, how are they to attain such knowledge? If one were to answer that we must first make everything known to them ourselves: then the question is again, how must this also happen? To which they seem more willing to agree than we dare grant them. Finally, it is also very

<column-break/>

# Outer Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.

<sig>E</sig>
<meta>catchword: of</meta>

much to be doubted whether anyone from the Church Triumphant <note>original: "triumphirenden Kirche"; this refers to the community of saints and believers who have died and are now in the presence of God in heaven.</note>—so long as they remain in their heavenly Principle of Glory and are not specifically sent out as ministering spirits or angels—sees, hears, or recognizes anything outside of it, or, in a word, is in any way sensible of it. Therefore, I am convinced that the safest path in such an uncertain case—and where the danger is so exceedingly great—is to maintain that they do not; until we might be assured of the contrary through a clear Divine revelation. So that, to conclude, even if we were to admit that our blessed Savior really could deny His Virginal Mother (and other souls) nothing they asked, they might imagine they have won; but we do not need to, and therefore do not admit, that they ask anything of Him in such a specific way. 

And since we have now to some extent cleared these old, dilapidated Roman landmarks <note>The author refers to Catholic dogmatic arguments as "baufällige Römische Merckmahle," suggesting they are like crumbling ruins or obsolete markers on a path.</note> out of the way, and thereby paved our path a little—as much as was necessary—I will now proceed more directly to show how Mary, the mother of our blessed Jesus, came to be made a pure and immaculate virgin. This I will do in the following points, namely, by proof and demonstration: 1. Who made her so. 2. The manner and way in which she was made so. 3. Where this happened. 4. The time when it happened. 5. What the effects and workings of it were. 6. The causes or necessity, and why she had to be made a virgin.

**1. Point or Circumstance.** Who made Mary’s person into a virgin. Here I must (for the sake of an orderly and correct method of teaching) first report that before she was made into such a one, she was not born a (completely pure) virgin. Also, it seems to me I have already indicated before that I use these words, **Virgin** and **Virginity**, in a singular and not common sense. For otherwise, in common usage or understanding...

<page-num>62</page-num>
<header>The First Little Treatise on the</header>
<meta>catchword: But</meta>

...of the word, I recognize her as a virgin—that is, as a chaste, sensible female person who never knew a man. But the sense of the word **Virginity**, as I use it in this little treatise, refers solely to the **Virgin Nature** <note>The author is likely referring to "Sophia" or Divine Wisdom, a central concept in Christian mysticism (specifically Behmenism), where the "Virgin Nature" represents the pure, celestial humanity lost by Adam and Eve.</note> which Eve possessed in Paradise before the Fall, but which she lost after the same. And therefore, according to the sense in which I use the word, it is not actually a belittlement of the Mother of our LORD if I say that she was not a virgin before she was made into one.

Now, to give a plain and correct answer to the question: it is evident from these words in the Angel’s message in Luke 1:28, *The LORD is with thee,* etc., that it was the Holy Trinity that made Mary into a virgin. And this may be enough in general. But in truth, within the Holy Trinity, the principal instrumental cause <term>instrumental cause: the means or agent through which an action is performed</term> of making her a virgin was her Mother: and indeed not according to her natural birth (for that was Anna, the wife of Joachim), but according to her new and spiritual birth. If you ask me who this spiritual mother was, I answer that it was none other than the **Divine, Eternal Wisdom of God**. This Divine Wisdom was just as much the mother of the Virgin Mary as the Virgin Mary herself was the mother of Jesus. So that we have here a mother within a mother, just like a wheel within another wheel. Ezekiel 1. <note>A reference to Ezekiel’s vision of the celestial chariot (Merkabah), used here to describe the mystical indwelling of the divine within the human.</note>

However, I am sufficiently assured that this knowledge of Mary's virginity will be viewed today as quite inconsistent, strange, and wondrous; especially since so many learned, fruitless attempts have been made and efforts spent to prove that she was never carnally known by Joseph (a matter which, whether true or false, is of little consequence). Therefore, I only ask those who come to read this to have patience until they have read through these six circumstances.

<page-num>63</page-num>
<header>External Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.</header>
<sig>E 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: 19. He</meta>

But it seems to me the Scripture is clear and distinct therein, that Wisdom is both a mother and, as such, also has children. For why else should Wisdom be so constantly expressed in the female gender? *Does not Wisdom call? And does not Understanding let her voice be heard?* Proverbs 8:1. *She stands at the top of the highest,* etc. verse 2. *She calls at the doors,* etc. verse 3. The person spoken of here is clearly a female person. And so also in Proverbs 1:20–21 and 3:15–18. And in truth, throughout the entire book. According to this, how could we find a more suitable person (and one who fits more appropriately with the whole content of the matter) upon whom to fix our eye, and to recognize her as that female parent whom all (namely those who are deprived of their natural parents) should heed and obey? *My son, obey the discipline of your father, and forsake not the law of your mother,* Proverbs 1:8 and 6:20. The command here is free and without any restriction or exception: it is incumbent upon us all and at all times; yet it can never bind us with respect to our natural mother, whose law in many cases conflicts with God's law. In contrast, the law of the Mother of whom we speak here, when compared with other places, is always one and the same as the law of God Himself; and it is such a law from which no one can ever be exempted. If we now allow Wisdom to be this Mother, it is easy to understand how she will preserve us if we do not forsake her, Proverbs 4:6, and promote us if we exalt her; bringing us to honor and delivering to us a crown of glory, verses 8–9. Such a mother, indeed such a one alone, is qualified to be a Law-giver and may justly demand unrestricted obedience. Furthermore, that Divine Wisdom is both a mother and a female person is clear from the fact that her children are mentioned in the Scripture. And how can there be a more remarkable testimony of this than that which our Savior Himself gives in Matthew 11:19: *He...*

19. He—who is Himself the Wisdom of the Father—nevertheless says that **She** (in the feminine gender) is justified by Her children: <note>This refers to Matthew 11:19 or Luke 7:35: "But wisdom is justified of all her children."</note> which was also spoken incidentally as a way of identifying Himself, as if He Himself also fell under that same naming. Therefore, Wisdom is a universal Mother. But who are her children, to whom her instructions are directed, and by whom she is rightly praised? These are the very same Eternal Spirits who are now born again out of her body or **Principle** <term>Principle: in mystical thought, this refers to a fundamental source or realm of existence</term>; and who therefore, because of this New Birth, are called "new-born children" in the New Testament. It is not the outer, mortal, and temporal or transitory man (for even if you were to crush him in a mortar, his foolishness would still not depart from him <note>A reference to Proverbs 27:22: "Though thou shouldest bray a fool in a mortar... yet will not his foolishness depart from him."</note>); but rather the *New Man*, who is created after God in righteousness. It is in respect to this New Birth that it is said of Jerusalem (which is the same as Divine Wisdom) in Galatians 4:26, that **She** is the mother of us all—which must be understood with good judgment. For just as Eve is the universal mother of all living things under the state of the Fall or apostasy, so too is Divine Wisdom the Universal Mother of the Church or the Congregation of the Firstborn: and therefore also of all new-born children in general. But so that we do not stray too far from our purpose: first, there is a good foundation for our knowledge of Divine Wisdom in that She is a Lady or of the female gender and a Mother. This is a fairly good ground or indication that She is the Mother of the Virgin Mary in the spiritual understanding of the New Birth. But before I proceed further, I will only add here that although God in truth, in and of Himself, is neither male nor female (and can therefore use one name as well as the other as His own, even if He is neither of them, whenever it pleases Him to assume one), as He undeniably often does in the literal...

<meta>catchword: Book-</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>65</page-num>
<header>outer Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ.</header>

...letters of the Scripture; we must not hastily deviate from any such naming. Especially since there may be a deeper ground and cause for this in one or another revelation of the Divine Essences than we might notice at first glance. For names and titles are always given in the Scripture to both persons and places with a special significance. And I would gladly be taught why the Holy Spirit should have used less caution with the Divine names that occur in Holy Scripture. Therefore, let the terms [He and She], when they are attributed to Divine Wisdom, perhaps teach us that although God’s Wisdom is truly or essentially incorporated and embodied with the Blessed Trinity (in view of the perfect unity of the Essence), She is nevertheless not to be confused with the Holy Trinity; but rather is to be considered as being in a subordinate distinction from the same. Were this teaching to be accused of either absurdity or heresy, it would by itself overturn everything I have presented about her. But this I deny, and I will appeal to anyone who will only take enough trouble to rightly grasp my pronouncement and to compare all its parts with one another. But as for those who will not do so, they have no right to the truth, whatever it may be.

Now to proceed again from where we left off: I say that Mary (in her person) was made into a virgin through the mysterious cooperation of the Blessed Trinity with this Divine Wisdom, as an instrumental cause, without which it could not have happened. From this we may observe: that because this Divine Wisdom is the Mother of Mary (insofar as she is a virgin and from whom she was born), this Mother must indeed have existed before, and consequently is to be preferred above the daughter. And then again, what becomes of that proposition of the Romans <note>The author is referring to the Roman Catholic Church.</note>: that the Blessed Virgin Mary is now in heaven in the highest glory and dignity?

<meta>catchword: Seed-</meta>

...be? But this I mention only in passing; I now proceed to the

2. **Point or circumstance.** Namely, in what manner and way She was made a Virgin. Yet this is so mysterious that words can grasp nothing more than a very common report of it. Divine Wisdom—the Mother of all true and spotless Virginity—was in the spirit and soul of Mary; and from there, in union with the Trinity therein, she flowed the immortal seed of this Divine Virginity into both [namely, her spirit and soul]. Thus, She became a Virgin in **Essentiality** <term>Essentiality: In mystical theology, this refers to a transformation of one's actual nature or "being," rather than just a change in legal or moral status.</term>, and not through mere **imputation** <term>Imputation: The theological concept where something (like righteousness) is credited to a person’s account without necessarily changing their inner nature.</term>. This may give some light to that obscure promise: that the *seed of the woman* should crush the serpent’s head. For we may well say—though without excluding the common explanation of the words—that the seed spoken of here in Genesis 3:15 points us to the *higher seed*, the Eternal and incorruptible seed of true and immortal Virginity, which flows solely from the Virginal nature of Divine Wisdom. This is especially true when we consider that what is said of the woman's seed—namely, that it should crush the serpent's head—is, in the full sense of the words, more immediately (and in a higher degree) to be applied to this Divine and self-subsisting, essential seed.

However, concerning this seed (of which, according to 1 Peter 1:23, all who are born again likewise become partakers) there is a great dispute, particularly regarding the time of the sowing. For some maintain that all people are born into the world with it (a); while others, on the contrary, assert just as stiffly that it is only poured in afterward. For my part, if I might intervene as a mediator, I would seek to reconcile both parties. For although I admit and confess that (for example) the Virgin Mary brought this

<margin>(a) This is "the mystery among the Gentiles" in Colossians 1:27 in the original text.</margin>

<column-break/>

# Outer Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ.
<sig>E 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: gens</meta>

seed with Her into the world, it was nonetheless without effect, and as if dead, until it pleased the blessed Trinity, in union with Divine Wisdom, to open, awaken, quicken, and animate it. Thus, before this resurrection of the seed, it was not active, and was almost no better for Her than if She had not possessed it; indeed, She did not know in the slightest, not at all, that She had it. And therefore, we too must not regard ourselves as being in a state of grace simply because the seed itself might already be granted to be within us; not until it one day reveals itself through its own blissful effects and glorious workings.

3. **Point and circumstance.** Regarding the locality, or the place and site of the act; where it happened. To this, the answer provides itself: namely, in Her own person, and not in another’s; in Her **inward man** <term>Inward Man: A term used by mystics like Jacob Böhme to describe the eternal, spiritual part of a human being that must be "reborn" in God's light.</term>, in Her spirit, and in Her soul, and in the instrumental powers, strengths, and properties of both; and through the Inward, also in the Outward, to which Her body was attached as an outgrowth. Finally, it happened here on earth, and not in heaven. How is it, then, that some among us say that the virginal state of spotless purity is not to be reached before death? Is the arm of the LORD really shortened, or has He declared that He no longer wishes to stretch it out? <note>A reference to Isaiah 59:1: "Behold, the Lord's hand is not shortened, that it cannot save."</note> Until this is proven, I shall take the liberty to believe otherwise.

4. **Point or circumstance.** At what time it happened. The Scripture testifies to this sufficiently, as can be seen from the Angel’s message: *The LORD is with Thee,* etc. <note>Luke 1:28</note> Before the blessing was announced to Her, She was merely a chaste and sensible maidservant, as others are; but then, in that very moment, She became a pure Virgin. For previously, Her spirit was stained with earthly imagination, and Her soul with the earthly life and the earthly senses of this world. But at the time of the announcement of the blessing...

# The First Little Treatise on the
<meta>catchword: the</meta>

...the Holy Trinity clothed Her—through the hand of Divine Wisdom—with the Royal Robe of Eternal Virginity; and the contrary life was renewed and set right once again.

**5. Point or Circumstance.** The effects and resulting outcomes of all this were those that follow here: **1.** Such a change was brought about that, whereas She was previously only the daughter of Joachim and Anna, She now became through this the high-born Child, Daughter, and Heir of Divine Wisdom herself. **2.** She was thereby restored to the Virginal state which Eve lost in Paradise. **3.** She was, in a special manner, translated and brought back from a sinful state into an immaculate state of purity. Her spirit became blameless, the instrumental powers of her intellect and will became without blemish, and her soul without spot or wrinkle. In a word, her entire inner man was restored to that degree and measure of holiness of which the Apostle speaks in Ephesians 1:4: *To be without blame* <note>original: "ohne Tadel"; meaning to be in a blameless or irreproachable state.</note> *before Him* <note>original: "vor Ihme"; specifically, before the eye of Eternity Itself.</note> *in love* <note>original: "in Liebe"; which the author defines here as the Virginal Essence of Divine Wisdom.</note>. See also Ephesians 5:27, where this Virginity is explicitly called "immaculate." **4.** For She was first freed from original sin and its stains—the bloody discharge <term>bloody discharge: original "bluthigem Eytherflusse." In mystical literature, this refers to the hereditary corruption of human nature resulting from the Fall, often described in medical or biological imagery.</term> <margin>(a) Psalm 38:6, Isaiah 1:6.</margin> which otherwise could not have been separated from us. Regarding this, a deeper sense prompted the fervent prayer of the Royal Prophet: *Wash me, and I shall be whiter than snow* (Psalm 51:9). This is what God also promises to do for His Church in Ezekiel 36:25. This is the true Water-Baptism—indeed, to be baptized with the Water of Life itself, which is as clear as crystal and flows from the Throne of God and the Lamb. With this, Divine Wisdom baptizes (though in an invisible manner) all Her true children.

<column-break/>

# External Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ
<sig>E 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: or</meta>

And the effect and outcome of this baptism—this clear water (as the prophet Ezekiel emphatically calls it)—is that it washes away and separates all sin and selfhood <note>original: "Selbsheit"; the egoistic nature or self-will that seeks its own end rather than God's.</note> from the spirits and souls of our inner man. This is, therefore, the constant mark of the true, Heavenly, and Evangelical Water-Baptism. **5.** Likewise, the advantages were not merely negative or privative; for through this, original righteousness was also introduced into Her as a supernatural principle of the Virginal Life. This life, **sixthly**, consists in the highest life of righteousness, holiness, and purity; and this, in turn, consists in the cessation or quieting of the mind from all longing, and of the will from all willing; and finally, in a withdrawal of the senses themselves from all earthly objects of this visible creation, in order to attain that perfect joy and total contentment which they can find only through life in a pure, holy, heavenly element. For the Virginal Life consists in the freedom of the spirit, mind, and will from nature and the creature, and in dominion over the earthly life. It consists in passive stillness <note>original: "leydender Stille"; a state of mystical receptivity where the soul does not act on its own but waits to be moved by God.</note>: not to move unless one is moved; not to stir unless one is first stirred; nor to act in the slightest unless one is acted upon. It is to see nothing and know nothing except what Wisdom has breathed into the spirit; nor to will or desire anything except when Wisdom moves and works within our seeing, knowing, willing, imagining, and thinking. It consists in a perfect union with the Father of Spirits, so that whoever possesses it speaks, fellowships, and walks in the Spirit. Finally, it consists in the true inward and spiritual service and worship of God, and in Divine Wisdom in Her principle of Light; waiting at the doorposts of Wisdom to hear Her voice, to obey Her laws, and to heed and follow Her counsels.

# The First Little Treatise on the
<meta>catchword: RE-</meta>

...or regarding the necessity why Mary was made into a true Virgin in such a manner, we may consider: **1.** That the prophecies which speak of Her as such could not have been fulfilled in any other way according to their full and corresponding true sense. One such example is the glorious prophecy in Isaiah 7:14, compared with Matthew 1:23: *Behold, a virgin shall conceive* <note>original: "Sihe eine Jungfrau wird schwanger werden &c."</note> etc. **2.** That the title of Virgin could not belong to Her in any other form; yet this title is given to Her more than once in Holy Scripture; and God names things with such names that correspond with the true state in which they stand. **3.** That otherwise Jesus would not have been born of a Virgin: Nor **4.** would Mary herself have been a Virginal Mother: For She must have been a Virginal Mother. **5.** That She had to be the Daughter of Wisdom in just such a true and proper sense through a spiritual birth, just as She was previously the daughter of Eve through a natural birth. For the fruit and branches of every tree share in the nature of the root itself, and are either corrupt or good, just as the root itself is. So that, just as our natural mother, because she was sinful, first conceived us in sin; so also Wisdom, because She Herself is a Virgin, can likewise bring forth and produce no others than pure, immaculate, and virginal children. But these are the mysteries of the New Birth; which *no eye has seen, no ear has heard, nor has it entered into the heart of man to conceive* <note>A paraphrase of 1 Corinthians 2:9, often used in mystical literature to describe direct spiritual experience.</note>.

# The Second Little Treatise
### On the Mystical and Inner Incarnation of Jesus Christ /
->or<-
### His Birth in Us, and Our Birth in Him.
->A Discourse /<-
->In which the nature of the New Birth is revealed.<-

### The First Chapter.
### Reflections on the Love of God in Christ / regarding the Birth of Christ in Man.

->Instruction<-

1. Now here behold the wonderful love of the Father / in that He sent His Son into the corrupt world / into corrupt humanity / into the corrupt soul / to be born in you / so that He might also die in you / and shed His heavenly blood in you / and thereby extinguish the Father's grim and wrathful fire / and also rise again in your soul-forms <term>soul-forms: a term from mystical theology (likely Böhmean) referring to the various qualities or "gestalts" that make up the human soul</term> from the death of God's wrath / and thus finally ascend through you again into the Father’s glory.

2. Behold further the love of Jesus Christ / the Son of God / in that He thus descends from the glory of the Father into the corrupt essences <term>essences: the fundamental spiritual "substances" or building blocks of the soul</term> of your soul / to be born there in you / as a seed / and as a child / also to die therein in the death of the Father’s wrath / which was revealed in your soul’s essences after the Fall: yes, to speak even more clearly / to be crucified by the dark world / and by the devils / as inhabitants in the wrath-fire of the Father’s property: and all that / so that / through this His death and crucifixion

...overcome death, the curse, and the devil: and might pave a way through everything in His accomplished Ascension into His Father's Glory, from which He descended. But the majesty and the nobility, as I may call it, of this truly great and astonishing work consists in this: namely, that it is begun, continued, and completed or perfected in the power and might of JESUS Christ: that it is maintained even against the storm-winds of hell, against the cleverness, cunning, and violence of all fallen angels together—not even excluding Lucifer himself—against the great red dragon <note>A reference to the adversary described in Revelation 12, symbolizing the forces of chaos and evil that oppose the divine birth.</note> and the spirit of this world; that it happens invincibly against the united storm-winds, tempests, and assaults of all of them; yes, that it is continued with wind and tide in an irresistible, triumphant manner that is fitting for the Son of God in every way and consistent with His Highness; or to sum everything up in one word: the nobility and majesty of this great work consists in this, that the Father's grimness and fire of wrath are thereby extinguished and Divine Justice is reconciled.

Therefore, marvel with all the holy angels, and unite yourself with them in the contemplation of the same, and praise the Lamb for it and thank Him from now until eternity. And so that it may never be forgotten by you, write the process of it entirely into your heart, and make the impressions so deep and lasting that they can no longer be erased eternally. Only beware first of all that the excess of your joy does not raise you above your goal and measure into spiritual pride <term>spiritual pride: An occupational hazard for the mystic where one takes personal credit for divine gifts or feels superior to others due to their spiritual experiences.</term>; considering that in all this there is nothing that you can actually call your own; nothing that you have not received; and nothing but what is worked through the sole power of the Son of God. For to have the same thing actually done in us is one thing; but to see, recognize, understand, and grasp it as being worked or done is another thing. The work may be essentially and truthfully worked and done in millions of souls, and they may nevertheless not know it nor recognize it; so that this is a degree of mercy which is given to you above and beyond that, so that you have the spirit of faith, which gives you this special testimony in the spirit of His gradual progress, from this first departure to His Second Coming and the Birth of Divinity <note>original: "Gottheits-Gebuhrt." This is the first benefit: and the second is no less than this; but before yet...

Now, this faith is, as it were, the hand of the soul <note>A common metaphor in 17th-century divinity, suggesting that faith is the reaching out and grasping of God's promises.</note> through which she receives these benefits and makes them her own: and it is required by God as the condition on our part to believe in Christ—and that in His birth, life, death, etc., both outside of us and within us, etc., both literally and mystically.

### Chapter II.

->**Warnings against Spiritual Pride through the false appropriation of the titles and prerogatives of Christ to the inner man, as if in the state of Rebirth.**<-

The soul, or the inner man <note>term: "inner man" (inwendiger Mensch). In mystical theology, this refers to the spiritual core of a person that is being renewed by Christ, as opposed to the "outer" or worldly self.</term>, in whom this work is done or wrought, can hardly restrain herself from

<meta>catchword: puffing-</meta>

***

<page-num>81</page-num>
<header>inner Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ.</header>
<sig>F 3</sig>

puffing herself up and elevating herself beyond her proper measure, namely by appropriating to herself those very titles and claiming them as her own which belong to Christ alone—and alongside these titles, also the special privileges, prerogatives, promises, and prophecies that are attached to them; unless she distinguishes carefully enough between the Person of Christ and her own person, in which the other only dwells. As for example: because one of those titles given to Christ, as a seed of life within us, is "the Son of God," the soul or the inner man is quite inclined to call itself the Son of God, the LORD God, etc. And therefore, when it speaks, even if of itself, it must immediately and confidently be in that haughty manner of speaking: "Thus says the LORD-God." And so, through the inspiration of the Devil, she also claims and assumes the privileges and rights of Christ, even though they belong to Him alone. And because He was born of a pure Virgin without sin, and was a perfect LORD and King, the soul is consequently very prone to harbor such thoughts of herself, as if she herself no longer recognized any sin, but were altogether perfectly a pure lord and king to rule over sin, over the devils, and over the world. Yes, she will make no conscience of calling herself "Immanuel" <note>Hebrew for "God with us." The author warns that a believer should not claim to be the literal incarnation of God.</note> and explaining the prophecies concerning Him as being fulfilled in herself. But in all of this, the inner man (as is clear and manifest) is miserably deceived, and instead of triumphing over hell and the devil, he truly makes himself a mockery and a laughingstock to them.

Another cause or fountainhead of Spiritual Pride <note>original: "Geistlichen Hoffarth"</note> is the false imaginings and misinterpreted concepts of the work itself; and the misunderstanding and error is this: namely, because this work of Christ—His Birth as a seed of life, His death, resurrection, and ascension—was entirely done in the essences, properties, and forms of His

<meta>catchword: His</meta>

<summary>This page warns believers against "Spiritual Pride," where a person undergoing spiritual rebirth mistakenly claims the divine titles and sinless nature of Christ for their own ego. The author emphasizes that while Christ dwells within the believer, the individual remains distinct from the Savior and must avoid the delusion of self-deification.</summary>

<meta>The previous page concluded with a discussion of Christ’s work on behalf of the soul and the necessity of seasoning one's joy with the "salt" of humility. This page moves into a warning against spiritual pride—specifically the error of believing that because Christ’s work is finished historically, the individual soul has no further spiritual labor to perform.</meta>

<page-num>82</page-num>
<header>The Second Little Treatise on the</header>

of his own soul; thus the inner man immediately concludes that, therefore, the work is now consequently also fully done and completed in Him. Now, it is indeed completed on Christ’s side: but what a wretched ground of **spiritual pride** is this, that it wants to base itself upon it? The end of all this is still yet to come, namely Christ’s second coming in the Spirit <note>The author refers to the internal experience of the Holy Spirit as a "second coming," distinct from the historical return of Christ at the end of time; this is the subjective realization of the Gospel within the believer.</note>, as on the Day of Pentecost, to give the Holy Spirit, so that He might make an end of sin, death, hell, and the curse in the **New Man**, and introduce everlasting righteousness and Eternal Life, so that the Holy Spirit in His working power might become all in all. According to this, the knowledge and recognition that this is not yet done will preserve the soul in the gentle **humility of Christ**: whereas, on the other hand, to think or imagine that it is already done serves only to puff oneself up with **Luciferian pride**, in which Lucifer himself must necessarily rejoice. For it fills the unwary soul with negligent laziness, making her imagine she now has nothing more to do than to always rejoice, and to praise and extol the free grace and rich mercy of God in gladness and exultation, and to live according to her own pleasure under a strange pretext of **Christian freedom** <note>The author is criticizing "Antinomianism"—the belief that because of divine grace, a person is free from the moral law and no longer needs to strive for personal holiness.</note>. This is an imagination which, once the Devil has thoroughly impressed it upon the mind, actually throws into a heap and frustrates all the benefits that Christ has previously worked in such a soul, and prevents her forever from finishing and completing that work which Christ left for her to complete within herself. In case you ask me what kind of work that is, which Christ has left for the soul or the inner man to complete between His Ascension and His Return in the Spirit? Then I answer, that such a work is to be **reborn in Christ**, to live in the faith of Christ, and to obey His teachings and commandments, to follow His footsteps on the path of rebirth, and to display in one's outer man the exact image of Christ in His suffering, death, resur-

<column-break/>

<page-num>83</page-num>
<header>inner Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.</header>
<sig>F 4</sig>

rection and ascension, in order to live and reign with Him in the Heavenly life.

A third occasion and cause of **spiritual pride** is also the knowledge of the mystery of Christ itself, which (alas!) is misused by far too many to judge others as ignorant, to belittle them, and to regard them as nothing. For you must know that there is a twofold knowledge of Christ; the one is **Historical**, which originates merely from the external letter; the other is **Mystical**, which originates from the Mystery: The first concerns Christ *outside* of them, and the work done in His own person from His birth until His final ascension; the other considers Him as He is *within* them themselves, and works and performs all those same things in their own particular persons which He formerly did outside of them <note>This "internalization" of the life of Jesus—where the soul undergoes its own spiritual birth, death, and resurrection—is a cornerstone of 17th-century mystical theology.</note>. And this gives me the guidance to distinguish Christians into two kinds; namely, into **Historical** and **Mystical**: However, I must reserve this distinction for the content of the following chapter.

### Chapter III.
->Of Historical and Mystical Christians.<-

I proceed now to the description of this distinction. There are, therefore, as has been said, two kinds of Christians or believers; **Historical** and **Mystical**. The **Historical Christian** confesses that he believes in the letter of the Gospel <note>original: "Evangelii"</note>, that Christ was God and Man, truly born of the blessed Virgin, lived in the world, and died the true death on the cross, was buried, actually lay in the grave, descended into hell, rose again on the third day, and after a short time ascended into heaven in an open and visible manner, triumphing victoriously. All this he believes, and further he believes not. Whereas, on the other hand, the **Mystical and Spiritual Christian**, alongside this entire historical account of the external and literal Christ, also believes the mystery of the same...

The **Historical Believers** <note>Those who focus primarily on the external, historical facts of Jesus' life and the literal words of scripture.</note> cry out vehemently against the **Mystical Christians** and denounce them as "new lights," **enthusiasts** <note>original: "Enthusiasten" — at the time, a derogatory term for those claiming direct personal inspiration from God, often viewed as fanatics.</note> or "spirit-drivers," even as heretics, apostates, or those who cause division and schism in the church, and claim that they are therefore worthy of being cast out and banished. This senseless severity, bitterness, and false judgment, however, can never be of God. The **Mystical Christian**, on the other hand, judges the Historical one and decrees that he is no true Christian, but rather a fleshly, literal, and mere **mouth-Christian** <note>One whose Christianity exists only in speech, lacking internal transformation.</note>, devoid of all power and life; but this too should not be; for it tastes of spiritual pride and is likewise from and of the devil.

The entire discord, strife, and quarreling is merely, as the saying goes, over a **hollow nut** <note>An idiom meaning something worthless or a triviality.</note>, of no importance and not worth remembering. And to show that this is so, let us weigh both sides in an equal balance, and according to the just weight of truth, regarding both the <term>Credenda</term> <note>Latin: "things to be believed"; the doctrines or articles of faith.</note>, or teachings necessary to believe, and the <term>Agenda</term> <note>Latin: "things to be done"; the practical duties and conduct of a believer.</note>, or those things one must do and practice; and I dare to assure you that, when everything is precisely weighed, it will be found that neither party has a just cause to persist in their shouting and complaints against the other, because the level balance will remain at the proper value it had from the beginning. We will begin with the **things to be believed**, and herein it is clear:

1. That the Historical as well as the Mystical believer have precisely one and the same object of faith, namely, **JESUS Christ**. Might I then not justly ask and say with the words of the Apostle: **Is Christ divided?** Christ, whether He be from without or from within, is indeed always Christ, and only one Person. Indeed, Christ within each individual Christian is not multiplied into a greater number than the one and only Christ; but remains

just the same/ although in many births, in different ages, and as if in different degrees. Can, therefore, the different considerations of Him, as inward or outward, justify all these heated and vehement disputes that we have among one another? Let us rather consider that we are brothers more than once over, and therefore, instead of one judging the other, let us all remain in love and wait for the **Comforter**, the Holy Spirit, who may settle and reconcile our dispute, which must necessarily vanish upon His arrival. The **Mystical Christian**, however, likewise requires faith as a necessary condition and attribute; wherein then lies the difference?

The Historical Believer further requires not only a common faith, but that faith which expresses and manifests itself in manifold special effects: the first of which is to grasp the history or story of Christ in all its circumstances; the second is to give assent and agreement to the truth of the same; a third is, they say, to depend upon it, to rest within it, to set one's hope upon it, and to trust in it for salvation; and a fourth is to appropriate all these things to oneself in particular. And in all these, I am assured there can be no essential difference between the Historical and the Mystical Christian.

The Historical Believer requires a fifth effect of faith, in that he wills that one should give glory to God, to Christ, and to His free grace for the work of redemption that was accomplished in Christ’s own person, for the benefits and advantages that accrue to him thereby, in believing that Christ did all

Thus, this is ultimately the main point, the center, and the basis of the entire difference between them.

But to remove from him this error in the high conceit he has formed of himself, let us grant him, as is only fair, that Christ in us is the substance and the self-existent essence, and in no other way than inwardly. But how can the Mystical Christian say that this substance, which he calls Christ in him, is not also in the Historical Believer, even if at such a time he perhaps does not understand it, nor is he in the least conscious of such an inner work or actual experience (of which the other boasts so highly)? For to have the work done is one thing, and to perceptibly recognize what it is, is another—according to the distinction already made in the first chapter of this little treatise. For there is no absolute necessity for it to be felt and known <note>The author argues that God can be working within a person even if that person does not "feel" a mystical connection or have a conscious "experience" of it.</note>. The Historical Believer is presupposed, through the entire process of the argument, to live according to his faith in and according to the commandments of good conduct. Now, even the most mystical Christian can do no more than this: where then remains any occasion for boasting? And on what apparent grounds can he now despise his believing brother? But if he continues to do so, might not the Historical...

Believer answer the Mystic thus: that because he claims the entire inward fulfillment (which he so earnestly strives to defend), such as the birth, life, death, as well as the resurrection and ascension of Christ—yet, when he has ascended into the glory of His Father through the properties and forms of the soul, he himself cannot pretend that he has Christ within him, but rather that he has gone far away from Him <note>The author uses the mystic's own logic against him: if the mystic claims to have "ascended" spiritually like Christ, then he has technically "departed" from his own internal presence.</note>. Then they might live together in the gentle humility of the true Christ in the Spirit, and be found striving in nothing but the work and labor of love, in active assistance to one another, and in bearing one another's loads and burdens!

From this now flows a word of terror to both parties—a word to shake the "old heavens" and the "old earth" of both the Historical and the Mystical believers, and to topple the foundations upon which they have built until now. This is done so that others may follow in their place which, according to the words of the Apostle in Hebrews 12:27 <note>The verse says: "And this word, Yet once more, signifieth the removing of those things that are shaken, as of things that are made, that those things which cannot be shaken may remain."</note>, can no longer be moved. Thus, I may awaken both at once to a righteous sense of their condition, and then finally—after they are thoroughly penetrated by terror—I may work upon them or make an impression on them, so that they might let these old foundations pass away and exchange them for those which are truly new, better, and more lasting. But this shall be the content of the following chapter.

<header>The Second Little Treatise on the</header>

## Chapter IV.
### Necessary Admonitions and Warnings to the Mystical Christian.

Following this, to make a beginning again with the Mystical Believer, it is my earnest plea to let him know that it is good for him to feel a fear and terror within himself. It is beneficial for all his foundations—I mean his former works of faith—to be shaken, and for the useless, stinking, and foul blood of spiritual pride <term>Hoffarth</term> <note>Hoffarth is an archaic term for a specific kind of haughty, spiritual arrogance where one feels superior due to their religious experiences.</note> and self-exaltation over others, as well as his fleshly confidence and presumption, to be drained away. This is so that he may be brought down from his high and arrogant imaginings, becoming a little child in the gentle humility <term>Demuth</term> of Jesus, and may work out his salvation with fear and trembling.

Let me present to you—as an unfailing mirror in which you may fully examine yourself—a few astonishing and fearsome reflections in the following specific points and circumstances:

I. First, for example, several Scriptural passages which clearly and distinctly show that, despite Christ’s indwelling in you and all your resulting acts of faith, there is nonetheless still a possibility that you could fall away again.

II. Further and second, other passages that encourage and admonish you not to get stuck here or simply build upon what you have, but to go further, to press onward, and to persevere until the end.

III. And third and last, the examples of those who have actually fallen away from the state in which you now stand; some into Luciferian pride <note>Referring to the fall of the angel Lucifer, used here as a warning against the pride that comes from high spiritual status.</note>; others into a purely earthly and worldly-minded state. These, I hope, may all together wake you from sleep in time and bring you to a true, essential feeling of your condition.

I. Accordingly, I begin with those terrifying Scripture-

<meta>catchword: Scripture-</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>91</page-num>
<header>inner Prayer and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.</header>

Scripture-passages which clearly and distinctly witness that, despite the indwelling of Christ in you and all your resulting acts of faith, there is nonetheless still a possibility that you could fall away again. Such is, first, the passage from Paul in 1 Corinthians 13:1: *And though I speak with the tongues of men and of angels, and have not love, I have become as sounding brass or a tinkling bell.* And though I had the gift of prophecy, and understood all mysteries, and had all knowledge <term>Erkanntnus</term> <note>The author distinguishes between historical "knowledge" and deep, experiential "recognition" or "insight."</note>, and though I had all faith, so that I could move mountains, but had not love, I would nonetheless be nothing. And again in verse 8: *Whether there be prophecies, they shall be done away; or tongues, they shall cease; or knowledge, it shall vanish away.* To all of these, Paul contrasts love as the end, the substance, and the perfection of all, which cannot cease. And so it is in truth, as you yourself will recognize and find, if you have ever enjoyed the divine love of Christ burning in your heavenly heart. You will fully agree with what he testified shortly before in 1 Corinthians 8:1: namely, *that knowledge puffs up.* Inasmuch as he speaks of all knowledge, or of knowledge in general: and if historical knowledge does this, mystical knowledge will do so much more. In contrast, love builds up and improves, because it is the gentle love of God in the soul, which builds it up and improves it in humility.

Consider further what is testified in Hebrews 3:14: *For we have become partakers of Christ, if only we hold the beginning (or principle) of His self-existent essence* <term>Selbständiges Wesen</term> <note>original: "Selbständiges Wesen." This refers to the substantial reality of Christ's nature being formed within the believer, rather than just a mental idea of Him.</note> *firm until the end.* And again in verse 6 of that same chapter: *Moses indeed was faithful as a servant, but Christ as the Son over His own house; whose house we are, if only we hold fast the confidence and the rejoicing of hope until the end (or to perfection).* And again in verse 1 of this same chapter: *Therefore, you holy brothers,*

He cites 1 Corinthians and Hebrews to emphasize that even those with great spiritual knowledge can fall away if they do not persevere to the end.</summary>
