<page-num>92</page-num>
<header>The Second Little Treatise on the</header>

...you who are partakers of the heavenly calling, pay close attention in your minds to the Apostle and High Priest of our confession, Christ Jesus. <note>original: "der/ die Ihr des Uberhimmlischen Beruffs theilhafftig seyd...". This is a translation of Hebrews 3:1.</note>

From all these scriptural passages, several specific observations are to be drawn: 

1. That such things have been witnessed and spoken through the Spirit of Truth. 
2. That their sense and meaning are truly terrifying; especially as they clearly and distinctly indicate and give to understand that the state of those who have Christ within them is still a changeable state, and not yet a fully established one. 
3. That these words—considering and reflecting upon the One from whom they come or were spoken—could never have been spoken in vain, much less could they cause terror where there was no righteous ground or cause for fear. 
4. They contain within themselves, and clearly reveal, that the work of Christ in us has a beginning and an end; the first part or beginning of which, before the work can be brought to its proper perfection, must be swallowed up by the latter part or the end. 
5. And even more specifically: that accordingly, the Mystical Believer, if he does not wish to deceive himself, must not remain stuck in the beginning of this work, nor be satisfied with it and rest therein. 
6. It is no less clear from these same passages that at the very same time, the Holy Spirit truly testifies and confesses through them that the birth of Christ in them—their participation in Him, from His Divinity and Humanity—is nonetheless true, essential <term>wesentlich</term>, and substantial <term>selbständig</term>. <note>These terms emphasize that the spiritual experience is not just a feeling, but a real "substance" or "essence" being born within the soul.</note> This may therefore serve as a constant warning to those who deny that this is so, and who immediately ascribe everything of this nature to fantasy and imagination. For if that were the case, the Holy Spirit would have had to say: "you have indeed become partakers of Christ, yet not in truth, but only in appearance." But if this participation were not essential, the Holy Spirit would not have encouraged them to go forward in it and to persevere until the end in that which was merely a false, outward

<column-break/>

<page-num>93</page-num>
<header>Inner Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.</header>

appearance. 

7. Since I observed in the fourth point generally that the work of Christ has a beginning and an end, I will now, in this last point, remind you again what this beginning and this end are. According to this, the beginning of the work of Christ in us looks to His birth, death, resurrection, and ascension within us; upon which—even though they are true, essential, and substantial—one can nonetheless build no permanent foundation, as has already been said. But the end is the birth of Christ in the Spirit within us. For after He ascended, He promised to come again a second time, to be seen without sin by those who wait for Him for salvation <margin>(a) Hebr. 9 v 28.</margin>; that is, to give them the Comforter <term>Tröster</term> <note>The "Comforter" refers to the Holy Spirit (from the Greek 'Parakletos'). In this context, it represents the final, stabilizing stage of the soul's union with God.</note>, whose coming is to lay the finishing hand upon the work and to bring the whole work to perfection. He is the one who makes an end of sin in the soul and brings in eternal righteousness in its place; He also swallows up hell, eternal death, the curse, and the wrath of God into His eternal, essential love. For love must finally triumph over all, and then the earthly life will be swallowed up by the Heavenly; which Heavenly life shall also, as Lord, rule and reign over the spirit of this world. And such a birth of Jesus Christ in you—in such an age, measure, and degree—is a firm and constant ground upon which to build your hope of eternal life.

If we now add to this the Parable of the Sower, according to Matthew 13, verses 3 to 9; then it is certain that Jesus Christ is the one who goes out to sow. He does this either immediately, in His own person when He was on earth, or mediately, through authority in His disciples and ministers of the Word. The seed that He thus sows is His pure Divinity and Eternal Humanity. The soil into which He sows this seed is the inner man of the heart, that is, the human soul.

<summary>The author explains that the initial mystical experiences of Christ's birth and resurrection within a person are only a "beginning" and remain unstable. True spiritual security only comes with the "End"—the perfecting work of the Holy Spirit (the Comforter) which establishes a permanent foundation of eternal love.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's argument that the spiritual "seed" of Christ in a believer is not guaranteed to remain if the believer is negligent. <page-num>94</page-num>
<header>The Second Little Treatise on the</header>
<meta>catchword: still</meta>

But here I must ask: does the fruit in the parable fully answer the expectation of the sower? To which the response is: no, not at all. What was the cause, then, that it failed, and what was lacking? Answer: Not in the seed: for it was of one kind, and the very same seed that was sown throughout, and therefore an equal growth and prospering should rightfully have followed; from which it is clearly and distinctly to be seen that the fault lay in the soil. Know therefore that although the word [which is nothing else than **Christ in you**, as a seed of life] is sown into you, it may yet not have deep enough earth in you, and thus not take root in you, but wither; or else be burned by the burning or scorching heat of the **wrath of God**: <note>In the mystical tradition of Jacob Böhme, the "wrath of God" (Zorn Gottes) represents the harsh, fire-like quality of nature without the tempering light of Divine Love.</note> or be choked by the cares and thorns of this world in the earthly animalistic life, and thus either cannot spring up, or finally be devoured by the birds of the air—that is, by the devil and other evil spirits, his companions, who live in the wrath of God—on the high thorny path of your heart or soul. Therefore, even if we might confess with St. Peter that Christ is born in you, not of perishable but of **imperishable** <note>original: "unvergänglich"</note> seed, which lives and remains forever: yet the very birth of Christ in you may still perish. Should you perhaps wish to use the attached word, *imperishable*, to your advantage, and conclude therefrom against what I assert in this case; then I will help you out of your error in the following answers: 1. Firstly, that the seed is such is an occasion and actual tinder: <note>The author uses "tinder" (Zunder) to suggest that the presence of the divine seed can actually ignite a greater fall if misused, as it provides the "fuel" for spiritual pride or rebellion.</note> and indeed both in the fallen angels and in your first father **Adam**. For both **Lucifer** and **Adam** in Paradise had this eternal, immortal, and imperishable seed of Divine Love in them, and were of a much higher rank and a hundred times more strongly graced with it than you, at your best, might ever be. So that, because they brought forth a monstrous birth, <note>original: "Misgebuhrt." This refers to a spiritual failure or a perverted manifestation of life.</note> you

<column-break/>

<page-num>95</page-num>
<header>inner Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ.</header>
<sig>G 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: 4. Where</meta>

have all the more and greater reason to live in fear. 2. And even if the seed sown in you is in itself immortal and imperishable, and thus in and for itself incapable of corruption from eternity to eternity; yet it may nonetheless perish in a literal sense with regard to you; its perishing, however—to speak in clear and distinct words—is nothing other than its sinking back into its **Eternal Essence**, <term>Essence (Essenz): The fundamental spiritual substance of a thing in mystical theology.</term> and thus allowing itself to be enclosed in the Father's wrath, through which captivity it can take its way back into eternity and return to God and Christ, from whence it initially came. This, I say, is its dying, miscarriage, and extinguishing in you, namely its fading away in you, so that neither life nor movement of it is felt in you anymore.

But should someone wish to object further against what is said here and say that this seed is not only an imperishable seed in and for itself, but also, according to **1 John 3:9**, a constantly remaining seed, of which it is said: *Whoever is born of God does not commit or work sin; for His seed remains in Him*; from which the conclusion is both easy to make and follows naturally: that wherever it is once born, there it also remains forever and eternally until the end. To which I again answer as distinctly as I ever can and am able (since it is the refuge and the chief scriptural passage that the **Mystical Christian** has, to which he is accustomed to flee) and indeed in these following specific points and circumstances.

1. That the seed, merely in and for itself, is indeed an imperishable and thus, for that reason, also a remaining seed.

2. That it does not follow from this, however, that it must therefore also be a remaining seed with regard to the person or his place of residence.

3. That it is indeed a remaining seed precisely in regard to that, because and as long as it is itself present there.

<summary>The author explains that while the "seed" of Christ is immortal, it can still "perish" within an individual if the person's soul provides poor soil, citing the falls of Adam and Lucifer as warnings. He begins to refute the idea that "once born of God, always remaining" by distinguishing between the seed's eternal nature and its temporary presence in a person.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s theological exploration of the "spiritual seed." He is specifically refuting the idea that once the seed of Christ is planted in a person, their salvation is automatically permanent and unshakeable regardless of their subsequent spiritual state.</meta>

4. But if this should not suffice, I must—in order to give a fuller and clearer answer—first state and lay down as a foundation that this **Eternal and abiding Seed** has many and various births within the **inner man**; <note>The "inner man" is a concept from Pauline theology, referring to the spiritual or hidden self that is being renewed by God.</note> some of which are only beginning; others are progressing, growing, and increasing; and others, finally, are the perfecting births of completeness and total consummation. And from this distinction between the different births of the **Divine Seed**, the answer is both easy and clear as to which of these births St. John refers to in the cited passage as the **imperishable seed**, indeed, an **abiding seed**: namely, not the beginning birth, for that is a birth of weakness and is therefore still subject to miscarriage; <note>The author uses the metaphor of "miscarriage" (Mißgebehrung) to describe a spiritual life that starts but fails to reach maturity.</note> nor even the progressing or increasing birth, for these likewise can still result in miscarriages. Therefore, it must be the completely perfected birth. For this is in truth a strong birth: a birth in **Masculine Power**. In this birth, the **Male Child** (as seen expressed in Revelation 12:5) is born, which final birth is the birth of Christ’s second coming, <note>In this mystical context, the "second coming" refers to an internal spiritual event where Christ's presence is fully realized in the believer's soul.</note> wherein the Holy Spirit is given—which did not happen in the preceding births. And in this birth, yes, in this alone, is the seed an **abiding seed**, etc.

5. My final or last answer, however, is that these words are not spoken of **Christ’s birth in you**, but of **your birth in Him**; *whoever is born of God*, says the text, that is, out of Christ or from God. Now, having Christ born in us is one thing, and conversely, for us or in respect to us, being born in Christ is another. **His birth in us** is only a preparation for **our birth in Him**; and after His birth in us is completed in His **Ascension**, <note>The "Ascension" here likely refers to the spiritual stage where the Christ-life within the believer rises above earthly attachments.</note> our birth in Him usually only then begins for the first time. So that it is, by chance, a great error and deception when one says: "Christ is born in me, therefore I am also born in Him," and as much as on this counter-

---

<page-num>97</page-num>
<header>Inner Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.</header>
<sig>G 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: Leib-</meta>

-argument. Now we also proceed to the other point and circumstance, namely:

II. Certain passages of Scripture, in turn, which encourage you not to stand still here, or to cling to and build upon this, but to go further, to press forward, and to endure until the end.

You will perhaps answer here (I am still speaking to the **Mystic Believer**): "Jesus Christ, as a seed of life, has not only had a birth in me, but His death and passion have also had their birth in me; His **Heavenly Blood** has been shed in me, etc., so that this must finally be admitted as a certain and immovable foundation." But before I answer further, I must ask you to consider and weigh frequently and earnestly in your deepest thoughts what the words written in Revelation 13:8 mean: *The Lamb that was slain from the foundation of the world.* By the **Lamb**, it is generally understood that Jesus Christ, the Son of God, is meant, who—I say—in His Divinity, as united with His **Eternal Humanity**, was slain and crucified. To this, you will perhaps answer and say it happened in His purpose, counsel, and foreknowledge, and that He was thus slain in this particular text. This I grant; but I add further that, besides this slaying of the Lamb which exists only in thought and purpose, He was also **actually and essentially slain**. And indeed, first, in the **Fall of the Angels**: <note>The "Fall of the Angels" refers to the rebellion of Lucifer. The author suggests that because the angels were part of God’s divine order, their rebellion "slew" the divine life within their specific sphere of existence.</note> for they had Divine essence, self-subsistence, and corporality from God's Divine Wisdom within them; they had the **heavenly blood** of Divine love and eternal life in them, the blood of the Son of God, which flowed in and through all their properties. And when they became devils through their fall, this blood was shed, the Divine self-subsistence, etc., was crucified and slain in them, and that must indeed have been before the foundation of the world. Adam also had this very same spiritual corporality—

<note>The text begins mid-sentence, continuing from the previous page's discussion of divine nature.</note> -ness and **Heavenly Blood**, which were actually slain and crucified again, not long after the foundations of the world were laid. Are you then stronger than the angels who fell? Are you greater than your father Adam? If you say so, then your **spiritual pride** proves enough that you have actually fallen into that very same damnation. For they were both ten thousand times more highly "graduated" <note>The author uses the academic or alchemical term "graduated" to describe the level of spiritual refinement or state of being.</note> in the **Heavenly Blood** of the Son of God than you can be in your present state. Consider, therefore, how strongly this scripture passage from Hebrews 6:6 shines in your eyes: *as those who crucify the Son of God for themselves all over again.* From these words, note again the following circumstances:

1. That it is no unavoidable necessity that you must crucify the Son of God anew; rather, it is presented only as a possibility.

2. That the person thus crucified is the **Son of God**, Jesus Christ, and no other.

3. That the place and location where He is thus crucified is not in Heaven, but on Earth; and where else there, than within **yourself**.

4. That His crucifixion occurring anew in you indicates a preceding crucifixion, which He had previously undergone in His own person upon the Cross.

5. That this blood crucified in you is not His earthly, but His **Heavenly Blood** (for the other can no longer be crucified); the Divine and Spiritual Blood of Eternal Love and Eternal Life, which is shed in your own soul. So that in this way—even if you are already a partaker of it—you are nevertheless not yet past the terror that is truly indicated here.

Read also the same Apostle in Hebrews 10:29: *How much worse a punishment (do you think) will be judged for him who has trodden the Son of God underfoot, and considered the Blood of the Covenant, by which he was sanctified, as an unholy thing.* Thus he clearly and distinctly sets forth that the Heavenly [Blood], which

which he calls the **Blood of the Covenant**, was first received in the **inner man** and then trodden underfoot—that is, held in too low esteem and despised, even regarded as common—wherein he further adds that such a person has insulted **Grace** (that is, God’s free mercy which had infused this into the soul). Now I proceed to the third and final circumstance. Namely:

### III. Regarding the Examples of Those Who Actually Fell from the State in Which You Yourself Are

Some fell into **Luciferian pride**, others into a purely earthly and worldly-minded state.

Here you will doubtless stop me again and claim that Christ has also arisen in you with great power as **Lord and Monarch**, as King, Priest, and Prophet in His New spiritual body; and that such a bright-shining garment of His Spiritual Body, once obtained, is difficult to lose again. However, I must in turn remind you of what has happened in similar cases. Thus, to say nothing of others, I will hold to my first-cited examples of **Adam** and the **fallen angels**, as these are the best known and against which one cannot easily raise an exception or objection. For I have already indicated above that they possessed Heavenly Bodies, Heavenly Flesh, and **Heavenly Blood**. Now I will only ask you yourself: was it not through their own and willing disobedience that they both lost these things? And then, consequently: might you not also lose these very same things yourself by sinning against your light or knowledge <note>The term "Erkandnus" refers to spiritual insight or experiential knowledge of God.</note> and against the reproof of your conscience? Especially since you testify to having them at present—or would you otherwise want to make God a respecter of persons? The very same scripture passages which testify to the crucifixion of Christ in us, after He has become alive again...

### Chapter V.
#### Necessary Admonitions and Warnings to the Historical or Literal Christian.

But so that we may proceed again to the <term>Historical or Literal Christian<note>The author uses these terms to describe someone who accepts the facts of the Bible as historical truth but has not experienced a "spiritual rebirth" or internal transformation.</note></term>, the highest necessity requires that He also be awakened to a right feeling of his condition. For if the danger to the inward and mystical Christian is as great as we have explained—so that no one might be arrogant—how then shall the outward, or Historical and Literal Christian, stand when God shall enter into judgment with Him? Let him therefore know that his foundations must be shaken to the core, which shall be demonstrated and made clear from the following considerations.

1. The Historical and Literal Christian believes, according to the testimony of the Scripture, quite rightly: that the birth of Jesus Christ occurred through the pure power of the Holy Spirit. He believes that His conception and birth occurred from a pure, unstained Virgin, who herein obtained the highest privilege over all others of Her sex; so much so, that She was also the most blessed or favored among all women. He believes that the Godhead took the Human Nature as Its own proper dwelling, and that the Eternal Word pitched His hut and <term>tabernacle<note>A reference to the "dwelling" of God among men, echoing the language of the Old Testament tent of meeting.</note></term> in our mortal flesh, and thus "became flesh" (John 1:14). And thus he further believes that the Person of Jesus Christ—this incarnate Word—was full of grace and truth, so that "from His fullness we may all receive" (v. 16).

And

<column-break/>

<page-num>103</page-num>
<header>->Inner Birth and Incarnation of Jesus Christ.<-</header>

And that His glory and revelation in all things was as the glory of the Only Begotten of the Father. Now, all of this is indeed a healthy foundation and ground upon which to build one's faith and hope for salvation: it is building upon the Rock, Christ. And the Apostles have clearly and plainly taught us that there is no other foundation than Jesus Christ: who is this Rock, and is truly God revealed in the flesh for the redemption of the lost human race. This foundation is indeed quite good and healthy; nevertheless, the Historical believer must know that he still falls far too short if he remains a stranger to the **Mystical Incarnation of Christ** within himself. Especially since even the devils themselves believe as much as this, and tremble because of it. For which reason I earnestly admonish you, O Historical Christian, to examine yourself closely in the faith of the Gospel which you believe, and to test, try, and search yourself to see whether you can find Jesus Christ in you. For if you do not find Him born in you; if you do not see His image brought forth in your corrupt humanity; if you do not catch and receive the Heavenly Light in the earthly darkness of your imprisoned soul; if the immortal and incorruptible Word is not revealed in the essences and properties of your inward man in such a way that it gives you power to be called a child of God and a member of Christ—why then do you call yourself a Christian? Consider then: what will it benefit you to believe that He was born of an external Virgin, if your soul remains continually in the impurity of **spiritual harlotry**? Or what benefit will it give you to believe that His wonderful conception occurred through the power of the Holy Spirit, if you are estranged from that same Holy Power and do not understand what is meant by the **Divine Overshadowing**? Consider, I pray you, what you are doing when you only believe in a historical manner that

2. The **Historical and Literal Christian** also believes—and quite rightly—the life of Jesus Christ as it is described in the history of the Four Evangelists; through unbelief, he denies none of the miracles that Christ worked through the mighty finger of God, nor any of His deeds and words; rather, he believes them all firmly and places his hope in them. And so far, this foundation is very good; but he must not let it rest there: for if he does so, it will be found much too inadequate and far too weak. For what will it help you, O believer-in-the-letter, to know the history of Christ’s life if Christ does not live in you, so that you can then say with the Apostle: *I live, yet not I, but Christ lives in me* (Galatians 2:20). ...or whether you live according to your own will in the flesh, or to God in the Spirit? If you live to the latter—namely, the Spirit—then your life is truly hidden with Christ, and His life has become yours. He lives in you, and you in Him; He is your Rock, and you build upon Him; He is your Wellspring, and you draw living water from Him. He is the spring of Israel within you, and the **fire-spirit** <note>In the mystical tradition of Jacob Boehme, the "fire-spirit" refers to the soul's natural, burning hunger and desire, which must be quenched and transformed by Divine Love.</note> of your inner man does not cease to drink from it continually. But where you live according to the earth, or according to your selfhood in the flesh, you are estranged from the life of Christ; estranged from the nature of God, and you are held captive by the power of the spirit of this world, and under the spirit of the dark world, in all those properties of your soul. And what communion then does Christ have with you, O man! What benefit will it bring you to know these Divine teachings which He left recorded for your good, if there is no **principle** <note>original: "Principium," meaning a fundamental source or starting point of life.</note> or beginning of His life within you that might lead you to follow them, or to understand those parables which He spoke concerning the Kingdom of God? 

If the mystery of the Kingdom of God is not opened within you, and you do not find its **pearl** <note>A reference to the "Pearl of Great Price" from the Gospel of Matthew, symbolizing the discovery of the Divine within the soul.</note> in your own inward ground and nature? Or if you cannot speak those prayers which He Himself prayed, or taught His disciples to pray, because the Spirit or the life is not in you—that which was in Him, and which alone can teach you how to pray as you ought? Or to hear the report and account of the miracles which He performed externally, if you do not have the power of those same miracles within you to work upon yourself, and to transform you out of the corruption of nature into the incorruptibility of the pure...

---

<page-num>107</page-num>

<meta>catchword: suffered</meta>

...**heavenly essence**, which is in Christ. If your soul, or inward man, is still blind, how are you any better than those who were externally blind before He opened their eyes? Which do you think is the greater blindness: the physical or the spiritual? 

So it is also when you are deaf to hearing the voice and the Word of God, which calls to you with a **gentle rushing or whispering** <note>Reference to the "still small voice" heard by Elijah in 1 Kings 19:12.</note> in your inward parts, even when the "day has become cool" <note>An allusion to Genesis 3:8, suggesting a time when the soul's passions have calmed, allowing God's voice to be heard.</note>—that is, when the harshness and bitterness of the rising **fire-spirit** within you is softened by the gentle water of the Divine life. What benefit or advantage will it bring you to hear of those who were miraculously cured of their physical deafness? Or how will it help you more to hear of the multitude who were possessed by evil spirits and delivered by Him when He appeared in our mortal flesh, if your soul remains a receptacle for evil and unclean spirits? Indeed, if it is not itself made into a temple and sanctuary of the Holy Spirit? 

In truth, if you knew or understood the power of God in Christ, you would not fail to prefer the **Inward Miracles** to the external ones; you would not be able to look upon your inner man as sick, or lame, palsied or **moon-struck** <note>original: "Mondsüchtig." But if this is not enough, I have more to add.

The knowledge, wisdom, and understanding that flows from the life of natural powers, or from the pure life of reason, we call **Rational**: But that knowledge, wisdom, and understanding which comes from the life of Divine powers, or from the life of God, is called **Spiritual**; and that indeed not so much because the objects of this wisdom and knowledge are Divine and Spiritual, but because the attainment and reaching of the same happens solely through the **infusion**, **engrafting**, and **revelation** of God. ### Chapter 28 v. 12: Where shall wisdom be found? and where is the place of understanding? In these words, Job makes a comparison between **Natural Knowledge**, which is attained through art and diligence, and **Spiritual Knowledge**, which is attained solely through infusion <note>Theological term for grace or knowledge "poured" directly into the soul by God.</note>. For here he speaks clearly of spiritual wisdom and spiritual understanding, and of the mystical knowledge of spiritual things, in such a way that it cannot be grasped by natural reason. It is exactly as if he had said in express words: where shall spiritual or infused wisdom be found? where is Her place or station? And where is the place of spiritual understanding? To which the answer is not expressed in clear words, yet is sufficiently indicated. For Her place is clearly to be understood as being in the Divine or Heavenly World, in the **Holy Principle of Light** <note>In the mystical tradition of Jacob Böhme, this refers to the realm of God’s love and clarity.</note>, in the openings of the Divine World, in the **Eternal Word**, through which all things (worlds) were made. There is Her place and station, and there alone, wherein Divine objects <note>original: *Vorwürffe*, meaning the prototypes or spiritual realities that the soul contemplates</note> and ideas are to be seen. Therein the Divine powers of that world are always present and set before the children of wisdom at all times—those whose eyes Christ has opened to look upon them. Now, in the 13th verse, he says in a negative way where She is *not* to be found: Man (he says) knows not the price thereof; that is, the **Natural** and **Rational man** does not know the high price and value of **Spiritual Wisdom**. Neither is She found in the land of the living. To be sure, it is not impossible for the natural man to reach a knowledge of natural things, or even of spiritual things in a natural way—and indeed he does reach it. In this "land of the living," one may undoubtedly attain natural knowledge of both natural and spiritual things or objects by attending universities and through the common aid of study and diligence. But this *other* wisdom is solely the gift of God, of which Job speaks here; namely, that

<column-break/>

<page-num>115</page-num>

<sig>P 4</sig>

She may not be attained through any human study, diligence, or labor, nor can She be reached through any art or natural subtlety. Rather, the **inward man** alone is capable of Her, and is admitted to Her through the free and immediate grace of God, and is permitted to see Her high price and precious value. He continues further: The Deep says, She is not in me. The inhabitants of the deeps, or of the abyss, must likewise confess that She is not in them, but is above them, and is certainly not to be reached by them. Just as She cannot be searched out by spirits who are in the flesh, so She cannot be found by those who are outside the flesh. She is too high, so that She may not be discovered or found by the **Human** will, nor even by the **Devilish** will.

And therefore this world, which is compared to a turbulent sea, may certainly say much more: She is not in me; I am not the place where She is to be found. Let then the high schools of learning, the universities of this world, say that in and among them are found the heights, the depths, the lengths, and the breadths of Natural and Theological knowledge. Yes, even if they could bring and extend their knowledge—through their human studies—ten thousand times higher than it is currently found in any man: what is it all when compared with **Spiritual Knowledge** and **Wisdom**, which comes down from above, and is taught and learned only at the **Heavenly University**, in the **School of the Holy Spirit**? Of this kind of knowledge he speaks further in the 14th verse: She cannot be gotten for gold, nor can She be attained for all that man can give or do for Her. She is not for sale for money or for labor; the gold of Ophir <note>A biblical region famous for its wealth and high-quality gold.</note> is not to be valued equal to Her, etc. Upon this he then concludes in the 20th verse, saying: Whence then cometh wisdom? But of this Divine and Spiritual life, we wish to treat in the following chapter.

### Chapter VI.
#### On the Life of Infusion.

It is a Divine and Spiritual life which the inward man obtains from God through infusion <note>original: *Eingiessung*; the theological concept of grace or knowledge being "poured" directly into the heart by the Holy Spirit.</note> and inspiration; and therefore I say once more, that the life which flows from these Divine powers is more to be desired and far to be preferred to that other. Here some might perhaps expect that we—at the mere thought or mention of this life of infusion—should break out into an angry storming and grim outcry against the universities of this world, as well as against the languages, arts, and sciences, and in a word, against all human scholarship, as many are accustomed to do whose zeal runs toward ignorance. But God forbid that we should so thoughtlessly reject a thing so hearty in itself (as they undoubtedly are)! Much less can we side with those who wish to see them completely rooted out. But on the other hand, may God also preserve us from standing and fighting for them in such a way that we recognize or approve of nothing else,

<column-break/>

<page-num>119</page-num>
<header>Inner Birth and Incarnation of JESUS CHRIST.</header>
<meta>catchword: the</meta>

nor that we should recognize a spirit of revelation and infusion from above accompanying them! For through this, the knowledge of both natural and spiritual things is attained in a spiritual manner. And it is so far from the truth that this [spirit] should exclude all human studies, that it rather unites itself with them: so that to deny it to them would be a most uncharitable act, not only in regard to the students and graduates at the universities, but also to the Divine Goodness itself, both in the Father and the Son. And the reason is, because such a thing is not some special favor to be granted or given only to a select number of persons; but because it is the common and undisputed right of each and every true believer: so much so, that whoever does not have it is, for that very reason, not truly a Christian, regardless of his visible membership and outward relationship with the Church, even if he has been incorporated into it through baptism itself. Compare Matthew 11:25 with Romans 8:9 <note>Matthew 11:25: "I thank thee, O Father... because thou hast hid these things from the wise and prudent, and hast revealed them unto babes." Romans 8:9: "Now if any man have not the Spirit of Christ, he is none of his."</note>. And so much for this Divine and Spiritual life, the inward man of every true Christian believer.

Regarding the life itself, it consists in spiritual convictions, enlightenments, gifts, and graces. It consists in the work of the Father and the Son. The work of the Father consists in the Spirit who convicts of sin; in the Spirit who makes one sorrowful over it, and pours in humility upon humility, while also pointing out and demonstrating the insufficiency of our own righteousness for our salvation. And the work of the Son through infusion consists in the infusing enlightenments of the Father’s free love, rich grace, and free mercy in Christ toward lost sinners; His free remission and forgiveness of sins in Christ; His free offerings of <term>Justification</term> <note>The act of being declared righteous before God through faith.</note> and <term>Redemption</term> <note>The deliverance from sin and its consequences through the sacrifice of Christ.</note> in Christ and for His sake. It further consists in the infusing enlightenments of JESUS CHRIST, in the merits of His Heavenly blood and the all-sufficiency of His death and sufferings...

<summary>This page discusses the "Life of Infusion," arguing that divine inspiration is superior to academic learning but should work in harmony with it. The author asserts that this spiritual enlightenment is a necessary mark of a true Christian and involves the combined work of God the Father and the Son.</summary>

<page-num>120</page-num>
<header>The Second Little Treatise on the</header>
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

...to eternal life and salvation: These latter inspirations <note>original: *Eingeistungen*; a term describing the Holy Spirit breathing or pouring life into the soul.</note> of the Son belong to the Gospel, just as the preceding ones of the Father belong to the Law. The life of inspiration further consists in spiritual gifts, such as the gifts of prayer and clear speech to warn, to exhort, to rebuke, and to comfort, so that they make one capable and fit to express oneself skillfully, fully, and freely according to whatever new matter is provided or inspired. Furthermore, the life of inspiration also consists in such inspired graces: such as inspired faith, inspired regret, sorrow, and penance, and an obedience inspired by Christ, and many other such Divine graces besides, both from God the Father and from His Son Christ; all of which, in a word, are in and of themselves true, substantial, and independent things <note>original: *wahre wesentliche und selbständige Dinge*</note>, and not mere names, images, and fantasies; for otherwise they could not come from God, because they would not be worthy of Him. And although it is true that the Scripture sometimes speaks of a false faith, and also of feigned, hypocritical penance, etc., these are nevertheless not inspired. If they are therefore true, substantial, and independent, it is clear that they cannot be attributed to the Devil. I confess, indeed, that he may transform himself into an Angel of Light; but even then it is only in appearance. For this reason, the Scripture explicitly labels those who (like him) appear under a deceptive appearance, pretending to be Christians and claiming feigned graces, with the name **Hypocrites**; whereas it calls those who are clothed with true graces **Believing Christians** and **Saints**, which are names never attributed to others.

## Chapter VII.
### On the Degrees of Infusion.

It truly appears clearly at this time that the Divine and Spiritual life of inspiration—which flows from the powers of the world to come, and which we have treated until now—is both highly to be desired and never to be esteemed or valued enough above all others, whatever names they may have. But here we must now, according to the Apostle’s counsel in **1 Corinthians 2:13**, compare spiritual things with spiritual <note>The author is referring to the practice of "discernment," using Scripture to weigh different types of spiritual experiences against one another.</note>: namely, inspirations with inspirations, instructions with instructions, convictions with convictions, enlightenments with enlightenments, gifts with gifts, and graces with graces. For there are degrees and steps in all of them, which must therefore be carefully distinguished. For some of the inspirations are weak and low; just as others, by contrast, are high and strong; some are imperceptible, others undeniable; some belong to the Holy Spirit under the work of the Law alone; but others belong under the Gospel; some belong to the Holy Spirit under the workings of the Father, others under the workings of the Son; and others, finally, under the proper workings of the Holy Spirit Himself. So that, to say it here in passing, all the Divine teachings or instructions, inspirations, and enlightenments, of whatever degree or kind they may be, are all testified to be under the Holy Spirit as their producer.

I will begin first of all with the very weakest and lowest, and these are the imperceptible inspirations of the Holy Spirit, which the inward man has when he is filled with spiritual convictions of sin and grace; when he has a true work of the Law and Gospel within himself, and thus has Christ Himself, as a seed, within him; yet for that time knows nothing of it, so much so that it is a great trial <note>original: *Anfechtung*; a term for a spiritual crisis or a period of intense doubt and testing.</note> to him...

is that they are so imperceptible. They remember neither the time, the place or location, nor the instrument of their conversion; rather, the Word, the text, the sermon, the day, and the servant who convinced them have entirely slipped their minds. Nevertheless, they find that they are being worked upon in secret in a wonderful and rare manner; they themselves do not know how it is with them, nor how they should believe, do penance, and be obedient. This one thing they do know: whereas they were previously blind, they now see the nature, loathsomeness, and hideous power and overflow of sin in the mirror of the Law; and, on the other hand, they also recognize the nature of God's free love, grace, and mercy in the mirror of the Gospel—to forgive lost sinners their sins in and for the sake of His Son. And so it happens that they are truly converted and live accordingly.

Beside these inspirations <note>original: *Eingeistungen*; refers to the "breathing in" of the Spirit.</note>, however, there are still others, as I have said, which are both perceptible and also irresistible. And those who have such are distinguished from the preceding ones in that they not only actually experience and feel them in themselves according to every circumstance, as the others do; but they can furthermore also demonstrate and give a rational cause for the whole work, and for the irresistible manner and way of the same, from one degree to the next, from the first to the last.

Now, concerning this difference between the imperceptible and irresistible inspirations, let us speak a word or two by way of warning to two kinds of persons. Namely, 1. To those among our contemporary theologians who wish to restrict and bind the **Holy Spirit** solely to such inspirations, convictions, etc., which are perceptible and irresistible. These must therefore know that the keys to this single school—namely the <term>Pentecost-school</term> <note>The "school" where the Holy Spirit teaches the believer directly, as at Pentecost.</note>—hang only at the side of Our High Teacher and Headmaster, who alone has the privilege to admit or exclude whomever He will. Who are you, then, that you presume to judge the manner and way of the **Holy Spirit**

to teach out of your own shallow understanding? And that you dare, contrary to the doctrine of **Holy Scripture**, to exclude His beginning saints from this school: and thus, by that very act, to nullify His <term>inspirations</term> (because they are imperceptible). But secondly, let those on the other side, who are made partakers of the more perceptible and irresistible workings of the **Holy Spirit**, also take heed that they do not exalt themselves in spiritual pride over their lowlier brothers who do not yet recognize these things, and perhaps despise them for that reason. For the **Holy Spirit** has no need for anyone to teach Him whom or how He should teach. So very absurd and foolish is the practice of those who wish to judge the life and standing of all others. Such a person is a pure formalist, or one who hangs merely on the outward form and has never had the work of God itself within him, etc.

I call those "low and weak" which are attributed to the Father in the work of the Law; such are inspired convictions, <unclear>contritions</unclear> <note>OCR: *Zerschmischungen*; likely *Zermirschungen*, meaning the crushing of the heart or deep remorse.</note>, and humiliations. By convictions inspired by the **Holy Spirit**, I mean those where a certain innate, brightly shining light from Him shines upon the inward man with the letter of the Law, thus opening the letter of the same, and presenting to the soul the nature of sin, as well as its shamefulness, power or greatness, impurity, and defilement, together with the guilt and due punishment.

The properties of this light are as follows, namely: 1. It is a clear, bright-shining light, and is to be seen only through its own light. 2. It proceeds from the Holy Spirit under the work of the Father <note>The author uses a traditional theological framework where the "Work of the Father" corresponds to the Law and the conviction of sin.</note>. 3. It is internal and inborn and shines into the mind of the inward man. 4. It shines through the letter of the Law and accompanies the Word itself. 5. It is an irresistible light. 6. It is also a convincing light that answers all objections and causes all deliberations of reason to become mute and silent; indeed, it even forces the mind to fall down and confess that the Holy Spirit is truly within it. The mind has nothing to say against it but confesses that it is righteous and good. No cloaking, covering, nor excuses can refute it there; rather, the soul in which it appears is immediately forced to confess: "I myself am the hindrance, my mouth is stopped, and I have become guilty before God." And upon this follows immediate repentance and deep sadness over sins previously committed and done; for the person is gnawed or pricked in the heart, so that they unceasingly cry out: "Oh, what shall I do that I may be saved?" Thereupon, shudders, terrors, and servile fear <note>original: *Knechtische Furcht*; refers to a fear of punishment like that of a slave, rather than the loving "filial fear" of a child.</note> rush into them in heaps and take possession of their conscience.

After this follows an in-spirited <note>original: *eingegeistete*; literally "breathed-in" or "inspired" by the Spirit.</note> humiliation of this same Holy Spirit from the work of the Father in the Law, and indeed in this manner: for after the soul has come into this state, it immediately turns away from its sins, thinks, and also resolves to leave its former evil life and in the future to observe the duties and obligations incumbent upon it—such as to pray, and to hear the Word, and in short to lead a life that is perfect and blameless, in the hope of finding relief and peace for the conscience through these means. But then, a new radiance of light falls into the mind in and with the letter of the Word, through which the person is convinced that their own righteousness and

<column-break/>

<page-num>125</page-num>

<sig>I</sig>

personal doing and working is not enough—indeed, it is much more impossible—that they should ever be able to satisfy the Father’s Divine Justice for the sins already committed against Him. And this light is also so strong that it overcomes all the powers of the soul, whereupon the soul gives itself up as conquered, submits, falls down, and confesses. Here the Holy Spirit also unlocks the meaning of this and other similar scriptural passages, such as **Galatians 2:16**: *We know that a man is not justified by the works of the law; for by the works of the law shall no flesh be justified.* This I call a "light of sight," or a revelation in and with the letter of the Word, which gives the soul to see clearly that its own works, its own obedience and righteousness, are altogether fruitless and insufficient. And thus also **Galatians 3:10, 11, 12**: *For as many as are of the works of the law are under the curse: for it is written: Cursed is every one who does not continue and remain in all things which are written in the book of the law, to do them. But that no man is justified in the sight of God by the law is evident: for the just shall live by faith. Now the law is not of faith, but the man who does them shall live in them.* This is another in-spirited humiliation, or Divine opening, in and with the letter of the Law or the written Word. All these and similar convictions of sin and of our own righteousness (as they all belong to the operation of the Holy Spirit under the operation of the Father in the work of the Law), although they are true, essential, and indisputable, they are nevertheless low and weak when compared with those that come from the Divine powers of the Eternal World.

<meta>catchword: truly</meta>

pel to speak, which we may call **Evangelical Convictions**. The light here is indeed the very same light and has the very same or identical properties as that under the working of the Father in and with the Law, and it proceeds from the very same Holy Spirit. Despite this, however, there is a difference within it, and such is to be seen in these following circumstances. The preceding light proceeds from the Holy Spirit under the working of the Father; this one, however, proceeds under the Son. For neither the Father nor the Son ever works without the Holy Spirit. Again, the preceding light goes out in, with, and through the letter of the Law; but this one comes from the revelation of the Son in, with, by, and through the letter of the Gospel. The object <note>original: *Vorwurff*; in this period, it refers to the "object" or "subject matter" being presented to the mind.</note> of the former is the Law, but that of the latter is the Gospel. The one, in turn, has sin as its object—and with it, at the same time, its power or greatness, shamefulness, and defilement—and what is accustomed to follow it, such as guilt, the curse, eternal death, wrath, and damnation, which are its due. The other has as its object the riches of free grace, the free love and mercy of the Father, together with the beauty and glory of grace itself: whose brilliance, when it opens itself in and through the veil of the letter, surpasses the sun in its power, because it is without any spot or blemish. So, from the Law, the one may be called a **Legal Light**; the other, an **Evangelical Light**, named after its object, the Gospel.

### Chapter VIII.
->Concerning the Seal of Acquittal or Deliverance and Justification through the Holy Spirit.<-

Here I will now also reveal what the inspirations or illuminations of the Holy Spirit are under the working of God the Father in, with, and through the letter of the Gospel. After the preceding work, whereby the soul is brought to where it truly 

<column-break/>

<page-num>127</page-num>
<header>Inward Prayer and Incarnation of JESUS Christ</header>
<sig>I 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: depths</meta>

truly feels and perceives its state of sin, and has also become hopeless and helpless in itself; and, like a prisoner, lies enclosed within itself under the watch of the eternal wrath of God (which is due for its past as well as present sins), bound as if with chains and shackles of servile fear <note>original: *sclavischer Furcht*; a fear motivated by the dread of punishment rather than love.</note>, as if hell and eternal damnation should be its portion forever and ever: the Holy Spirit is then accustomed to radiate into the mind of the lost sinner a clear, bright-shining, convincing light of the nature of the Father, who feels himself thus eternally lost; and this opens to him again a door of hope, and a possibility of forgiveness from the pardoning grace and mercy of the Father. This light, however, is commonly also accompanied in and with some letters of the Word. For the letter and the Spirit go together: the letter of the Gospel is the proper carriage upon which the Holy Spirit rides. So it is that these truths now shine clearly to the mind in their depth and full sense, of which the mind previously had hardly any concept. Therefore, no one should set aside and despise Holy Scripture under the appearance that it is only a "dead letter"; for the Holy Spirit can make it living—indeed, for whom He will, and when and how it pleases Him. As for example, if the LORD should cry out to a troubled mind that is tempted and distressed because it cannot grasp that its sins are forgiven and that it is acceptable before Him, as He did to Moses in Exodus 34:6, 7: That He, the LORD God, is merciful, gracious, long-suffering, and of abundant goodness and truth, keeping mercy for thousands, and forgiving iniquity, transgression, and sin. Or if the divine light of the Spirit should address him in the similar passage of Micah 7:18, 19: *Who is a God like unto thee, that pardoneth iniquity, and passeth by the transgression? He retaineth not his anger for ever, because he delighteth in mercy &c. And thou wilt cast all their sins into the depths*

Or with this, from Isaiah 43:25: *I, even I, am He who blots out your transgressions for my own sake, and will not remember your sins.* Or Chapter 55:7: *Let the wicked forsake his way, and return to the LORD, and He will have mercy on him; and to our God, for He will abundantly pardon.* Or Jeremiah 31:3: *I have loved thee with an everlasting love, therefore with loving-kindness have I drawn thee to me.* Or John 3:16: *God (the Father) so loved the world, that He gave His only begotten Son for it.* We cannot go beyond this first, original, and eternal Love. When, I say, the light of the Holy Spirit shines into the dark mind of the comfortless person through one of these or similar scriptural passages, he instantly grasps the forgiving nature, grace, and mercy of the Father as his object <note>original: *Vorwurff*; in this context, it refers to the spiritual "object" or "aim" presented to the mind’s eye.</note>, since it is the Father's prerogative to forgive sin. And not only that, but he further grasps the generosity, richness, and all-sufficiency of that same forgiving love, grace, and mercy. He also clearly understands the free and heartfelt offers of the same toward all those who hold fast to it, believe, and place their reliance and trust in it, and find rest therein. Thus, the poor soul is drawn, as if by ropes, to believe in the Father and to throw itself into the arms of His infinite mercy. The beauty, the radiance, and the glory of God's 

<column-break/>

<page-num>129</page-num>
<header>Interior Prayer and the Incarnation of Jesus Christ.</header>
<sig>I 3</sig>

love, free grace, and mercy are, in these spiritual convictions, such an astonishing brilliance to the sight that the light of the sun, in its full power and glory, is not half so unbearable to the mortal eye of the body as this light is when it shines with an unveiled face through the "cloud of the letter" <note>The "cloud of the letter" refers to the literal text of the Bible, which the author believes can only be truly understood when illuminated by the Holy Spirit.</note> into the soul.

Regarding the nature of this acquittal from sin—in the forgiveness thereof, together with the guilt, shivers, and terrors of the conscience—we may imagine it happening in this way: namely, in the manner of a public proclamation, as if He were to say with the words of Isaiah 43:25: *I, yes I, am He who blots out your transgression for my own sake: and will no longer remember your sins.* I, God the Father, am He who can and will blot out your transgressions; the prerogative is mine to release and to forgive; I forgive freely and for nothing; who then should dare to undertake to condemn? And I will do it because I will, and that solely for my own sake. I am the same, the Father of Mercies, etc. 

For because God the Father is the First Person in the Holy Trinity, He is now the great and just Judge. The eye of the anxious conscience perceives Him to be so, and the guilty sinner feels himself to be the prisoner standing before the judgment seat of this great and strict Judge. There, his mouth is stopped so that he dare not open it; his sins stand (like an army) before him, and his conscience accuses him like a thousand witnesses and condemns him. While he thus expects the final sentence from the mouth of God the Father—the strict Judge—at any moment; He, yes even He, contrary to all hope and expectation of the prisoner, cries out from His own tender and merciful nature into the conscience of the poor, death-deserving sinner who confesses his guilt: "I grant and forgive you all your past and present sins; I release you from all of them; I will remember them no more, just as if they had never been committed." ...afterwards I acquit you of all guilt thereof / in the courts both in heaven / and in your conscience / freely and for nothing. All the angels in heaven shall be witnesses to this my acquittal and discharge—and all the devils in hell shall take note of it! From which voice of the Father / who makes this announcement of forgiveness in the conscience of the troubled sinner / such a Divine power enters at the same time / that it takes away the guilt; the tremors, anxiety, and terrors of the conscience flee / and thus makes all slavish and hellish fear cease. And besides this, at the same time as this announcement, the power of the Father's merciful and tender nature itself penetrates into the anxious and troubled conscience, which awakens within it inner peace and joy to rest in the forgiving grace and mercy of the Father / and to place its trust therein. So that you see clearly / that this release or acquittal from sin is not an empty sound or voice / but a loudly resounding announcement / which resounds with great power through all worlds / heaven / hell and through the guilty conscience itself.

But here one will undoubtedly ask / whether this forgiveness is not general / and extends so far / that it covers future sins as well as past and present ones? To which I answer / that God the Father / as a righteous judge / forgives all past and present sins / which have already actually aroused the wrath of God: these alone are the objects <note>original: *Vorwürffe*; in this context, it refers to the specific matters or "targets" presented to God's mercy.</note> of God’s present forgiving grace and mercy. But as for the sins not yet committed / such are not yet / but are left to be the objects of Divine mercy after the sinners have felt repentance and sorrow over the sins committed / are contrite and humbled in their hearts / and have implored and prayed for New Forgiveness. Then God the Father will be so righteous as to forgive and grant that which was done wrong...

---

<page-num>131</page-num>
<header>inner Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ.</header>
<sig>J 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: the</meta>

...will be found to have been committed. But He waits then / for one to call upon and ask Him for such forgiveness: He first wants / that one should believe in Him / and first confidently trust and rely on His grace / before the conscience reaches the point of feeling its power. For so we read 1 John 1 v 9: *If we confess our sins / He is faithful and just to forgive us our sins* / so that future sins / in a word / are the objects of still future grace.

Against this you will perhaps object and say / that / if it be the Father’s property to forgive sinners their sins so freely / after they have confessed them / embraced faith and prayed for their forgiveness / then there would have been no further need for a <term>Mediator: an intermediary between two parties, here referring to Jesus Christ between God and humanity</term>. The consequence or conclusion of this objection may be summarized in this one short question: To what end did the Savior of the world humble Himself to be born into the same / to live and to die / to what end did He then suffer / what was the price of His blood / which He paid for it / and why was it necessary / that through Him a <term>satisfaction: the theological idea that Christ's suffering paid the debt for human sin</term> must still occur? Answer:

1. That this is so / namely that the Father has this prerogative to forgive sin out of His own free mercy / good will and pleasure / because He in and of Himself is a merciful and gracious God / is indeed clear and manifest from various undeniable scriptural passages already cited.

2. But nevertheless, it can never follow from this / that it was therefore not necessary / for Christ to come in the flesh and make <term>reconciliation: the restoration of a broken relationship</term> for the sins of the world. For how / regardless of this acquittal and forgiveness of sins through the hands of the Father / could the soul / which had once become unclean / because it is stained with sin / ever enter into heaven / and be admitted into the New Jerusalem? because the Scripture testifies so clearly / that nothing unclean shall enter into that city / and without <term>sanctification: the process of being made holy or pure</term> no one can see God. Therefore it is an unavoidable necessity / that he who is to be <term>born again: the spiritual rebirth required for salvation</term>...

<summary>This passage explains that while God the Father has the sovereign power to forgive sins and calm the conscience, this forgiveness is applied to past and present sins upon repentance. It then addresses why Jesus Christ's incarnation and sacrifice were still necessary: because forgiveness alone does not remove the impurity of sin, which requires Christ's work for sanctification and entry into heaven.</summary>
<keywords>absolution, conscience, repentance, divine mercy, Mediator, satisfaction, reconciliation, sanctification, New Jerusalem, spiritual rebirth</keywords>

--- Page 69 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of divine forgiveness. The previous page established that God the Father’s forgiveness is an authoritative announcement or decree that relieves the conscience of guilt and terror, acting as a precursor to the transformative work of Christ.</meta>

<page-num>132</page-num>
<header>The Second Little Treatise on the</header>
<meta>catchword: Father's</meta>

...must / who wishes to enter into the Kingdom of God. For which reason it is necessary enough / that Christ wash and purify us / and make a clean vessel out of an unclean soul / so that he may prepare and suit it for Heaven.

3. That the **Royal Prerogative of God the Father** <note>The "Royal Prerogative" (Königliche Vorrecht) refers to the absolute, sovereign right of a monarch—in this case, God—to grant a pardon that no one else can grant or overturn.</note> to forgive sins can never be mixed with that very same prerogative of God the Son. For Holy Scripture actually attributes the forgiveness of sins to Christ himself in the same measure / and links it with the purchase made by his blood. We see it thus in Ephesians 1 v 7: In whom we have redemption through his blood / the forgiveness of sins / according to the riches of His grace. And Colossians 1 v 14: In whom we have redemption through His blood / namely the forgiveness of sins. Romans 3 v 25: Whom God has set forth to be a throne of grace <note>original: *Gnaden-Throne*; often translated in English Bibles as "propitiation" or "mercy seat."</note> through faith in His blood / that He might show His righteousness through the remission of sins that are past. And again, Hebrews 9 v 22: And without shedding of blood there is no forgiveness. Indeed / Christ Himself teaches us as much in Matthew 26 v 28: This is my blood of the New Testament / which is shed for many for the forgiveness of sins. Therefore, to reconcile these scriptural passages with the preceding ones, you must know / that there are different ways in which sin is forgiven: For example, one is among the works of the Father / another among the works of the Son / and yet another among the work of the Holy Spirit. And these can never be contrary to one another / much less withstand, cancel, or destroy one another. The Father’s release and forgiveness goes before and makes preparation for the Son’s. The Father’s forgiveness is after the manner of a proclamation / as we have explained before. The Father, in turn, forgives freely and for nothing out of His own tender / merciful and compassionate nature / without regard for any moving cause outside of Himself / but His own pure good will or pleasure and Royal Prerogative to do so. Finally, the Father’s...

<column-break/>

<page-num>133</page-num>
<header>inner Birth and Humanity of JESUS CHRIST.</header>
<sig>I 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: Re-</meta>

...merciful forgiveness stands in the justification of the sinner / while he still stands in his sins / and as if in his ungodly nature / before his soul is purified from its uncleanness and defilement through the blood of Jesus. For the verification of this truth I must refer you to Ezekiel 16 v 4 to 9 / from which it is evident / that the time of the Father's forgiveness is while the soul still lies in blood / that is / in the uncleanness and defilement of sin. See also on this Isaiah 43 v 22 to 26. Compared with Romans 4 v 5.

In the meantime, however, this does not in the least cancel the manner of the Son's forgiveness; as Christ Himself testifies to us in Matthew 9 v 2, 6, where it is written: Jesus said to the sick man; be of good cheer, son / your sins are forgiven you. But that you may know / that the Son of Man has power on earth to forgive sins / and if on earth / how much more now in heaven. Now, the Son’s forgiveness of sins is distinguished from the Father’s forgiveness in these particular circumstances: 1. The forgiveness of the Son follows the forgiveness of the Father. 2. It is also a consequence or fruit of the blood of Christ, first poured into the heart and conscience / and even into all the properties and forms of the soul. 3. It is under the Son’s peculiar <note>In this context, "peculiar" means "specific" or "proper to him alone."</note> operation just as the Father’s forgiveness is also under His. 4. It becomes free after the washing and purification of the soul through the blood of Christ from all uncleanness both of the flesh and of the spirit; as these scriptural passages clearly testify in 1 John 1 v 7, 9: And the blood of Jesus Christ / His Son / cleanses us from all sin. See also Romans 1 v 5 and compare it with Hebrews 9 v 13, 14. As well as with Revelation 7 v 14. And no less Jeremiah 33 v 8. And further also Matthew 26 v 28 / as we have cited before: This is my blood of the New Testament / which is shed for many / for the forgiveness of sins. From this last text it is clear / 1. That the blood of Jesus Christ performed a preceding service for the forgiveness of sins. 2. That it is shed / and indeed not only once upon the Cross / but is still shed daily in the hearts of all true believers. See further Luke 24 v 47 / and Acts 5 v 3.

<note>Left incomplete by the author.</note>

<summary>This page distinguishes between the Father's sovereign decree of forgiveness, which justifies the sinner even in their "uncleanness," and the Son's forgiveness, which is a transformative purification through His blood. The author argues that these two acts of the Trinity are distinct but harmonious steps in the process of salvation.</summary>
<keywords>justification, forgiveness of sins, blood of Christ, Trinity, Royal Prerogative, purification, New Testament, mercy seat</keywords>

## Since we have the same spirit of faith, etc.

<summary>This page concludes the index for the second treatise, focusing on the nature of the Father's forgiveness and the "magnetic pull" of the divine, and introduces the title page for John Pordage's 1676 work on the "Spirit of Faith."</summary>
<keywords>John Pordage, index, forgiveness, transfiguration, mystical Christianity, historical Christianity, rebirth, redemption, Spirit of Faith, 1676</keywords>

--- Page 74 ---
<meta>This page marks the transition from the index of the second treatise to the beginning of a new work by Johann Pordage. The previous page (140) concluded the alphabetical index for his earlier writing. This page serves as a personal introduction and the opening chapter of his "Spirit of Faith."</meta>

<header>Introduction.</header>
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

As I was in my eternal spirit in a special conversation with Jesus in the spirit, He convinced me not only that I lacked faith, but also that faith is excellent and necessary. He showed me clearly that I lacked this Spirit of Faith: and that without it, it would be impossible to please God.

And I also saw that it was so; and that it would indeed be impossible to live the life of faith unless one has this Spirit of Faith: also that I truly still lacked this Spirit, and that despite all my applied effort and labor, I had not yet attained it.

But Jesus told me it was in His power to give me this Spirit (or grace) of Faith: And just as it was in His power to give it to me—provided I would earnestly petition Him for it—so He would not fail to give it: For this Spirit of Faith is the gift of Christ, which He gave to His disciples.

At this point, He revealed to me what the Spirit of Faith was; what the nature of this Spirit was with its fruits and effects; what its excellence was; indeed, what the necessity of the Spirit of Faith was.

And this drove me to search in the Holy Scripture and to find what was recorded therein concerning the Spirit of Faith. And this same thing moved me to write the following little treatise on the Spirit of Faith; because it is such an entirely necessary and excellent gift to know.

->Johann Pordage.<-
<note>The original German spelling "Pordädsch" reflects the phonetic spelling of the English author's name, Johann Pordage (1607–1681), a famous Anglican priest and mystic.</note>

# The Spirit of Faith:

## A Little Treatise
->Concerning the Degrees and Kinds of Faith.<-

### Chapter I.
**Concerning true faith and its various names and properties.**

<margin>1</margin>
<image-desc>Ornamental initial letter D decorated with floral scrollwork.</image-desc>The subject or theme <note>original: *Vorwurff*, an archaic German term for a subject of discussion or "object" of focus</note> which I have been moved to write about here is faith. By which I understand only true faith: namely, that which the Holy Scripture otherwise calls "unfeigned faith," as in 1 Timothy 1 verse 5. In that place, true faith is set in opposition to the false; the unfeigned to the hypocritical; and the living to that which is dead, which James makes mention of in Chapter 2 verse 20. I shall not, however, deal with all these kinds here; but only with true and living faith, with unadulterated, upright, and unhypocritical faith, through which we must be saved.

<margin>2</margin>
2. This is and is called the faith of God's elect, because it is given to the called and the elect; just as one may read in Titus 1 verse 1: *According to the faith of God's elect.* <note>original Latin reference: *Tit. 1 v 1*</note> It is not given to everyone, but only to some, as is seen, who are God's chosen and elect ones, who receive faith through the denial of themselves; and faith chooses them, in that it calls them out of the world and separates them from it. It is given to children, adults, the strong or

But how few (alas!) are those today who can claim for themselves that same precious faith that was in them. Is the Apostolic faith given to everyone today, or is it common to all? Have all received it, or have all submitted themselves to it—to be chosen by it, and to be set apart and sanctified for God in holiness and truth? By this, I mean the great general multitude of Christians, and not those few holy souls who are still found in the midst of this general and destructive apostasy. 3. This true, healthy, and active-working faith is called by various names in the Holy Scripture. Sometimes it is, for example, called simply "Faith." In this way: "The testing of your faith produces patience." And again in 1 Peter 1:5: "Who are kept by the power of God through faith unto salvation." 7. It is likewise also called the Grace of Faith, as it is a fruit of the Divine Spirit in us, worked upon our soul or inward man through the operation of God. It is worked in us through a threefold revelation of the Spirit of God: according to which it can be said that we believe either in the name of the Father, or in the name of the Son, or finally in the name of the Holy Spirit. It proceeds from this Triune Light, Power, and Name from one degree to another: and is thus in us, that is,

in our New Earth and Heavens—I mean in our hearts and spirits—*formed*. *Galatians 5:22:* But the fruit of the Spirit is faith; <note>The Greek term *pistis* is translated here as Faith, though many modern English Bibles use "faithfulness."</note> that is, the grace of faith is the fruit of the Spirit of God.

8. According to this, the distinction between the Spirit of God and the grace of faith is like that between the cause and the *effect*. The Spirit of God *generates* the grace of faith in us: and this grace is *generated* in us as a fruit of the Spirit of God in us. Therefore, the Spirit of God and the spirit or grace of faith must remain distinct in themselves and not be mixed with one another.

9. And so, in the same way, the spirit of faith and the grace of faith must also be kept distinct. For the spirit of faith *generates*, through its operation in us, the grace of faith; which is a fruit that proceeds from the same, because it is the birth and the child of the same. Thus, the spirit and the grace of faith are as distinct as the father and the child, or as the tree and the fruit that grows out from it. So it is properly the spirit of faith, as the *generator* <note>original: "Generator"; the one who produces or begets</note> and father of the same, which makes an unbeliever become a believer, and which creates the grace of faith where previously there was none.

10. # The Third Little Treatise on the
<meta>catchword: in</meta>

...neither is there, nor can there be, any opposition or contradiction; rather, these two spirits—namely, God’s Spirit and the Spirit of Faith—stand in **subordination** to one another in this act. The Spirit of God is the primary and most noble Worker, and the Spirit of Faith is the primary and most noble instrumental cause <note>original: "werckzeugliche Ursache"</note>: it acts as an agent in the hand of the Spirit of God. And thus, in all acts, these two are co-workers with one another. God’s Spirit does not wish to work without the Spirit of Faith; and the Spirit of Faith cannot work without God’s Spirit. They work in unity and union with each other, and in and through each other, in an inseparable manner: one is never without the other, and where one is, there the other must also be. And in this way it becomes clear that, because the Spirit of Faith is always subordinate to the Spirit of God and thereby united with it, there is no contrariety at all when one attributes everything that God’s Spirit does to the movement or operation of Faith—and conversely, what the Spirit of Faith does, to the Spirit of God. This is because a **communion of properties** <note>original: "Gemeinschaft der Eigenschafften"; a theological term (communicatio idiomatum) suggesting that when two things are perfectly united, the characteristics of one can be attributed to the other</note> arises from this, their exact unity and union.

11. Finally, there is also another name by which Faith is signified or called, and that is: **the gift of God**: concerning which, see **Ephesians 2:8**. This Faith, this Spirit of Faith, this Grace of Faith, are words that all signify the same one thing, which is the pure and free gift of Almighty God bestowed upon man. God is the sole author and giver of it, and His Spirit is the worker of it within us. Therefore, whoever has this Spirit of Faith within them—united and "harvested" <note>original: "eingeerntet"</note> together with their own Eternal Spirit—possesses a good gift, a perfect gift, a precious and dear talent. And because it is given from above through infusion and bestowal, and comes down from the **Father of Lights** (according to **James 1:17**), it is said that this spirit and life of faith is given to our will-spirits by God as an act of His free grace. Regarding this, see again **Ephesians 2**.
