...would be altogether unaccountable, if there were not something in our upper and eternal spirits that could agree or disagree, accept or reject and cast off what is offered to them.

14. Therefore one must firmly establish that the creature, for its part, may fail or succeed in believing or not believing, agreeing or disagreeing, or turning toward or away from an object <note>original: "Vorwurffe"; here meaning the "object of focus" or "subject matter" presented to the mind</note>. For these are solely the effects of our own spirits; they arise from our internal and essential instrumental powers <note>original: "werckzeuglichen Vermögen"</note> which are innate to our immortal spirits. The Grace of Faith, or the Spirit of Faith, however, is a spirit distinct from these effects of our spirits. It is the grace or the spirit through which our spirits become capable, in a life-giving way, of accepting everything that is revealed by God, of giving Him assent, and of willing everything He wants from them or requires them to believe. Now that I have spoken of the nature of faith in general, as well as its various distinguishing names and properties, I will now proceed to consider its specific types and most common distinctions.

### Chapter II.

#### On the Types of Faith: and specifically First, of Historical Faith.

1. The usual or common division of faith—namely, of true faith—is that it is divided into historical, temporary, miraculous, and active or saving <note>original: "Seeligmachenden"; literally "soul-blessing" or "making-blessed"</note> faith. Of each of these in their order: and first, indeed, of historical faith.

2. Historical faith is a true faith in its kind: and it is that which provides preparation and entrance to that faith which is active and saving. The object <note>original: "Objectum, oder der Vorwurff"</note> of this faith is the history of the Holy Scripture with the previously announced prophecies, predictions, prom-

<column-break/>

-ises, and threats contained therein. Furthermore, this historical faith may again be divided into a Legal and an Evangelical [faith].

3. A Legal faith has as its object Mount Sinai and the teaching of Moses. An Evangelical [faith], however, has Mount Zion and the teaching of Christ. A Legal-Historical faith accepts everything written in the Law, in the Prophets, and in the histories of the Old Testament. An Evangelical-Historical faith has as its own object the person of Christ in His birth, life, death, resurrection, ascension, and transfiguration (concerning which we have spoken in a separate Little Treatise), together with the visions and teachings of the Apostles.

4. The right object of true historical faith is thus shown to be a true and divine revelation, such as the Holy Scripture of both the Old and New Testaments is, which is a divine public revelation from God through His Spirit to those who read and hear it. Therefore, it is the nature and property of true historical faith, first and foremost, to believe everything revealed in the written Word—specifically as far as their minds understand the truth of it, and their wills are enlightened to agree with it.

<margin>(d) Philippians 3:15</margin>

5. But this is not yet all or enough, because the object of this type of faith extends even further, even to everything that God, the Revealer of Mysteries, might be pleased at some time or in some way to reveal to them beyond that which is written in the Word (d). Which certainly cannot be contrary to that Word which is written, but must perfectly agree with the same. Therefore, then, all divine visions and revelations given to anyone, all illuminations, in-spiritings <note>original: "Eingeißtungen"; a mystical term for the breathing of the Divine Spirit into the human soul</note>, and divine openings—as well as all experimental knowledge, sensations, and inner-speakings <note>original: "Einsprechungen"</note>—

<summary>Pordage defines the "Spirit of Faith" as a gift that enables human spirits to accept divine revelation, while distinguishing between Legal faith (Old Testament) and Evangelical faith (New Testament). He argues that true historical faith includes not only believing the Bible but also remaining open to ongoing divine inspirations and mystical experiences that align with Scripture.</summary>

## Chapter III.
### On the Inconstant or Only Temporary Faith.

1. Another kind of true faith is the faith that lasts only for a time. This temporary faith is of the very same nature and quality as the preceding one: it is only distinguished from it in the point of perseverance. If this kind of faith persists until it reaches the degree and measure of that faith which is commonly called **Saving Faith**, then there is no difference between the one and the other. For that which is now only Historical may be made active and effective: and that which is as yet inconstant and only temporary may well be made constant and abiding.

<margin>(g) 2 Timothy 4 v 7.</margin>
2. This faith is truly worked in us through and by God: but through the neglect of the means <note>The "means of grace," usually referring to Scripture, prayer, and the sacraments</note>, and through willful disobedience and turning away from the Light, and through a lack of cooperation on the part of man, it may well be entirely and completely lost. As the parable of the Sower in *Matthew 13 v 21* clearly testifies: *They endure only for a time.* According to this, it is undeniable that there are some who believe only for a time; and that not all hold fast to the faith (g) to which they were entrusted, but rather suffer shipwreck of it.

3. Accordingly, that is properly called a "Time-Faith" <note>original: "Zeit-Glaube"; a theological term for a faith that is sincere for a season but lacks deep roots</note>, through which a Christian believes something for a time, but afterwards no longer believes what he previously believed. He truly believes; but it hap-

<meta>catchword: pens</meta>

<column-break/>

pens <note>The catchword "schicht" from the previous column completes the word "geschicht" (happens/occurs)</note> into the Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ. It happens only for a time: He accepts the Word and Revelation of God with joy. But his joy vanishes at the hour of temptation <note>In this context, "temptation" refers to a trial or testing of one's faith</note>, and with it, his faith also vanishes.

4. The Scripture gives us (alas!) more than enough distressing examples of this kind of believer: and daily experience confirms them. One need only hear what the Holy Spirit says in *1 Timothy 1 v 18, 19*: *Fight the good fight, keeping faith and a good conscience, which some have put away and concerning faith have suffered shipwreck.* And again in *1 Timothy 5 v 12*: *Who have their judgment, because they have cast off their first faith.* And *v 15*: *For some have already turned aside after Satan.* These are those who, through their inconstancy and voluntary apostasy <note>falling away</note> from God, have turned their hearts away from Him; and who have fallen away from the Gospel, and, instead of going forward in faith, have gone backward. It is so certain that some of those who have been truly called and have truly believed can still fall away again, and thereby lose the Grace of God irretrievably: according to the warning of *David* to his son in *1 Chronicles 28 v 9*: *And you, my son Solomon, know the God of your father and serve Him with a perfect heart and a willing soul: for the LORD searches all hearts, and understands all the imagination* <term>imagination: here referring to the creative or formative power of the mind and the heart's inclinations</term> *of the thoughts. If you seek Him, you will find Him; but if you forsake Him, He will cast you off forever.*

5. Now the Spirit says clearly in *1 Timothy 4 v 1*, that in the latter times some shall depart from the faith; but how could they depart from that which they never had? And again in *1 Timothy 5 v 8* it is written: *He who does not provide for his own household has denied the faith and is worse than an infidel.* So also in *1 Timothy 6 v 10*: *They have erred from the faith and wandered, or they have been led away from the faith.* All of these pronouncements clearly prove and demonstrate that a faith 

<meta>catchword: exists/</meta>

<summary>This page defines "Temporary Faith" (Zeit-Glaube), explaining that while it is a genuine gift from God, it can be lost through human neglect, disobedience, or failure to endure trials. Pordage uses several New Testament warnings to argue that one can truly possess faith and subsequently fall from grace.</summary>

# The IV. Chapter.
## Of Miraculous Faith.

1. The third kind of true faith is that/ which is called **Miraculous Faith** <note>original: "wunderthätige Glaube"; also known as the "faith of miracles"</note>. Of which it stands written in John 14 v 12: *Truly/ Truly I say to you/ He who believes in Me/ he will also do the works/ that I do/ and will do greater than these/ because I go to the Father.* Christ clearly understands this much by it: those who have hitherto believed in Me/ will do these very miracles/ which they have seen Me do: yes/ even greater ones/ after they shall have received the Holy Spirit from me; which I will obtain for them/ through the ascension to My Father. This is obviously the primary sense and content of these words: but because they are/ as we see/ unlimited/ there is no reason/ why they may not also be understood in a further and more extended sense. And in this way all true believers/ according to the degree and measure of their faith/ have a greater or lesser right or claim to this promise made here.

2. And indeed/ this very miraculous faith itself/ however strange this might initially seem/ is no less an introduction to Living and Saving Faith/ than Historical Faith is. For true Saving Faith <note>original: "Seelig-machende Glaube"; literally the faith that makes one blessed or saved</note> has always worked Inward and Spiritual Wonder-Works; and such upon certain grounded promises: regardless that outward miracles have since the times of the Early Church become exceedingly rare and unusual...

# Chapter V.

## On Saving Faith: and on its three distinct kinds.

1. Now we come finally to treat of that which is properly called Saving Faith <note>The original German "Seelig-machend" literally means "blessed-making" or "salvation-bringing," the standard term for the faith that leads to eternal life.</note>; having prepared the way for it in such a manner: and of which we intend to speak somewhat more fully and circumstantially. Of this there are again three kinds / which / through their three different objects or focuses <note>The original term "Vorwürffe" is an archaic German theological term meaning "objects" or "that which is set before the mind," rather than its modern meaning of "accusation."</note> / namely Father, Son, and Spirit / must be distinguished. For which reason I shall speak.

->I. On Faith in the Name of the Father<-
->II. On Faith in the Name of the Son. And<-
->III. On Faith in the Name of the Holy Spirit.<-

2. To begin then accordingly with the first / which is faith in the Name of the Father / this is a true kind of Saving Faith / which has God / the Father / as its object or focus. Holy Scripture is everywhere quite clear and distinct in regard to this. But so as not to heap up too many texts upon one another / one need only consider and weigh that preeminently glorious passage Hebrews 11 v 6: **He who wants to come to God must believe that He is / and that He is a rewarder of those who seek Him.** <note>original: "Derjenige welcher zu Gott kommen will muß glauben / daß er sey / und daß er ein Vergelter sey derer die Ihn suchen."</note> This is clearly and plainly to be understood as faith in God / the Father: to whom whoever comes to offer any Divine honor / must believe that He is God / and therefore grants a reward to all His faithful servants / who / through faith in His Name / seek and search after Him with diligence. And this is the right foundation of all true Saving Faith itself / namely a faith in the wisdom, goodness, and power of God / the universal Creator and Father; without which it is impossible to do anything that can please God / or obtain His mercy and favor.

5. A third kind of this faith has the **Holy Spirit** as its **object** and **focus**. Of which the Scripture has likewise sufficiently—indeed, abundantly—testified. For proof of this, hear only the words of Christ in <term>John 14:16, 17</term>: *I will pray to the Father, and He will give you another Comforter (and Advocate), that He may remain in and with you eternally; namely, the Spirit of Truth, whom the world cannot receive, for it does not see Him and does not know Him; but you know Him; for He dwells in you and will be in you.* Also verse 26: *After the Comforter has come, whom my Father will send to you, He will testify of me.* Here it is clearly and distinctly testified that faith also has the **Holy Spirit** as its **object**, and that trust in His teaching or instruction and guidance is an effect of true saving faith. Such a commandment, however, is this: *Stay and wait in the city of Jerusalem until you are clothed with power from on high*; which was repeated once more for greater emphasis. But what does the Apostle say of this waiting? Does He not say, *we wait in the Spirit through faith for the hope of righteousness* <term>Galatians 5:5</term>. What then is the **object** or **focus** of faith here? Answer: The hope of righteousness. And, dear reader! What is this hope of righteousness other than the heavenly Jerusalem, which is from above and is the mother of us all; as was said shortly before, namely in <term>Galatians 4:26</term>. Compare this with <term>Revelation 21:2, 3</term>: *And I, John, saw the holy city, the New Jerusalem, coming down from God out of heaven, prepared as a bride adorned for her husband. And thus it is evident from this that the **Holy Spirit** may be no less the **object** of true saving faith than the Father and the Son.

Saving Faith, which must not be mixed with one another. For some of the believers may have the first kind without the second: and others may have both the first and second without the third. The story of *Cornelius* in *Acts 10* <note>original: "Actor. 10"</note> is a clear example. And in this manner, all the saints in the Father's ministry <term>Bedienung: a term for a specific era or dispensation of God's revelation to humanity</term> had faith only in God the Father; for they could not properly have faith in the person of *Christ* as God revealed in the flesh: since He was not yet born into it, or not yet revealed to them therein. Although I do not deny, but rather firmly maintain, that there was in them a kind of internal or unexpressed faith, and an acceptance of all the promises in and from Him. Likewise, the Jews to this very day believe only in the Father; but not in and on *JESUS Christ*, His Son: for His Person has not yet been revealed to them through the Holy Spirit. In the same way, the Turks <note>In the 17th century, "Turks" was a common term for Muslims.</note> also believe in the one only true God, or in God the Father, for their salvation. And they go no further.

7. Now, although these believe only in the name of the Father, it may yet be said truly enough of them that, if they believe in Him, they must also believe in the highly praised Trinity, even though they may be altogether ignorant of the distinctions therein. For it is impossible to believe in the Father without simultaneously believing in the Son and the Spirit, as they are within one another, and one cannot be without the other. Therefore, no one can ever believe in the Father to the exclusion of the Son and Holy Spirit; rather, they must believe in these with and alongside Him, whether they know it or not. In this way, Jews and Turks believe in the one true God, and through this—albeit hiddenly or not explicitly, yet not unclearly—also in the Eternal Unity of the Holy Trinity: however, they lack the other kind of true justifying faith, which is a true and living faith in *Christ's* Person, in His birth and life, in His

8. This faith in the person of *JESUS Christ*, both as the Son of God and as the Son of Man, is the entire purpose and drive of the Apostles' teaching in the New Testament. And the disciples of *JESUS Christ* have at all times—after the revelation of the Gospel and the teaching of the New Covenant were made known to them—come to this true Saving Faith in and on Him. This is what *Christ* Himself taught His disciples: this is what they afterwards preached, publicly proclaimed, and also testified to with their death. For which reason there can be no greater or more dangerous heresy <note>original: "Ketzerey"</note> than that which, through high boasting of a *Christ* within, would reject faith in the person of *Christ* as revealed externally for the reconciliation for our sins, and for the sins of all who believe in His name.

9. But to proceed further, there may well be upright and holy souls, and good Christians, who may have a true and living faith both in God the Father and also in the person of *Christ*, as God and man; who yet may still lack the third kind of faith, which is that in the name of the Holy Spirit. This faith and trust in the Holy Spirit, as the promised Spirit, the eternal Comforter and the Restorer of all things, we must pursue with earnestness. And whoever thus believes in the Holy Spirit, so that He may make an end of sins and bring eternal righteousness into the soul, and thus bring the Tabernacle or the dwelling of God down into and upon the earth; so that God may dwell in man and man in God, and the Holy Spirit may be everything in man and man be nothing; such a one truly believes in the Third Ministry <term>Bedienung</term>. <margin>so Luke 18 v 8</margin> <note>original: "si Luc. 18 v 8"</note> is to be understood: Do you also think that the Son of Man, when He comes, will find faith on earth? For it is difficult to believe that He here means or understands faith in His person, because the multitude of those who today believe in His person is great; and it is hardly to be feared that a general falling away from the acknowledgment of Him will happen before Christ's second coming. This shall be enough to distinguish the true saving faith in these three kinds, according to the three-fold name and power of the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, under Their different successive services or revelations. Now I will proceed to deal also with the grades of the true saving and justifying faith.

## Chapter VI.

->On the Grades of Saving Faith.<-

1. Just as faith is diverse in its many kinds; so is it also different in its grades. Nothing is clearer in the teaching of Christ and His apostles than that there are grades of faith. For we read of little faith and of great faith; of weak and strong faith; of victorious and triumphant faith; of active-work-
<meta>catchword: ing</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>167</page-num>
<header>inner Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ.</header>

ing and powerful faith; and of faith without doubt. Christ said to His disciples: O you of little faith. Regarding which the following places are to be seen: Matthew 6 v 30 and 8 v 26, as also Chapter 14 v 31 and Chapter 16 v 8. Of a great faith we read in Matthew 8 v 10: I have not found such great faith, even in Israel. And of a strong faith, Matthew 9 v 2: When Jesus saw that their faith in Him was so strong, He healed the paralytic <note>original: "Gichtbrüchtigen" — literally one afflicted with gout or palsy, usually translated as paralytic in this context</note> of his sickness. Other examples of this strong faith are to find in Matthew 9 v 22, 29; Mark 10 v 52; and Luke 17 v 19. For it was the strength of faith in the patients which always awakened such expressions as these: Be of good cheer, my son, your sins are forgiven you; or be of good cheer, my daughter, your faith has made you well. And was it not a victorious and triumphant faith which made Christ cry out: Let it happen to you according to your faith? Was that not a more than triumphant faith which the Syrophoenician woman <note>original: "Weib aus Syrophoenice"</note> had, who is mentioned in Matthew 15 v 27? Was that not a truly active faith which Christ commanded to be preached in all the world, of her who anointed His feet and washed them with her tears? Matthew 26 v 6–14. Compared with Galatians 5 v 6: In Christ neither circumcision nor uncircumcision avails anything, but faith which is active through love. And is that not a powerful faith, of which the LORD once said: Your faith has made you well? Through this powerful faith the eyes of the blind were opened, the ears of the deaf opened, the sick healed, the lame made to walk, the lepers cleansed, and the evil spirits cast out. And just as this happened outwardly, so it happens and is also worked inwardly and spiritually in the power of God through this faith, which works toward salvation, or true saving faith (1 Peter 1 v 5). To which active-working faith the prayer of the Apostle in 2 Thessalonians 1 v 11 also belongs.

<sig>2 But</sig>

<summary>This page argues that while many believe in Christ's person, few possess the "Third Ministry" faith in the Holy Spirit, and begins a categorization of faith into various biblical grades such as little, great, strong, and active faith.</summary>

2. But to go a little more precisely into the matter, let us hear what the Scripture says about the higher grades of faith—namely, such a faith which effectively brings about the desired or believed thing. For the result must certainly show which is a powerfully-working faith: and whether a faith is small or great, weak or strong, overcoming or overcome, doubtful or without doubt, the result will clearly demonstrate and prove. We read explicitly of a faith without doubting in Matthew 21 verse 21: JESUS spoke to His disciples: Truly I say to you; if you have faith and do not doubt, you shall not only do what was done to the fig tree, but also if you shall say to this mountain, be lifted up and cast into the sea, it shall happen. Following this in verse 22: And everything (NB. <note>original: "NB." stands for *nota bene*, meaning "note well" or "pay attention"</note> everything) that you shall ask in prayer, if you believe, you shall receive it. Here is a strong and powerful faith. This is a faith with a true testimony—an undoubting faith that can have everything it wants and asks for. We have a heroic example of this in Romans 4 verses 18, 19, 20: which is written for our teaching and encouragement in this grace, so that we may be true children of Abraham according to faith, and if we are children, we may also be heirs. It is said of him: And because he was not weak in faith, he did not consider his own body, which was already dead (since he was nearly a hundred years old), nor the dead body of Sarah; for he did not doubt the promise of God through unbelief (he did not doubt or hesitate in the slightest) but was strong in faith and gave God the glory. Nor do we have the single testimony of this Apostle alone for our encouragement, but all the other Apostles, whose writings we have, agree unanimously in this: that they give God the glory through such a faith as this. The counsel of Saint James is certainly good and sure, and may be applied and extended to all circumstances: If any among

<column-break/>

<page-num>169</page-num>
<header>inner Prayer and Incarnation of JESUS Christ.</header>

you lacks wisdom (or whatever it may be), let him ask it of God, and it shall be given to him. But let him ask (mark the condition) in faith and not waver, do not doubt, without any intermingling of unbelief, James 1 verses 5, 6. This grade of faith without doubting, which does not waver nor mix faith with unbelief, is indistinguishable from miracle-working faith, as we have noted. It can open the heavens. It can draw down the Holy Spirit with His working power to actually bring to pass that which one believes (to do or to receive); nothing is impossible for it.

3. We also read of the full certainty or assurance of faith, and of full persuasion in faith. In this manner Abraham was fully persuaded in his mind in Romans 4 verse 21—that is, in the spirit of faith—that what God had promised, He was also able to do; and thus through his faith, he gave Him the due honor and glorified Him. Regarding this, see further Hebrews 10 verse 22: how we should step forward to the throne of grace with sincere hearts in full faith, and consider what it means to come to God in the fullness of faith, or in perfect assurance, without any wavering or instability. For such a man who is inconstant and fickle or doubtful in his faith, let him not think that he will receive anything from the LORD, James 1 verses 6–9. Therefore, let us take good care that in all our prayers we approach God with sincere hearts, in the full assurance of faith. This is to be strong and rich in faith: this is also to have the mystery of faith in a good conscience, as mentioned in 1 Timothy 3 verse 9. And this is finally to attain to all the riches of the full certainty of understanding, to the knowledge of the mystery of faith in God the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, Colossians 2 verse 2.

4. Furthermore and beyond this, we read of an overcoming and victorious faith, as the highest...

It emphasizes that full spiritual assurance is necessary to receive answers to prayer and to understand the mysteries of God.</summary>

highest grade and ultimate goal of the same: and of our reaching the end of the race-course of faith. Of which the Holy Scripture makes mention here and there. This is the victory, says the Apostle, that overcomes the world, namely our faith *1 John 5 verse 4.* Look further regarding this in *Revelation 14 verse 12* and *15 verse 2.* Here are those who keep the faith of **JESUS**, says the Spirit. And what is this faith of **JESUS** other than the overcoming, victorious, and triumphant faith which was in Him; and through which we receive the victory over the beast, and over his image, and over his mark; and are made powerful to overcome, namely as **JESUS**, the author and finisher of our faith, has overcome, and to triumph with Him over all His and our enemies, to stand upon the sea of glass and to have the harps of God. For this brings us to receive the end of our faith *1 Peter 1 verse 9* and no grade that falls short of this, or is lower, is able to do such a thing. This will make us sing the song of praise of **Moses** and the song of the Lamb; which no one can ever learn except the mighty overcomers in the spirit of faith.

5. And in this manner, nothing is clearer than that there are grades of true <term>soul-saving faith</term> <note>original: "Seelig-machenden Glaubens"; refers to the faith that leads to salvation or "blessedness."</note>. And therefore the Scripture tells us so often of a progress in faith, and of going forward (as we may read in *Romans 1 verse 17*) from faith to faith, or from one grade of faith to another, from the small to the great, from the weak to the strong, and from the doubting to that which is without doubt. It exhorts us to grow in faith, to press forward in it, to increase in the grades of faith, and to grow up from one grade to another, until we finally come to the conquering and overcoming faith, to the completion of our faith, namely to the highest grade of the same. See *Luke 17 verse 5, 2 Corinthians 10 verse 15* and *2 Thessalonians 1 verse 3.* Which—to pass over others here—may be enough to set this matter beyond all doubt. And in truth, if the Apostle

---

<page-num>171</page-num>
# inner Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ

**Paul** <note>original: "paulus"</note> exhorts Christians to examine themselves whether they are in the faith; or whether they have the true soul-saving faith in themselves or not, *2 Corinthians 13 verse 5*, then every Christian is no less bound to examine himself and to investigate with diligence in which grade of true faith he stands; whether in a weak or strong grade, whether in a lower or higher grade; or whether he be poor or rich in faith: and to pray with the disciples of **Christ**: **LORD, increase our faith**; so that he may thus grow up into **Christ** in all things; yes, that he may grow in a more heartfelt or excellent way, as those at **Thessalonica** did; and because they were first nourished with the pure and unadulterated milk of the Word, they might grow up thereby to the perfect stature of **Christ's** mature age, and through faith receive the heavenly gift, as and according to how he will grow up, together with the strong meat <note>A biblical metaphor for advanced spiritual teaching, as opposed to "milk" for beginners.</note> which is then prepared for him. *1 Corinthians 3 verse 2, 1 Peter 2 verse 2.*

6. But that such various or different grades and growths of faith exist in the inward progress of <term>Rebirth</term> <note>original: "Wiedergebuhrt"; the process of spiritual regeneration.</note> may be seen even more clearly from this: that faith is a warring, fighting, and striving grace, and is also frequently tempted and searched in many ways by the dragon and his angels, by the beast and the reproaches of this world, by the false prophet of his own <term>outer man</term> <note>The physical or worldly self, as opposed to the "inner man" or spirit.</note>, and the corruptions of the spirit and flesh. But before faith can obtain the full victory over all its enemies, it must be mighty enough to be able to stand through itself; it must stand alone, and must stand against everything that sets itself against it; it must grow up through all patience and tribulations: so that it may become and be an overcoming faith, a victorious faith, a triumphant faith. It must strive and fight hard for the conquest before it can wear the crown of victory. *1 Corinthians 16 verse 13.* And in this way, faith reaches the point where it becomes steadfast, immovable, and invincible, standing firm and resisting all temptations.

<summary>This page describes faith as a progressive journey through various "grades," moving from weakness to a triumphant, "overcoming" state. It emphasizes the necessity for believers to examine their level of faith and strive for spiritual maturity through the internal battles of the "Rebirth."</summary>

Faith must fight the good fight against all its spiritual enemies / against the world / against the flesh and against the devil. Faith / I say / must fight the good fight; it must run its race-course and finish its course / before it can be crowned with the triumphant crown of Paradise over all its enemies.

# Chapter VII.
->An Investigation into the Ground of the Grades in Faith.<-

1. The ground of these grades of faith is taken from the nature of this spirit of faith / or the grace of faith. It is said / that it is initially sown in us as a **little grain** <note>A reference to the mustard seed (Matthew 13:31).</note> / that is / very small and weak: and thus it grows from this weak grade to a strong faith; and from a strong faith to a powerfully-working faith. It is initially presented only as the smallest among all seeds / however great it may afterwards become or be. Also, this is not only true of the grace of faith / but also of all other graces / such as love, gentleness, humility, patience, wisdom, etc. that they [grow] in a holy

weak when sown in the Father’s dispensation / and are subject to many assaults, trials, and temptations from the world / from flesh and blood / and the devil; however, through these temptations they grow up to become strong plants and mighty trees / under the dispensation and service of the Son / which is the Evangelical service; and thus they proceed to their full age and perfection of strength in the dispensation of the Holy Spirit: then they arrive at their full assurance / at their overcoming and victorious state.

3. But there is yet another ground for these graces of faith / and consequently also for all other Christian graces / which originate from this / which is taken from the consideration of the progressive state of Christianity / according to the state of natural birth and life. For just as in the natural / so also in the spiritual life there are different ages: and according to these ages (q) the Wisdom of God enters into holy souls / and makes them / according to their different capacities or receptivities / partakers of the Heavenly grace or gift. Therefore, some are children in Christ; others strong men; and again others elders or old men and fathers. But just as in natural life the child may eat and drink / and yet cannot add a cubit to his stature or size: even so a person may eat and drink spiritually / may partake of spiritual nourishment / and live by faith / and yet not be able to add the slightest bit to his spiritual stature and size. Considering that just as the growth of the natural and external life does not stand in the choice / will, or power of the one who eats and drinks physically: just as little does the growth and increase of the spiritual life / or the life of faith / stand in the choice or power of the one who eats spiritually

<meta>This page continues the author's exploration of the "growth of faith," moving from the initial "seed" stage discussed on the previous page to the specific stages of spiritual maturity: childhood, youth, and fatherhood. It uses the analogy of the sun and the "inward birth of Christ" to explain how a believer progresses toward spiritual perfection.</meta>

<page-num>176</page-num>
<header>The Third Little Treatise on the</header>

...and eats and drinks and lives by the Word of God: rather, it is the heavenly Father alone who gives it. Paul may plant, and Apollos may water: but no one, except Him, can give the increase and the growth. <note>Referencing 1 Corinthians 3:6: "I planted, Apollos watered, but God gave the growth."</note> This is well worth our marking and considering, if we are ever to arrive at any growth in faith and not remain forever children, or in our immature infancy.

**4.** The stage of infants and children is a delicate and weak stage: and likewise, their gifts and graces are weak, according to their childlike state. But after they have come up to the stage of young men, then their gifts and graces have become strong. And after they have become elders or fathers, then their gifts and graces have grown toward victory and to their full stage of perfection. And in this manner, the grace of faith is given to each among us according to the measure of the gift of the grace of **Christ** (Ephesians 4:7); that is, according to a certain proportion or symmetry, through which **Christ**, who is Himself the gift of God for us, reveals Himself to our inward man distinctly and by degrees, thereby making us younger or older in the Divine life. For just as the visible sun in the visible world always adds something to the age of the visible and natural man through the number of its rotations: even so the invisible Sun (which is the Sun of Righteousness) in the invisible world—in the intellectual and spiritual world, in the heavenly world of light—truly adds something in the same way to the age of the invisible spiritual man and his growth in the spirit and power of faith through its repeated visits and the lifting up of its Divine countenance. Concerning these different stages according to the measure of the inward birth of **Christ**, see Hebrews 5:13, 14; 1 John 2:12, 13, 14; and Ephesians 4:13. Compare these with Wisdom 4:8, 9. <note>original: "Sap. 4 v 8/9" refers to the Book of the Wisdom of Solomon.</note>

**5.** The stage of childhood lasts as long as we live under the Father's guidance, who leads us and

<meta>catchword: brings</meta>

<page-num>177</page-num>
<header>Inward Birth and Incarnation of JESUS Christ.</header>

brings us to the point where we believe with a living faith in and on **Jesus Christ**, the Son of God: so long as this is the case, we are small and weak. But after we are transferred from the Father’s time and service so that we may be under the service of **Christ**, the Son, then we reach the point where we become young and strong Christians. Those who are in this stage have the Word of God and the pure divinity of **Christ** abiding in them: His body and blood live in them; their powers and graces have become strong; they do not doubt, they do not waver; they can stand firm in nature and against temptations, and overcome the tempter, who is the Evil One (1 John 2:14). But when **Jesus** Himself leads them over into the service of the Holy Spirit, then they become, in Holy Understanding, elders and fathers in the New Jerusalem-state of Divine Peace; then they come to the venerable old age that is not to be measured by the number of years, but according to the growth of Divine faith and the life of God; then they recognize Him who is from the beginning, and the anointing from the Holy One teaches them all things; then Wisdom has become "gray hair" for them, <note>A reference to Wisdom of Solomon 4:9: "But understanding is gray hair for men, and a blameless life is ripe old age."</note> and they have become witnesses of the sufferings of **Christ**, and thus also of the power of His resurrection, being made partakers of Him in His revealed glory and majesty; then their persons, together with their graces, have come to their full stature and growth, to their total perfection, and to their overcoming and triumphant state in **Christ**, who is thus revealed and made known within them.

# On the Spirit of Faith:
->Which contains the words and matters found therein.<-

->A.<-
**Apostles**: their difficult understanding of faith in the name of **Jesus Christ** <note>Original: "harter begriff," suggesting the disciples struggled to grasp the spiritual nature of Christ's name</note>, chapter 5, verse 4. Their entire purpose of teaching was faith in the person of **Jesus Christ** as the Son of God and Man. Chapter 5, verse 8.

->B.<-
**Administration**, or the service of the stewardship of the Church of God is threefold: (1) of the Father, (2) of the Son, and (3) of the Holy Spirit; chapter 5, verses 6, 7–9. What the Jews and Turks <note>In this period, "Turks" was often used as a general term for Muslims</note> believe, or under what kind of administration they exist, chapter 5, verses 6, 7. How these 3 services follow one another. Chapter 5, verse 10.

->C.<-
**Christians**, those of today, who cling only to the outer confession, do not possess the precious faith, chapter 1, verse 2. **Christians**, the true ones, should be good stewards of the grace of faith and not forfeit it, chapter 1, verse 12. They can believe in the Father, and also in the person of **Christ** as God and Man, and yet—due to a lack of the **Baptism of Fire**—lack the faith in the Holy Spirit, chapter 5, 9, 10. Everyone is bound to examine with diligence in what degree of true faith they stand, chapter 6, 5. What everyone must observe if they wish to arrive at any growth. Chapter 7, 3.
**Christ**: is initially sown in us as a seed, small and weak in stature; also what is understood by Him in this context, chapter 7, 1 (*) 2. Reveals Himself to us by degrees, chapter 7, 4.

->D.<-
**Service**, the Evangelical, is under the dispensation of the Son. Chapter 7, 2.

->E.<-
**Elders**, their state is the ministry of the Holy Spirit; they are the fathers of the **New Jerusalem**. Their age consists in the growth of Divine faith, and wisdom is their gray hair. Chapter 7, verse 5.

->F.<-
**Peace**, the Divine, is the state of the **New Jerusalem**. Chapter 7, verse 5.

<header>on the Spirit of Faith.</header>
<sig>M 4</sig>
<meta>Continuity: This page continues the alphabetical index for the Third Treatise, focusing heavily on the various definitions and qualities of "Spirit" and "Faith."</meta>

->G.<-
**Gift**, the heavenly, is received through faith. Chapter 6, verse 5.
**Patience**, in this, faith must grow up through all tribulations. Chapter 6, verses 6, 7.
**Spirit** of Faith; see **Faith**, the true and living. Without the same, the life of faith cannot be quickened: Introduction, verse 2. To give the same stands in **Jesus'** power; Introduction, verse 3. **Spirit**, the Holy, desires to dwell in man; is, alongside the Father and Son, the object <note>Original: "Vorwurff," an archaic term for the "object" or "subject matter" toward which an action is directed</note> of faith, chapter 5, verse 5. In His service, He makes an end of all sin; today there are few who believe this, chapter 5, verse 9. His service in us leads to perfection and into the state of the Elders or Fathers, chapter 7, verses 2, 5. **Spirit**, our Eternal, is distinct from the Spirit of Faith, not to be mingled with it; it is generated and formed through the Spirit of Eternity, chapter 1, verses 6, 13. What it, united with the Spirit of Faith, may give, chapter 1, verse 12. It has its own powers and capacities for attraction and repulsion, which are not to be mingled with the heavenly gift, chapter 1, verse 13. These powers are made capable—through the Spirit of the grace of faith—of giving assent, of willing, or of believing what God reveals, chapter 1, verse 14.
**Faith**, the Historical. What is its object? It may be divided into the Legal and the Evangelical, chapter 2, verse 2. It also extends to private Divine revelations that are not against the Holy Scripture, chapter 2, verse 5. God is the author of the same, chapter 2, verse 8. What it is on God's and on man's side, chapter 2, verse 9. Can be made active and working, chapter 3, verse 1. To faith without doubt, nothing is impossible, chapter 6, verses 2, 3. **Faith**, the victorious: overcomes the beast, his image, and his mark, chapter 6, verse 4. **Faith**, the inconstant, or that which lasts for a time, chapter 3, verse 1. Can be entirely lost again through our neglect and disobedience, chapter 3, verse 2. **Faith**, the true living, is not given to all, but only to some, and to whom it may be given, chapter 1, verse 2.
**Spirit of Faith** is a spirit distinct from the Spirit of God, **Christ**, and the Holy Spirit, and also from our eternal spirit and from the Spirit of Wisdom, chapter 1, verse 6. Is wrought in us through a threefold Divine revelation, chapter 1, verse 7. Wherein the difference between the Spirit of God and the grace of faith lies, chapter 1, verse 8; chapter 1, verse 9. Difference between the Spirit of God and the Spirit of Faith, chapter 1, verse 10. God alone is the giver and worker of this faith in us, chapter 1, verses 11, 12. That there are three kinds of the same, chapter 2, verse 1. In what the faith in the Holy Spirit consists, chapter 5, verse 9. **Degrees of Faith** are various, such as small, great, weak, strong, victorious, triumphant, and without doubt, chapter 6, chapter 7. **Faith**, the miraculous...

180 Index of the Third Little Treatise

[The Spirit of Faith is] active; performs outer miracles like Christ, and even greater ones; chapter 4, verses 1–2. It is the form- and essence-giving means <note>The "form" of something in period philosophy is what makes it what it is</note> of saving faith and is inseparable from it. Chapter 4, verse 3.

**Believers**, the true ones; each can, according to the degree of their faith, arrive at miracle-working faith; chapter 4, verse 1. Which ones those are who believe in the **Holy Spirit**, and what they believe. Chapter 5, verse 9.

## H.

**Saints**, under the service of the Father; believed in God the Father; and strictly speaking not in the Person of Christ; but they had a kind of inner faith in all the promises concerning the same. Chapter 5, verse 6.

## J.

**Jerusalem**, the Heavenly; the hope of righteousness; chapter 5, verse 5. Who the Fathers of the same are, and how they are qualified. Chapter 7, verse 5.

**Disciples of Christ**, first believed in God the Father; chapter 5, verse 3. They were of a hard understanding <note>meaning they found it difficult to grasp</note> in comprehending faith in **Jesus Christ**. Chapter 5, verse 4.

**Youths in Christ**, have the Word of God, the pure Divinity of Christ, with His body and blood within them, and they stand firm without wavering in all temptations. Chapter 7, verse 5.

**Jews**, today believe in God the Father and not in Christ, and why; chapter 5, verses 6–7. They lack faith in Christ’s Person and the process of salvation; yet they still believe in the true God, and thus hiddenly also in the unity of the **Holy Trinity**. Chapter 5, verse 7.

## K.

**Heresy**, none greater than those who reject Christ’s external Person for the reconciliation of sin for the sake of the glory of a "Christ from within." Chapter 5, verse 8.

**Children**, in Christ; are led by the Father toward faith in **Jesus Christ**, the Son of God. Chapter 7, verse 5.

**Servants of God**, are bound to believe what God reveals to them. Chapter 2, verse 5.

## L.

**Body**, the Divine or Heavenly; is the root or **Body of Grace**, from which love, meekness, patience, wisdom, etc., sprout; which in us, together with these fruits, is initially a small and weak seed. Chapter 7, verses 1–2.

## M.

**Humans**, those who lack true faith, sense, and understanding are irrational; chapter 1, verse 2. They can, through their inner instrumental faculties, incline themselves toward or away from believing or not believing, consenting or not consenting. Chapter 1, verse 14. The acceptance of the mind and the consent of the will in that which God reveals to us out of free grace lies with the human being. Chapter 2, verse 9.

<meta>catchword: O. Re-</meta>

---

<page-num>181</page-num>
<header>On the Spirit of Faith. 181</header>

## O.

**Revelations**, private; are not all infallible, nor to be considered equal to Holy Scripture, and why; chapter 2, verses 6–7. To ascribe infallibility to them before one has reached the state of the **Holy Spirit** is sin and presumption; chapter 2, verse 7. To consent to and accept revelations from God lies within the human will. Chapter 2, verse 9.

## R.

**Boasting**, the high [boasting] of a "Christ from within," and through this rejecting faith in the external Person of Christ for the reconciliation of all our sins—we who believe in His Name—is one of the most dangerous heresies. Chapter 5, verse 8.

## S.

**Son of God**, in whose administration faith grows, alongside other graces, into a strong plant and tree. Chapter 7, verse 2.

**Sun of Righteousness**, the invisible one; always adds something to the growth of the spiritual person. Chapter 7, verse 4.

**State of Children**: A soft, weak state; however long it lasts, their gifts and graces are weak. Chapter 7, verses 4–5.

**State of Youths**: Their gifts and graces are strong, and one arrives at this state when one is transferred from the Father’s days or service into the service of Christ. Chapter 7, verses 4–5. (See further: **Youths in Christ**, and **State of the Fathers**.) When their gifts and graces grow toward victory and total perfection, they then stand in the service of the **Holy Spirit**. Chapter 7, verses 4–5. See further: **Elders**.

## T.

**Turks** <note>In the 17th century, "Turks" was the standard term for Muslims</note>, believe only in God the Father; chapter 5, verse 6. Yet hiddenly also in the unity of the Trinity; because no one can believe in Him without also believing in the Son and Spirit; yet they still lack the **Justifying Faith** in and toward Christ’s Person, and why? Chapter 5, verse 7.

## V.

**Father**: No one can believe in Him without also believing in the Son and Spirit, because they are in one another and one is not without the other; chapter 5, verse 7. In His administration, faith, with other graces, is sown small and weak, and is subject to many temptations; chapter 7, verse 2. He alone gives the growth in spiritual stature and size. Chapter 7, verse 3.

## W.

**Growth** in spiritual stature and size belongs to the Heavenly Father alone, and does not come from spiritual eating and drinking. Chapter 7, verse 3.

**Warning** to hold fast to faith. Chapter 3, verse 7.

**Wisdom** enters into souls (according to Wisdom 7:27 <note>A reference to the deuterocanonical Book of Wisdom</note>) according to their age or capacity. Chapter 7, verse 3.

**Word**, the proclaimed; must be mixed with faith if it is to be of help; chapter 1, verses 1, 2, 14.

## The Fourth Little Treatise:
# On the Union of Natures, etc.
### To which are prefixed some Propositions or certain presented Theses concerning the Outer and Inner Man:
->Composed and described for consideration<-
->by<-
## Johann Pordädsch <note>Anglicized as John Pordage</note> / M. D.
->August 3, 1676.<-

<margin>I.</margin> <note>The original features a decorative initial E featuring ornate scrolling foliage and floral patterns.</note> Every human being carries two persons upon and around themselves; namely, an outer and an inner man.

II. The inner man is the invisible man; hidden, invisible, and unknown; and therefore called the hidden man; and he has his dwelling or lodging in the outer man. The outer man is visible, tangible, and known; and is, as it were, the shell and case <note>or "casket/box"</note> of the inner.

III. The inner man is the eternal, immortal, and incorruptible man; the outer, however, is the temporal, mortal, and corruptible man; as one who is subject to a re-dissolution into his first beginnings.

IV. The inner is a complete and perfect man, who is joined together and consists of an immortal body, an immortal soul, and an immortal spirit. Likewise, the outer is also a complete man, who is composed and consists of a mortal body, a mortal soul, and a mortal spirit.

V. The inner man is the fallen man, or the man of the Fall and apostasy; he has lost Para-
<meta>catchword: -dise (dies)</meta>

---

<header>The Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc. 183</header>
<page-num>183</page-num>

-dise <note>original: *Paradies*</note>: but the outer is not the fallen man; for he was not born in Paradise, nor for Paradise; and accordingly has also never fallen out of Paradise. For he could not lose that which he never had in his possession. The outer man was born and created for this world; this is his paradise and his heaven, namely, that for which he was created: and thus he can nevermore enter into Paradise, which belongs solely to the inner man.

VI. The inner eternal man is the true Paradise-man; he stood in Paradise in his head, the first Adam, and fell in him; and is born a true Paradise-man, that is, he is such a one in his birth through infusion <note>original: *Infusion*; the spiritual "pouring-in" of the soul</note> into the outer; a true angelic <note>original: *Englischer*; in this context, the word refers to "angelic" (Engel) rather than the English nation</note> man, that is, he is a spirit-man; he is both in the form of an angel and in the figure or shape of an angel: Therefore the true Paradisiacal man also has the likeness of an angel in his external form and shape. But the outer man is a true animal-man; he has a body provided with instrumental members, with the reproductive organs; he has an animal nature and a sensory life; and is thus clearly in the likeness of an animal-man: only his rational spirit is the most excellent thing in him, through which he is exalted to be a lord over the animals in this his own out-birth, and to have dominion over them, and to rule all things in this visible creation.

VII. The inner eternal man is the sole subject <note>original: *Subjectum*</note> of sin, and thus also of the punishment that sin deserves; he alone is capable, together with the dragon and his angels, of being tormented in the dark world or principle <note>original: *Principio*; refers to one of the fundamental realms of existence in Behmenist philosophy</note>. The outer mortal man is not the subject of sin, nor capable of its deserved plagues and punishments: for he cannot endure in the hellish torment and pain, but perishes in his own mortal principle, along with the animals of the earth, because he is a purely earthly animal-man. So also
<meta>catchword: VIII. The (VIII. Der)</meta>

<summary>This page introduces John Pordage's "Fourth Little Treatise," which outlines a dualistic view of humanity consisting of an eternal "inner man" (angelic and fallen) and a temporal "outer man" (animalistic and worldly). Pordage argues that only the inner man belongs to Paradise and is subject to spiritual judgment, while the outer man belongs solely to the physical world.</summary>
<keywords>John Pordage, inner man, outer man, Paradise, The Fall, angelic nature, animal nature, immortality, spiritual anatomy, dualism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the "Fourth Little Treatise" by John Pordage. The previous page established that humans consist of two distinct persons: an inner, eternal man (soul/spirit) and an outer, temporal man (physical/animal). This section explores which of these "men" is capable of salvation and spiritual transformation.</meta>

<page-num>184</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Little Treatise on Union</header>

VIII. The inner eternal man is the sole subject <note>original: *Subjectum*, meaning the entity or vessel upon which an action is performed</note> of rebirth, or the heavenly new birth. In contrast, the outer mortal man is as incapable of the new birth or rebirth as an animal: he is born for this world to be an Esau <note>In the Bible, Esau represents the physical, earthly nature that forfeits its spiritual birthright</note> within it; and he is capable of no other principle; it is his birthright and the land of his birth.

IX. The inner eternal man is the sole subject of restoration or renewal; since he has fallen from his original-paradisiacal state of purity into a state of sin and misery, guilt and anxiety, he is capable of renewal through Christ. The outer man, however, is not the subject of renewal. For Christ comes to restore the lost paradisiacal man; but the outer man is not the paradisiacal man who was lost, because he was never in Paradise. And the final purpose of Christ's coming was certainly not to restore an animal-man <note>original: *Thier-Menschen*</note>.

X. The inner and eternal man is—just as he is the sole subject or object of rebirth—consequently also the object of redemption, justification, adoption as a child, sanctification, and of being made a new creature in Christ. He alone is capable of the freedom and privileges of the birth, life, death, resurrection, ascension, and glorification of Christ; and thus also of personal union and communion with His glorified person. He alone is washed by Christ’s blood from the guilt, stain, and dominion of sin; he alone is made alive by His Spirit; he alone puts on Christ’s divine body; he alone believes in Him, eats and drinks Him, and perceives his own rebirth, justification, and sanctification; his own dying in the name of Jesus, and his own resurrection in Christ’s pure body; and he is capable of being either an angel of light or of darkness. The outer animal-man

<meta>catchword: but (aber)</meta>

---

<page-num>185</page-num>
<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc.</header>

but is entirely and completely incapable of all these things. He is excluded from eternity: for he was never born or created in eternity. He never came down from eternity, and therefore he can never ascend into eternity.

XI. The outer man is entirely and completely contrary to the inner man; he fights against him without ceasing, opposes him and resists him; he is an evil neighbor to him. And until this enmity is removed, he is nothing but evil; no better than a pure Judas, a traitor, and a murderer; and this he is out of his own innate essence or being, until he becomes a servant to his younger brother, and then the prophecy is fulfilled: *The elder shall serve the younger.* <note>Genesis 25:23. In this mystical interpretation, the "elder" is the physical body/outer man, and the "younger" is the spiritual/inner man born later through grace.</note> However, so long and until he is thus made subject to the inner man, he is in truth and in every way only a bad master, though he may indeed be made into a good servant. Indeed, as long as the outer man is not made subject to the inner as his servant, he is entirely and thoroughly evil and dangerous to him: not only because of his contrary and envious nature, because he will not let the inner man rule over him; but also, and especially, because the devil and his angels, together with the beast and its constellations (*) within him, work through his properties to tempt the inner man, to seduce him, and to keep him imprisoned under the outer man. Therefore, insofar as the dragon works in him to incite the inner man to sin, and as long as the beast works in him and drives him to bow before its image and its idols and to worship them, to that extent the outer man is entirely an evil brother to the inner. However, the outer mortal man is in his—

This word also signifies the influence, power, and effect of the stars. This is for the sake of the simple-minded.

<summary>Pordage argues that only the "inner man" is capable of salvation and spiritual transformation, as the "outer man" is purely animalistic and bound to the physical world. He describes the outer man as a naturally hostile entity that must be subjugated to serve the inner man, likening their relationship to the biblical brothers Esau and Jacob.</summary>

<summary>This page explores the dichotomy between the outer, rational-animal man and the inner, eternal-spiritual man, contrasting their origins, dietary needs, and ultimate destinies. The outer man is governed by earthly reason and physical sustenance, while the inner man is sustained by the spiritual body and blood of Christ.</summary>

<meta>This page continues John Pordage’s "Fourth Little Treatise on Union." The previous pages established the distinction between the "inner man" (the eternal, spiritual soul) and the "outer man" (the mortal, animalistic, and rational self). It argued that while the outer man must be governed by reason to be "moral," he remains part of a temporary, earthly realm that cannot inherit eternity.</meta>

<page-num>188</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Little Treatise on Union</header>

lot and portion of the outer mortal man. He requires (ultimately) nothing that this world can give or provide; he has no need for its gold or silver, nor can he use them; and therefore he has no need to bow before the spirit of this world for the sake of a shoelace: for he was created to be a **LORD** over all things, whereas the outer man—who is meant to be his servant—must work and labor in this outer world for his own life and its preservation.

XIV. This outer world, in its own nature, is an evil and cursed thing to the inner eternal man; but to the outer man, it is a good thing.

XV. All beings of this outbirth <note>original: *Ausgebuhrt*. A term used in Christian mysticism to describe the physical world as a "birthing out" or manifestation of spiritual realities into a lower, material form</note>, or this elementary world, are in and of themselves merely temporal and transitory beings; but they are evil beings to the inner man: nonetheless, they are all good, useful, and necessary to the outer man.

XVI. The outer life, with all its arts, sciences, professions, trades, and crafts, is also evil in this regard; they are thus bad, useless, and burdensome to the inner man on his way to the New Birth: but they are all good, useful, and necessary to the outer man for his external maintenance.

XVII. The animal, sensory, and rational nature in this visible creation is exceedingly good, useful, and advantageous to the outer man, and even necessary for many things: but to the inner man, in view of the Mount Zion World <note>A reference to the heavenly or divine realm, contrasted with the "world of this world"</note>, his animal, sensory, and rational life is an evil life, entirely useless, and no more necessary to him than a fifth wheel on a wagon; indeed, it is wholly hindering, vexing, and burdensome.

XVIII. This visible creation and outbirth, considered in and of itself, is to the inner man of eternity both Babylon, Egypt, and Babel <note>Biblical metaphors for exile, spiritual confusion, and bondage</note>; but to the outer man, it is his heaven and paradise; it is the inner man's hell,

<meta>and</meta>

<page-num>189</page-num>
<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc.</header>

and the outer man's heaven; it is the inner man's curse, and the outer man's blessing.

XIX. This visible outbirth is the actual locality, place, and principle <note>original: *Principium*. Here meaning a fundamental state of existence or a governing spiritual realm</note> of the inner man’s fallen state, into which he fell through disobedience; and therein he encounters devils in their own principle, who tempt him without ceasing: for the devils' principle permeates this transitory principle through and through; and because their principle penetrates this perishable principle, he is subject to their temptations. However, he also encounters the Holy Angels of Mount Zion therein (for their principle also exists and passes through this principle), who strengthen him to resist the promptings of the devils. Herein he also finds the beast, the god and spirit of this world, who tempts and entices him to remain in his principle and not to depart from it. Here also the false prophet confronts him—that is, the reason of his outer man—who advises him to live quietly among the visible good things of this world and to lead a "devout" life in an outward form and appearance of godliness. But this visible outbirth cannot be a fallen principle or a tempting state for the outer man, whose happiness consists in being rich, high, great, and honored within it.

XX. This visible world, with all its arts, sciences, professional inventions, and trades, are the fruits brought forth by the Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil: they are not fruits that have sprung from the Tree of Life.

XXI. The outer life, with all the various trades and businesses, are the effects and fruits of the Fall: and all toil and labor comes from the loss of Paradise. Therefore, although they are good, useful, and necessary to the outer man, they cannot benefit the inner man.

XXII. Everything that may be said of this outer visible creation may also be said of the

<summary>This passage contrasts the "inner man" (eternal soul) with the "outer man" (rational/physical self), explaining that the physical world and human reason are "hell" and "exile" for the soul, while being "paradise" and "necessity" for the physical body. It characterizes all human arts, sciences, and labor as products of the Fall and the Tree of Knowledge, rather than the Tree of Life.</summary>

<meta>This page continues John Pordage’s "Fourth Little Treatise on Union," specifically concluding the comparison between the spiritual inner man and the fallen outer man. It focuses on the necessity of regeneration through Christ to overcome the physical and rational limitations imposed by the Fall of Adam.</meta>

<page-num>190</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Little Treatise on Union</header>

be said of the outer visible man, of the outer life, and of the outer body: since they all spring and come from one and the same root, namely from Adam's Fall. They are the fruits that result from the eating of the forbidden fruit; and are therefore effects or consequences that come from the Tree of Knowledge, and fruits of the curse, and of the separation of Paradise from this **Principle** <note>original: *Principio*. In Behmenist theology, a "Principle" refers to one of the three fundamental realms of existence: the dark fire-world, the light-world, or the outer astral/elementary world.</note>.

XXIII. The Kingdom of Mount Zion, the New Jerusalem, the invisible world of Light, Love, and Glory, and this outer creation—by which I mean here our lower earthly globe—are set against and in opposition to one another. Accordingly, Holy Scripture gives to each its due name according to its own nature: therefore, search within the same, and see what names are given to each therein.

XXIV. The **Renewal** <note>original: *Wieder-Erneuerung*. Often translated as Regeneration; the process of the soul being reborn into the divine light.</note> through **JESUS** Christ is this: that He brings us again out of this world, or old creation, into the New Jerusalem. For which reason He calls to us that we should deny it, go out from it, touch it not, nor have the slightest union or communion with it. The inner eternal man must circumcise his spirit from it, or cut himself off from it; he must, as it were, turn his back to it; and must direct the face of his spirit, his mind, and his will toward the Heavenly Jerusalem, and thus strive after the invisible creation with untiring persistence.

XXV. This Renewal through Christ brings us perfectly back out of this fallen state, and delivers us from all things that forced their way into us when Adam lost Paradise. So that through Him, we must be renewed in all things, even the least of those which are effects or workings and fruits of the curse: and such are these: (1) This visible creation, in the state in which it now stands—as separated from Paradise and in **opposition** <note>original: *Contrarietät*</note> to the same—

<meta>catchword: stands :</meta>

---

<page-num>191</page-num>
<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures &c.</header>

stands: (2) The outer life of the outward man, with his animal, sensory, and rational life and nature. (3) All arts, sciences, and trades; together with the toil and labor of the outer man. (4) The outer form and shape of the outer mortal man; with his coarse body of flesh, blood, and bones; and his teeth, intestines, and reproductive organs. (5) The diseases and weaknesses of the outer man. From all of these (because they are the effects of Adam's Fall, and the consequences of the loss of Paradise and of the curse, and stem from the Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil) we must be redeemed through **JESUS** Christ and renewed to a state of Eternal Life and Freedom.

->T H E  E N D .<-

**NB.** Whether, and how far, these **Propositions** or theorems were subsequently considered by the Author does not appear anywhere in his writings; except in his next following little treatise of *Experimental Discoveries*, which were probably written not long after these.

<summary>This concluding section of the treatise argues that true spiritual renewal through Christ requires a total rejection of the fallen physical world, including human reason, anatomy, and labor. It lists the physical body and its functions as "fruits of the curse" that must be transcended to reach the New Jerusalem.</summary>

<meta>This page begins the "Fourth Little Treatise on Union" by John Pordage. It follows his previous discussions on the spiritual and fallen states of humanity, specifically intending to bridge the gap between his theoretical "System of the Worlds" and the practical reality of the "mixed world" we inhabit.</meta>

<page-num>192</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Little Treatise on Union</header>

# A Little Treatise
->on the<-
## Union of Natures, etc., composed
## and left behind in manuscript
->by<-
## John Pordage,
->Doctor of Medicine<-

### The Introduction.

<image-desc>A decorative square woodcut initial 'D' featuring foliate and floral motifs.</image-desc>
This following little treatise was intended by the author—following his System <note>original: *Systemate*</note> or Treatise on the Worlds—to explain, for the most part, the mixed world, which is the Third and visible **Principle** <note>In the theology of Jakob Böhme, which Pordage followed, the "Third Principle" refers to the physical, material world we see around us, created from a mixture of divine light and dark fire.</note>. This world is assigned to the children of men under their present condition. The author intended to show this through the manifold unions and connections of the **seeds of nature** <term>Seeds of Nature: These are the fundamental spiritual essences or "blueprints" that give rise to physical forms.</term>; but more specifically through the discovery of the origin of the outer and inner man, the nature of their union with one another, and the complete process of both from the first beginning until the end of their goal in this life. Thus, he has laid down here a draft and ground-plan of a complete discourse on the twofold human nature, according to the three principles or parts that give form and essence to each. Had he not been prevented from completing it fully, we might have been able to judge the entire matter even better. His intention and purpose was that this discourse, regarding man alone, should consist of four parts. In the first part, he intended to deal solely with the production of the outer man: which he, for the first time, as was his custom, only
<meta>catchword: superficially</meta>

---

<page-num>193</page-num>
<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc.</header>

superficially reviewed, as if for himself and for his further contemplation; but he never prepared it to be fully worked out or brought to light. However, the persistent urging of certain friends who love his spirit did not want to leave such things lying buried. Therefore, it is presented here after the style was somewhat revised (yet without the slightest change in meaning), to which meaning we have adhered most strictly. For this reason, many repetitions remain within it, which one might otherwise have willfully omitted during the revision, had not the influence of a worthy person <note>Likely referring to Jane Leade or another prominent member of the Philadelphian Society who preserved Pordage's papers.</note> persuaded the editor otherwise. The public must remain indebted to this person for all the writings that have been preserved from this author and saved from destruction. In the second part, his purpose was to deal specifically with the humanity of the outer man, in body, soul, and spirit; with his names and titles; with his triple nature; with the process of his life; with its many changes or alternations; and finally, with his death. In the third part, he intended to deal fully with the ultimate purposes of his creation, and consequently with the Fall of Man. And in the fourth part, he was minded to proceed further to the opening of the **hidden pearl** (as he calls it) or the mystery of the inward man within the outer. This latter part was laid by him as the foundation of his **Sophia** <note>original: *Sophiæ*. Referring to Divine Wisdom, often personified as a feminine spiritual guide in this tradition.</note> or spiritual discoveries. Of the second and third parts, however, nothing has been found.

->FRANCISCUS LY,<- <note>original: *Franciscus Ly*. This is a Latinized signature for Francis Lee (1661–1719), a physician and key figure in the Philadelphian Society who edited Pordage’s works.</note>

<meta>catchword: Ex-</meta>

<summary>This page introduces John Pordage's treatise on the union of the inner and outer man, outlining a planned four-part work of which only parts of the first and fourth were recovered and edited for publication by Francis Lee.</summary>

# Experimental Discoveries
## of the Union of Natures / Essences or Beings / Tinctures / Bodies / Persons and Spirits.

->A Little Treatise / which opens the ground of the great mysteries in Nature / with a special focus on Man / and on his birth into this external world.<-

### Chapter 1.
#### Of the Union of the Outer and Inner Man / or of the Union of Natures.

<margin>1.</margin> <image-desc>A decorative square woodcut initial D containing foliate scrollwork.</image-desc>This mystery of the unions is mentioned in Genesis 25:22: *and the children struggled together within her.* v. 23 *And the LORD said to her: two nations are in your womb, and two kinds of people shall be separated from your inward parts <note>original: "Ingeweyden"—this refers both to the physical entrails and the deep spiritual interior</note>; and the one people shall be stronger than the other people; and the elder shall serve the younger.* This is now a true mystery: and it represents to us the union of natures, **essences** or beings, **tinctures** <term>Tinctures: In the alchemy and mystical theology of the period, a "tincture" is a spiritualizing power or "dye" that transforms and gives quality to a substance.</term>, bodies, and persons. We truly have twins in our womb; we have two nations within ourselves; we have two kinds of people in us, who are to be separated from our inward parts; and the one will be stronger than the other; and the elder must serve the younger. The letter and the history teach and present a mystery to us; and if we wish to understand it, we must also turn our eye inward; and we must read this secret figure which is presented to us in the letter <note>The "letter" refers to the literal text of the Bible as a symbol for internal spiritual reality.</note> within ourselves.

<margin>2.</margin> But to help you understand this secret number, two,
<meta>catchword: a</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>195</page-num>
<header>Of Natures / Essences / Tinctures etc.</header>

a little better, let us consider that here it speaks not only of two or of twins; not only of two nations, and of two distinct kinds of people, but of two different ones, namely the elder and the younger, Esau and Jacob. And you see both in one womb, and in one person, namely in the womb and person of Rebecca. But who these two are, St. Paul indicates in 2 Corinthians 4:16 in these words: *though our outward man perishes, yet the inward is renewed day by day.* These words well deserve to be taken to heart by us; as they set before our eyes the wonderful union of two such contrary natures in every man. For each of our persons is prefigured through the person of Rebecca; the two nations (or two natures) in ourselves through Esau and Jacob represent the outer and inner man; Esau the outer, Jacob the inner; Esau or the outer is the elder; Jacob, or the inner man, the younger.

<margin>3.</margin> These two nations struggling with one another within us constitute the outer and inner man, as has been said, in ourselves: these are the two kinds of people who must be separated and divided from each other within ourselves; they are distinct in their birth, in their natures, in their habits, and in all their properties and qualities: and they must be kept distinct within ourselves and not mixed with one another.

<margin>4.</margin> The outer man is called Esau, because he is the earthly man, generated and born through natural birth, in the line of nature, from natural parents; born into this visible earthly world, and made subject in all things of his birth to the earth; with an earthly life, to lead an earthly life, and for the sake of the earthly life; therefore the outer man (because he alone is capable of taking the earthly things of this world to heart; indeed, he was created for the very purpose that he [should witness] the
<sig>N 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: Won-</meta>

Pordage explains that every person contains two "nations" or natures that must be distinguished and eventually separated.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the "Fourth Little Treatise," expanding on the mystical interpretation of Esau and Jacob as archetypes for the human condition. It explains why the inner, spiritual man is considered "younger" and "fallen," while the outer, physical man is "older" but not truly "fallen" in a spiritual sense.</meta>
