should explore the wonders of this world) is actually called the man of the Earth, or the **earthly man**.

5. The inner man is called **Jacob**, because he is the **heavenly man**, born from above and in a supernatural way; born indeed *within* nature, yet in a supernatural manner; not in the line of nature through natural procreation, but through the path of **infusion** <term>Infusion: From the Latin 'infusio' (pouring in); the mystical belief that the soul is poured into the body by God rather than being generated by the parents.</term> from our **Eternal Parents**; born to lead a Divine life and not an earthly one, and to seek and observe the wonders of eternity and the wonders of the invisible world. The inner man is born from above, and must therefore live above. For this reason, he is not improperly called the **Paradisiacal Man**.

6. **Esau**, therefore, the outer man, is called the **Elder**; because he is born first into nature through natural birth. And **Jacob**, the inner man, is called the **Younger**, because he must stand and live within the nature of his brother Esau, as he has no other nature in this world in which he might live. Hence, as soon as the outer man has life and has been made alive in the **womb** <note>original: "Behrmutter" — literally "bearing mother," an old term for the womb.</note> of nature, the inner man is supernaturally infused into the nature of the outer; so that our natural parents only bring forth the outer and natural man, which is, as it were, the hull or shell of the inner and spiritual one. For the inner spiritual man is infused by our Eternal spiritual parents, in a spiritual and supernatural way, into the old man; to dwell in the outer man in a hidden, invisible way; to live in his nature and essences, and to work through them, and yet be neither seen nor recognized by him.

7. Now, because this inner man dwells in the nature of the outer man, it is called his **Fallen Nature**; and it is in truth to him the nature of the **Fall**, because he should have had his birth from his Father Adam into the nature of Paradise. What a humiliation and fall is this, therefore, for the inner man, who in the Paradisiacal nature

<meta>catchword: should have</meta>

<column-break/>

should have been born, and was created for Paradise, and should have lived in his Paradisiacal properties in Paradise; to find himself now robbed of it, born in the earthly nature of this world, united with it, excluded from Paradise, and stripped of his Paradisiacal nature? To be transplanted in such a way into the earthly nature, into the nature of the earthly man, instead of the Paradisiacal, may indeed in truth and obviously be called his **fallen nature**. Through this birth now into the outer nature, he becomes a child of the Fall, fallen from the Paradisiacal nature and life, and from the paradisiacal world; and fallen into the earthly nature, descended into this transitory visible world, to be therein as a stranger and a **pilgrim**; and thus he is most properly called the **fallen man**.

8. But the outer fallen man, **Esau**, is *not* the fallen man; since he was never created for Paradise, nor did he ever come down from above; but at his first birth, through natural generation, was born an **Edomite** <term>Edomite: A reference to Esau’s biblical descendants, used here to represent a person who is purely material, animalistic, and bound to the physical world.</term>, a natural, earthly, animalistic, and worldly man. He had nothing from which he could fall; therefore he is not the man of the apostasy. He is born of time, lies in time; has a beginning, and has an end; because he never fell out of eternity into time. But the inner man, because he **originates** <note>original: "urständet"—to take one's stand or origin from a source.</note> from eternity, and was created with the express purpose of living in the eternity of Paradise, in the **3rd Principle of Light** <term>3rd Principle of Light: In the mystical system of Jacob Boehme, which Pordage followed, this represents the divine world of light, love, and spiritual reality.</term>, is in this transitory world as one born in a foreign land, and lives outside his right fatherland, outside his own birthright, and outside the land of his inheritance.

9. But that it is said that **Esau**, the outer man, is the elder, this happens because the outer man is born first in the womb of the outer nature; and the inner man is said to be the younger, because he is [infused] through infusion after...

...born to him; and both come forth together in the **fullness of time** to dwell and live together in one fleshly body in this visible world. Wherein the mystery of these two natures must be revealed.

10. But where it is said that the **elder should serve the younger**, this means that the outer man should be subject and yielded <term>Gelassen: A central concept in German mysticism meaning surrendered or detached from self-will.</term> to the inner man, and bow and completely give himself over to his will; and the inner man, in turn, should govern and rule the outer. This promise is fulfilled in the **rebirth** of the inner man, when he is awakened and enlightened to recognize himself and his own eternal origin. And then, when he is born again through his heavenly **new rebirth**—his second birth—into and toward a state of Divine grace and life, he takes up the government and dominion over the outer man himself. And then the outer man comes voluntarily to serve the inner and to be governed by him.

11. This is now not only a prophecy; it is also eternally decreed in the purpose and counsel of God, that it should be an unchangeable law or such an ordinance that shall never be changed nor broken. For which reason all must know and recognize this ordinance: that the elder shall serve the younger, the most prominent the least, and the outer shall serve the inner.

12. Now consider the ground of this unchangeable order, which may be understood thus: Just as God and the life of eternity would live and dwell in the inner man to govern and rule him, so also shall the inner man live and dwell in the outer, that He may govern and rule him, and bring him to perfect obedience and subjection. For just as God lives and walks in the inner man through His own life to govern and rule in him, so also the inner man lives and dwells in the outer, through the Divine life of himself, [guiding] him by and

<column-break/>

<page-num>199</page-num>
<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc.</header>

through the same, to govern and rule. I say, just as God Himself lives and dwells in the inner man, as in His own kingdom and sitting upon His own throne, so it is here also. The **inner man** lives, moves, and dwells in the outer as in his kingdom. He has no other kingdom in which to govern, no other throne upon which to rule, no other nations to go in and out before him; he has nothing else to rule but his own outer man. For the outer man is to the inner what the inner man is to the pure Godhead itself.

13. And this is the blessed order, in which there is no confusion, which will remain unchangeably for eternity: that God shall dwell in the inner man to rule him, and the inner in the outer man to rule and govern him; until the union between the inner and outer man is broken by the **stroke of death** and they are thus separated from each other for eternity. In this manner the elder must serve the younger, and the outer must serve and obey the inner man when he is reborn. And this is the **mystery** <note>original: "Geheimnus" — referring to a hidden spiritual truth meant to be revealed to the initiated.</note> which lies hidden under **Moses' veil** <note>A biblical reference to 2 Corinthians 3:13-16, where the veil represents the inability to see the spiritual truth of the law until turning to Christ.</note>.

14. Following this, let us now weigh and consider the words of the **Divine Paul** a little, which are, as it were, the key to this mystery: **"Though our outward man perish, yet the inward man is renewed day by day."** <note>2 Corinthians 4:16.</note> Here likewise the same number is observed: an outer and an inner man; an Esau and a Jacob; an elder and a younger. **Two** is the number that God's wisdom has brought forth: we need neither add nor take away anything. Accordingly, we must necessarily confess that we have these two twins—an outer and an inner man—neither more nor less, within us.

15. The inner man has manifold names and designations, and yet is only one and the same man. He is called the **Fallen Man**; because he, as we have said, [exists] in the earthly nature of the outer man...

<summary>This page explains the spiritual hierarchy where the outer man (Esau) must serve the inner man (Jacob), mirroring the way God rules the inner soul. The author frames this dual nature as an eternal law and cites St. Paul's teachings on the daily renewal of the inner man despite the decay of the physical body.</summary>

man dwells. He is called the **old Adam** / the **man of sin** <note>original: "Mensch der Sünden"</note> etc., so long as he is one with sin and the evil within himself; or until his second birth and Divine **rebirth**. Conversely, he is also called the **New Man** / the **man of life and grace** / the restored and renewed man etc.; namely, so long as he is one with the life and Divine nature of God within himself: and this occurs in his second birth / and through the work of **rebirth** within Him. Thus / you see / the inner man is only one and the same man / though he passes through many states. For although he passes through many changes and transformations / through many qualities and names; yet he is / in all / and through all / always only one and the same inner man. Remember therefore at all times / that we have within us only two / that is / the outer and inner man. This inward man of the heart / whether he be the old man / or the New Man; whether he be in the state of sin / or of grace; whether he be in the first or second birth / whether he is yet to be called again / redeemed / and born again / or already called / redeemed / and born again / is not two / but only one.

16. Secondly, let us consider how these two men / namely the outer and inner man / live one within the other. The inner man lives hiddenly and secretly / and as it were enclosed within the outer; both within each other / and as if together in one house / and in one person; and in this way they are inseparable and indissolubly in union. These are the **twins in one womb** <note>A reference to the biblical Jacob and Esau, often used in mystical literature to represent the struggle between the spiritual and carnal natures.</note> / in one body of flesh; and in one person; and in this visible world / as if together in one world and kingdom / and are therein living one within the other / and thus the Inner within the Outer. And nevertheless / in this close union they are nonetheless truly and essentially distinct from one another.

17. This difference between the inward and outward man consists in their birth and origin / in

<meta>catchword: in</meta>

in their life and in their qualities and inclinations. The Outer Man is born first in the womb; but the Inner is **infused** <note>Infusion refers to the theological belief that God "pours" the soul or spiritual essence into the physical body.</note> / after the former is endowed with life or made living. The outer is born through the work of natural generation / by natural parents; the Inner through the work of infusion / in a supernatural way of generation. The Inner has his origin from above / from the inner-eternal world of **Paradise**; the Outer is from below / from the nature of the spirit of this world. The outer is a visible man / lives visibly; eats / drinks / walks and works visibly / in a visible form and body in this visible world: the Inner Man is an invisible one / who hides himself within the Outer / and cannot be seen; who lives / walks and works invisibly; in an invisible form / in an invisible (yet substantial) body / inside the outer man / in this visible creation. And because he cannot be seen through the outer senses of any man / he is called the **invisible man**. The Outer is a mortal, corruptible man; the Inner an immortal and incorruptible one / formed from the eternal ground of the one / pure **Holy Element**. The Outer is a worldly and earthly man; the Inner a Divine and Heavenly man; once he has been awakened within himself to his true life. The Outer or external is not the man of the **Fall** / he remains in the state in which he was created; but the Inner is the fallen man / as one who has fallen from Paradise into this miserable and sorrowful world. The Outer is an **elemental man** <note>An "elemental man" refers to the physical body composed of the four changing elements of the material world (earth, water, air, fire).</note> / born and composed from the conflict or adversity of the corrupted elements of this world; but the inner man / in regard to his inward body / is formed from the four Eternal Elements / as they / through the hand of **Wisdom** <term>Wisdom: Or "Sophia," a personification of Divine Wisdom common in the works of Jacob Böhme and his followers.</term> / are brought into a harmony and equal temperament /

<summary>This page explains that while the inner and outer man coexist in one body like twins in a womb, they have fundamentally different origins: the outer man is born of nature and the elements, while the inner man is a spiritual being infused from Paradise.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the distinction between the "inner" and "outer" man, transitioning from their dual nature to their tripartite composition of body, soul, and spirit, and how this relates to the visible and invisible worlds.</meta>

<page-num>202</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Little Treatise on Union</header>

...are / which is called the one Holy Element. The **Outer Man** is an **animal-man** / and has / like an animal / limbs of natural generation; the **Inner Man** is a **Masculine Virgin** <note>This term, common in Jacob Böhme’s theosophy, refers to a spiritual state of perfection that transcends biological sex, containing both masculine and feminine spiritual principles in harmony.</note> / who has the male and female within himself / and propagates his own likeness in a magical and spiritual way. The outer is therefore called a fleshly man; the inner, however, a spiritual one: the one performs all things in a lowly and fleshly way; the other in a high and spiritual way. The outer man eats or lives from the creatures of this visible world; the inner from the inflows and outflows of the eternal element of Paradise. The outer is the weak man / as one who is subject to all the buffeting of the four divided winds of this world / and the radiations and influences of the poisonous constellations of the stars; and thus stands in need of the help of the least of creatures: The Inner is the strong man / as one who is subject to none of these / but is higher and above them all; as a man of the **One Element** <note>The "One Element" or Quintessence was believed by alchemists to be the pure, incorruptible substance of Paradise, unlike the four conflicting elements of our fallen world.</note>. The outer finally / as the man of the earth / is made subject to earthly powers: the inner, however, is subject to no one on earth / but to God himself alone; no man / no king / no emperor / can tax him; no one but God can call him to account. This one / because he is a paradisiacal man / eats and lives from the fruits of Paradise / which grow forth within himself / without any toil or labor; he does not worry or think about what he shall eat or drink / or with what he shall clothe himself; he is subject to no toil or labor / by whatever name it may be called / for Paradise itself provides the inner man with all things. In this manner and through these properties, it is easy to make a distinction between the Inner and Outer man; provided that anyone should otherwise be so blind as to confound or mix them together.

18. Now we must proceed to the **Number Three**. Wherein you must know and earnestly consider /

<page-num>203</page-num>
<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures etc.</header>
<meta>catchword: stead</meta>

that these two distinct men within us / namely the Inner and the Outer / are each composed of three: I mean of a body, soul, and spirit. I say the Outer man is composed of the number Three / and consists of a body / soul and spirit / which belong to himself; and are distinct from the inner man. And again I say / that the inner is also composed of a body / soul and spirit / which belong to himself / and are distinct from the outer man’s body / soul and spirit. And in this way, man (I mean by this the whole man) comprises all worlds within himself / and carries them about him in his own fleshly body; for which reason he may in truth be called the **Man of all worlds**. For he has something within himself / that relates to all visible and invisible worlds; whereby he may attain to the knowledge of all worlds / and may grasp and understand their wonders within himself: according to how he shall search into the mystery of this number Three / externally and internally.

19. The outer visible man / who lives in the body of flesh / belongs to this outer visible world: and is the man of this world. Who, through the **spirit of reason** / which is implanted in him / is capable and fit to recognize and understand all the wonders of this visible creation; all the wonders of God in the heavens above / all the wonders of God here below on the earth / and all his wonders in the deep / and in subterranean caverns. This is as it were the **vulture’s eye** <insert>(a) Job cap. 28 v. 7.</insert> <note>Job 28:7 refers to a path which "the vulture's eye hath not seen." Here, the author uses it to describe the keen but limited power of human reason which sees the physical world but is blind to the spiritual.</note> in man / which is given to him to see through everything that is in nature within this visible out-birth.

20. But the inner man / who is invisible / and lives secretly and hiddenly within the outer / belongs to the inner and invisible worlds: and is the man of those same worlds and creations. For he has the essence of the invisible worlds within himself; and is composed of both eternal darkness and eternal light. And because he is thus—

...manner, because he has the root of both the eternal dark fire and the eternal light-fire within himself, he is capable—by means of his own eternal soul—of recognizing and grasping all the wonders of both the inner dark world and also the light- and paradise-world, which is his own birthright and fatherland. And through the power of this ascending spirit, which is centered and implanted in his Eternal Mind, he is capable and fit to recognize and grasp all the wonders of God, and all deep and hidden things of the Godhead in the eternal world; which otherwise would be impossible for the inner eternal man to recognize and grasp.

22. In such a way, man is to himself a **wonder-man**. And if man would only recognize himself within himself, he would see and recognize that he is a wonderful man; and he would recognize that he is composed of all God's wonders together. If man would only recognize himself within himself, he would recognize himself to be a great and deep mystery, which is composed of all the mysteries of God: and that he is a secret man within himself. And if man would only recognize himself within himself, he would recognize himself to be a **Small World** <note>original: "Kleine Welt" — This refers to the concept of the Microcosm, the idea that the human being contains the entire universe in miniature.</note>, indeed, a man of the worlds: for all worlds are in man—namely, the Eternal World in the Eternal Spirit, which has its seat in the Eternal Mind and Will; the inward world in the eternal essences of the eternal soul, which has its seat, as it were, in the heart; and the outer world in the outer man.

In this way you see that this secret "three," repeated, makes only two men: the Inward and the Outward; and these two appear as only one man. Because the Inward and eternal man is in the Outward and temporal man; and lives, moves, and works in him: and yet, even so, the one is not the other, though he is hidden in the other; but they are truly and essentially distinct, as has been said.

## Chapter II.
### Concerning the Creation of the Outer Man; with his Body, Soul, and Spirit.
## The First Day’s Work
### The Preceding Preparations.

<margin>1.</margin>The outer man, when considered as he stands in his own state and in his own line of Outer Nature, is a **wonderful man**; he is a **Wonder-Man**, whether you consider him in his birth, or in the way and manner of his creation, or in the manifold workings, properties, powers, and wonders that arise from him. If you consider only his body in its creation and formation—in the various parts belonging to it, in their number, their order, their position, their figure, their size, and their connection—is not the outer body of the outer man itself also a **Wonder-Body**? <margin>(b) Psalm 139 v 14.</margin> Regarding this structure of the body belonging to the outer man, Saint David says: *I am wonderfully made* (b). Furthermore, if you consider him in the creation of his soul, he is an even greater Wonder-Man; in this, he is made fearful and wonderful. There is nothing in the visible body that may be compared with the subtle sources of the soulful life; but especially and primarily, if you consider him in the creation of his spirit, he is in truth a Wonder-Man. For through his spirit, which is centered and implanted in his mind <note>The original German term "Gemüthe" refers to the seat of the emotions, will, and intellect—the inner heart or "disposition."</note>, he is fit to recognize all the wonders of God in outer nature; and everything that is in this visible creature may be opened to him.

<margin>2.</margin>And in this way, even the outer man is a won-

<sig>O 2</sig>

-der <note>The word "Wunder" (Wonder) is split across the page break in the original German.</note> born in and of himself; and he is the Wonder-Man in this world. He is born the great Monarch and Emperor of this world, and is its sole regent and ruler under the Spirit of the World <note>original: "Geiste der Welt"; likely referring to the *Spiritus Mundi*, the animating force of the physical cosmos.</note>; and thus he reigns and rules over all worlds within this world, such as over the vegetable, animal, and mineral kingdoms therein. He grasps the creatures, and is grasped by none, and ruled by none. His dominion extends over the birds of the air, over the fish of the sea, and over the creeping things of the earth; indeed, it even extends over the outer elements themselves. He also grasps and understands the order of the starry heavens and searches out how he might protect himself from their poisonous influences. This world is his kingdom, the creatures within it are his subjects, its riches are his inheritance; the world with all its fullness is his birthright, and this globe of earth is the land of his birth. In this manner, not only the inner man but also the outer man himself—under all considerations—is a wonderful man, or a man of wonders.

<margin>3.</margin>Now, let us proceed to consider the wonderful creation of this outer man in the precise union of his body, soul, and spirit, as they essentially and inwardly belong to him. This outer Wonder-Man is not born in the full stature and size of a man; rather, he comes into this visible world out of the body or womb as a small child. He is born—according to body, soul, and spirit—through natural birth and propagation; and thus his life and essence are communicated and propagated from parents to parents, gradually, by descent <note>original: "ex Traduce"; a Latin theological term for 'traducianism,' the belief that the soul is generated by the parents rather than created anew by God for each person.</note>, according to the order of this visible creation.

...must be an active and a passive / a male and a female. <margin>5.</margin> But one might ask here / from what then do these unions arise? To which I answer / that they are worked out through the earnest longing and heavy drawing or desire / which one tincture has for the other / which lies hidden in the seed of the Temporal and Outer Nature. For in the tincture of the male / and also in the tincture of the female / there is a strong magnetic or attractive power / which draws them one to the other / so that they may thus become one flesh / one nature / yes, to a certain degree, even as one person.

<margin>6.</margin> This tincture is called the outer tincture / because it belongs alone to the outer man. And such lies and has its root in the seed: in the male it is the masculine / and in the female the feminine tincture. Now the masculine tincture is of the masculine property; and has its influence from the planet **Mars**, which belongs to this world / with a strong fiery property: but the feminine is influenced by the property of the planet **Venus**, and is qualified with a gentle watery property. These two / when they come together / wrestle in / with and

through one another for the upper hand: and whichever then maintains the upper hand / according to that one the outer man receives the sign of distinction in the outer birth. If the Masculine Tincture obtains the upper hand / then it becomes a male: but if the Feminine / a female.

<margin>7.</margin> Now from the union of the tinctures arises the union of the bodies / and the union of the persons; and out of two they become one flesh. Here consider further / how from this union of the tinctures the union of the seeds and natures arises: because it is impossible to unite the tinctures without the union of the seeds and natures; considering there is such a close relationship of the one with the other; since they are essentially linked with one another; and / regardless of their difference / they essentially penetrate each other as one: so that the tincture is not without the seed; nor is the seed without the tincture. And they are thus placed into one another / according to the order of the **Spirit of this World** <note>The "Spirit of this World" (Spiritus Mundi) was a common alchemical term for the vital soul of the universe that bridges the gap between the physical and the spiritual.</note>; which cannot be broken.

<margin>8.</margin> In such a way / I say / the union of the seeds necessarily arises from the union of the tinctures; and from the union of the seeds, the union of the natures. For the seed is the nature itself: and the curdling together of both seeds into one is called a union of the natures. It is therefore impossible to have a union of the tinctures without the union of the seeds; it is impossible to have a union of the bodies without the union of the persons. For their bodies are in their persons; and the tinctures / seeds / and natures are in their bodies. And this is the wise order / and unchangeable connection / that exists between the union of the tinctures / seeds / natures / bodies and persons; which proceeds from the **Spirit of this World** / who is the wise ordainer and ruler of the same / under God.

...this [pertains to] the outer soul—the soul that belongs to the outer man and woman. In truth, it is a miserable, lowly, and vain paradise; it is like the **crackling of thorns under a pot**, <note>A reference to Ecclesiastes 7:6: "For as the crackling of thorns under a pot, so is the laughter of the fool: this also is vanity." 10. And for this reason, he has implanted a strong, burning flame of desire into the <term>Tinctures: Vital spiritual essences that animate physical matter.</term> and <term>Seeds: The underlying potential or "blueprint" within nature.</term> of <term>Nature: The physical world and its biological laws.</term> within his children—namely, into the <term>Seeds</term> of their souls. From these flames, implanted into the <term>Seeds</term> of <term>Nature</term>, that which we properly call **Lust** is born. Because of the delight that accompanies it, it is known in this world by the name of **Love**. However, it serves here to remind us that the root of these burning flames of fleshly love, implanted into the <term>Tinctures</term>, exists because they are impregnated with these qualities by the influences of <term>Mars: The planet associated with heat, drive, and masculine energy.</term> and <term>Venus: The planet associated with beauty, attraction, and feminine energy.</term> in the starry heavens.

11. These burning desires, so essentially implanted into the <term>Tinctures</term> and <term>Seeds</term> of the soul, are awakened by beautiful outer objects. From the awakening of the <term>Tinctures</term> flow the most fervent and powerful attractions of one person toward another. When some are drawn together in this way by the mutual desire of the <term>Tinctures</term>, they thereby draw the senses of one another; and from this drawing or attraction arises a pleasure of one for the other. From this, in turn, comes their revelation of one to the other through the communal **Union** they create between them, according to the law and freedom of the lower <term>Nature</term>—that is, according to the strong drawing of the <term>Tinctures</term> which they have toward each other in their inner <term>Seeds</term> or natures.

12. And if it happens thus according to the law of <term>Nature</term>, then it is done according to the will of their father—that is, according to the will of the **Spirit of this World**. For his will is always aligned with the will of <term>Nature</term>, and his will is within the will of <term>Nature</term> (namely, the outer <term>Nature</term>). He is constantly revealed through this sensual drawing, so that everything done through the pulling power of the lower <term>Nature</term>, in and through these natures, is brought about or done by this Spirit.

Their inclinations and promptings occur in and through the drawing of the <term>Tinctures</term>, whereby they reveal and explain the will of the Spirit of this World. In this manner, he is the **Great Generator** <note>The "Generator" is the one who brings forth or procreates; here, the Spirit of the World is seen as the father of all physical life.</note> and Father of the outer man, whose fatherly care is revealed in this: that the sons of the <term>Tinctures</term> meet one another. In this way, he is the Father, the Giver, and the High Priest of this communal **Union** of natures, essences, <term>Tinctures</term>, and <term>Seeds</term>.

14. Under him, **Kings** and **Emperors** <note>original: "Keyser"</note> are, as it were, his subjects; they must rule, govern, and make laws under him. In this way, the outer man becomes subject to the Spirit of the World and to the kings and all higher powers under him, who make wholesome and rational laws. These laws obligate every man to have his own wife and every woman her own husband—especially since nothing but confusion and disorder would arise in this visible world if a man were allowed to take many wives;

15. Just as the outer man and woman stand subject to the Spirit of this world as their regent, so it is certainly fitting for them to submit to all His rational laws and ordinances. These are imposed upon them by Him for their own benefit and outward happiness. Furthermore, just as every outer man and woman stands subject to Him as the **High Steward** over the world under God—governing the same through rational laws—so they also without doubt ought to be subject to all officials under Him, whether it be to the **King** as the highest, or to all other lower authorities, by whatever name, title, or dignity they are distinguished, in all places and through all nations of this visible world. And they are bound to obey all those good and rational laws which are imposed upon their **old man** <note>The "old man" is a Pauline term (Romans 6:6) referring to the unregenerate, physical, or fallen nature of a human being.</note> by the high authority for the sake of outward order, and no less for their own benefit and preservation as well as that of the community.

16. Thus, alongside the inner draw of the <term>Tinctures: The subtle, spiritual qualities or "colors" of the soul that drive attraction.</term> (in which the inner essentiality of all these unions and joinings consists), they must also submit to the **superadditional** <note>Meaning "added on top of" the natural/spiritual attraction.</note> or added bond of the priest in a public manner, whereby their personal union is made known to the world. Which, when it—

17. And after the actual completion of this personal union of two persons to become one, there arises immediately a new kinship between them from the Spirit of this world and his subordinate official, which is the kinship of husband and wife.

And the law to do so is grounded in the **Rationality** of the Spirit of the world, the Grandfather of all parents, who sees high cause, justice, and equity therein. And those who act contrary to the rationality of this law will feel a "too late" repentance within themselves for their deed.

## Chapter III.
### On the natural procreation and propagation of the external man.
### The Second Day's Work:
### The essential principles or seeds.

1. We shall now proceed to the next step/ which leads us to the natural procreation and propagation of the external man; which consists in the work of procreation itself: everything said until now has only been preparatory for this. For this, there must be two distinct persons/ each of whom is in themselves pregnant with their own **tincture**: <term>Tincture: In this context, a "tincture" is a vital spiritual essence or a "coloring" of the soul that gives life and specific qualities to the physical body.</term> and so that they might not be unchaste or impure/ these two must, through unanimous consent, each oversee their own person and betroth themselves to live together as property for such and such ends. Then they stand before God and the spirit of this world in a true matrimonial relationship toward one another. For as far as the added public declaration of the same/ by the priest and the consent of the parents is concerned; the first is only for the sake of the government/ whose subjects they are; and the latter only for the well-being of their close union/ not for the essence itself. But all the essential belongings of this matrimonial bond to make them man and wife/ are truly and effectively already done beforehand.

2. Now as for the matrimonial work itself/ without which no natural procreation can occur;

<meta>so</meta>

---

<page-num>215</page-num>
<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures etc. 215</header>

so this consists properly in the tinctures: and the most noble and inner-essential belonging is the mixing of the same: from which/ if all other things cooperate in the orderly course of nature/ an external child will undoubtedly be born in due time. There are several necessary and special circumstances to be considered in the mixing of the tinctures/ and seeds of nature; which are:
> I. What these seeds are?
> II. What their mixing with one another is?
> III. What is hidden and contained in this mixing?
> IV. What the cause of the formation is?

3. We now begin with the first of these points. These seeds are the essential principles or beginnings of generation/ from which the child is produced/ and without which there would be no matter for its bringing forth. And just as these seeds are absolutely necessary for the materiality of the child; so is their intimate union just as necessary for its generation. Their nature is/ that they are procreative and generating/ and by their constitution they are spirit-filled bodies or beings. By spirit-filled bodies I mean a matter full of living spirits/ which is pregnant with natural spirits/ and is qualified in itself with a fruitful or generative power and capacity.

4. Here, however, it may not be unhelpful to search a little for the creator of these generating seeds. And to look to a higher cause than the natural parents are. Those who follow the philosophy of **Plato** <note>original: "Platonis"</note> wish to point us toward the spirit of the world: and tell us/ that the soul or the spirit of this world is such a spirit-filled **substance** <note>original: "Substanz"</note>, and a spirit so full of living spirits/ that it spreads itself through the whole world; and that this is the generating spirit/ the creator, author, and worker both of the seed-vessels/ as well as what is contained therein. I acknowledge the natural parents to be the immediate and next-following

...to be the generating cause: but he it is/ who forms the **seed-vessels** in them/ <note>In the author's worldview, these "seed-vessels" are both the physical organs and the spiritual structures required for procreation.</note> and after he has formed them/ also immediately fills them.

But because He is the **First Cause**/ so has He also created the Spirit of this World beneath Him/ that he might be as it were God’s official or representative over the visible world; and has therefore/ together with his subordinate planetary regents/ qualified and empowered Him with such influences and effluences/ <note>These "effluences" are the spiritual energies believed to flow down from the stars and planets into the physical world.</note> which are suited to such ends and purposes.

6. Notwithstanding this, it remains always in the power of the Spirit of Eternity to bind and to loose these influences and effluences from the Spirit of the World with all its stellar movements/ as and according to how it is well-pleasing to Him/ in His wisdom and mercy. And in such a way God makes a barren woman/ so that she becomes a fruitful mother of children/ and also the contrary: through the opening or closing and withholding of the generating influences of the aforementioned Spirit in His upper world. He moves in and through this Spirit of the World as it pleases Him; and when it pleases Him/ then He withdraws Himself into His own **still eternity**. <note>The "still eternity" refers to God's state of being apart from the active, moving world of time and matter.</note> So long/ however/ as the Spirit of Eternity does not wish to withhold His ordinary governing rule Himself and thereby show a miracle; so one must always hold that the steady course of nature is His good pleasure. Through

<column-break/>

a **miracle**/ however/ I understand a deed/ which is above and outside the usual or ordinary government of the Spirit of this world.

7. In such a way we actually see/ that there are many generators of these seeds/ one over the other/ and one subordinate to another/ and without the slightest confusion or disorder. The most immediate and lowest of the authors of these generating seeds are the man and the woman in subjection under the Spirit of the World/ and as lower tools in His hand. The other more distant main and **active cause** is the Spirit of this World/ as the most noble instrumental agent in the hand of the Spirit of Eternity. And so we ascend step by step to the Highest Agent and first origin/ that is/ to God Himself.

8. This was why the **Patriarchs** of old <note>The "Patriarchs" refers to the biblical fathers like Abraham and Isaac.</note> (and every Christian and true believer today should also do the same) in cases of infertility took their refuge in God/ as the highest Active Cause of both the one and the other. Thus although **Sarah** was already 90 years old/ and her womb was barren/ God nevertheless blessed her and made her fruitful. Likewise **Rebekah** was also barren; but **Isaac** entreated the LORD for his wife/ because she was barren: He did not seek the Spirit of this world/ nor the influences of the outer and stellar heavens. No: He made use of the right way. For/ as it is written/ He entreated the LORD for his wife; and the LORD let himself be entreated by him and Rebekah his wife became pregnant. And Rebekah found two twins struggling together in her womb/ what did she do then? She went there to ask the LORD for counsel.

9. Thus we also/ in similar cases/ must seek counsel and help not from the lower and other causes/ in respect of the man and woman/ nor even from the Spirit of the World with his stellar influences; but must go through these subordinate tools to God Himself/ who (as these examples show us) is the highest Active Cause and the first mover.

## Chapter IV.
## The Third Day's Work.
### Of the Natural and Philosophical Mixing of Seeds.

1. The next required circumstance is to explain nature; how the seeds penetrate one another, without which no natural generation can ever occur. Nothing is more certain than that both **seed-tinctures** <note>In the alchemical tradition, a "tincture" is a spiritualized essence or "dye" that can penetrate and transform matter; here, it refers to the vital life-force within male and female seeds.</note> must necessarily be mixed with one another and must curdle together by means of an inward penetration of one by the other, if the external man is to be brought forth into this visible world. Of which Saint Job speaks thus:
<margin>Chapter 10 v 10.</margin>
> Did You not pour me out like milk and curdle me like cheese?

2. Now, after these have been united with one mind and thereby curdled together into a lump, through the affection implanted in them by the spirit of this world, they figure and thicken themselves in the **womb** <term>Behrmutter: literally "bearing-mother," the historical German term for the uterus.</term> of the woman, as if in an **oven**: so that they may be kept secure, preserved, and nourished therein until they come forth in the form of a perfect child. For here there is now nothing else to do but to seal the womb with the **Seal of Hermes** <note>original: "Siegel Hermetis." This refers to an airtight, hermetic seal used in alchemy to prevent the escape of vital vapors. Here, the author suggests that nature itself "seals" the womb after conception.</note>: and in truth, the womb actually closes itself. Now the seeds themselves come to life and form themselves by their own power into a body of flesh, a living soul, and a life-giving spirit.

3. There is no more work or art required from either the father of the male seed, nor from the mother of the female na-
<meta>catchword: ture /</meta>

---

<page-num>219</page-num>
<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc.</header>

ture, other than to pour them together; and after they are poured together, to let them mix and mingle with one another; and after this has happened, to set them together in their proper place, namely the womb, and let them do their own generating work therein. Let them be nourished and maintained in their own **glass oven**, sealed with the Seal of Hermes, with a steady fire day and night, with a heat of equal temperature from the **Natural and Central Fire** <note>The "Central Fire" was believed by alchemists to be the internal heat of the Earth or the human body that drives all biological and chemical transformations.</note> and warmth of the womb: thus, at the end of the ninth month, a young living child will come forth, who will break the oven and bring himself forth to the joy of the father and the mother. Now you see a New Kinship: and therefore the man and woman are given a new name; for as soon as the child is born, he is called a father and she a mother.

4. You, therefore, who are those noble **Philosophers** <note>The author is addressing "alchemists," who were often called "natural philosophers" or simply "philosophers" in this period.</note> seeking the external tincture and the external seed which is to be the matter of the **External Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, which was believed to be able to transform "imperfect" base metals into "perfect" gold.</note>, and which shall transform and make perfect the imperfect metals; allow me to present to you this mixing of seeds in the womb of the mother as a figure or pattern. I believe that there is an external Stone belonging to this external world which may be made by art to transform external metals. But here, a true process is placed before your eyes.

5. Only recognize the seeds and matter of your Stone. Recognize your little man and your little woman, your husband and your wife, your King and Queen: and recognize the seeds of your Stone, whether they be generative seeds or of a generating ability; and not dead seeds, but rather impregnated with a living spirit and tincture: the male seed with the male tincture, and the female with the female. For where your seeds do not have their living tinctures within themselves, you can never generate.

6. But then, if your seed is a generating—

...is a **tinging seed** <note>original: "tingirender Saame"; in alchemy, "tinging" refers to the power of a substance to penetrate and transform the nature of another, much like a dye.</note> / so it unites them with one another in number, weight, and measure: thus you will see them flow into one another, impregnating each other with great joy and desire / and merging into a single clotted lump.

7. At that point, recognize your true **womb** <term>Behrmutter: literally "bearing-mother," an archaic term for the uterus.</term> and **Matrix**, by which I mean your true glass furnace / your Philosophical furnace: and seal it with the **seal of secrecy** <note>Likely referring to the "Seal of Hermes," a method of airtight sealing (hermetic sealing) to prevent the escape of the spiritual "vapors" during the process.</note>. And leave them therein / so that they may be nourished by their own central fire from within / and by the Philosophical fire from without: and let them remain together in their bed of union; thus they will become one and, in their own proper time, they will form the Stone itself / without any external help / and present the same to you / to your great joy.

8. The Philosopher must know all these secrets: and if he lacks even one / the Stone will turn out as a miscarriage. This is what makes the formation of the Stone so difficult / and why there are so many miscarriages even among the Philosophers themselves. If they knew these circumstances / it would be as easy to form and complete the **red stone** <note>The "Red Stone" or *Lapis Ruber* is the final, most potent stage of the Philosopher's Stone, capable of transmuting metals into gold and acting as a universal medicine.</note> / as it is for a clumsy peasant to help a maid have a child. Just give him a maid / and through the mutual union of their bodies, without any academic learning or instruction / they will bring forth a child. They will make no secret of bringing forth a secret / which is just as great / and greater / than the formation of the Philosophical Stone is to any Philosopher.

9. For one would marvel more over this work of the generation or procreation of a child than over the greatest work in the world / that one could bring forth an image equal to oneself / if it were not so common; but because it is so common / and happens every day / and because people know the way and manner of the formation of this natural wonder; it is held to be almost no wonder at all. If, however, the matter / and the place and the mixing of the seeds with one another / were not known / or were kept <column-break/>

secret and hidden among only a few; then one would, without doubt, marvel at the formation of a child with the highest astonishment.

10. Likewise, if the Stone itself / its seeds and its matter / as well as its furnace / its fire / and its mixings / in number, weight, and measure were commonly known; it would be regarded as a common recipe / and held in as little esteem / as the formation of a child in a mother's womb.

11. It is quite certain / that there is an external transforming stone / which belongs to this visible creation: and it is therefore indisputable / that the living generating seed or **sperma** in the Vegetable, Mineral, and Animal kingdoms is to be found by the true Philosophers. However, in extracting the seed through fire from plants / animals and minerals, the generating property is destroyed and lost. At that point / you Philosophers, your labor is in vain.

12. There is a certain and closer way: which is this / that you take your living seeds / with their tinging, generating power, out of the **Matrix** and womb of the one pure Holy Element / and from the dust of paradise; I mean from the **Paradisiacal Earth**: thus you may, without any artificial fire, extract your living seeds with their generative and procreative **tinctures** <note>Life-essences</note> therefrom. Then give them (if you will) to a wise Philosopher, and he will show you their use.

13. But who shall this wise Philosopher be? Do you wish to entrust the matter / which is extracted from the ground of Paradise / to another / than yourself? Therefore you must know / that your own Philosopher is within yourself: He is your brother; it is your inward, eternal paradise-man. He / yes, He alone / is the wise Philosopher. This wise Philosopher of yours must be **born again** <note>A reference to the "New Birth" or spiritual regeneration, a central theme in Böhmean and Pietist alchemy where the soul must be restored to its original state.</note>: and after the work of his New or second birth is completed / then you must send him into paradise; for he knows the <meta>catchword: "way" (Weg)</meta>

# Chapter V.
## The Fourth Work-Day.
### Concerning the Causes of Formation.

1. Now that the seeds have congealed together / and the **tinctures** <note>original: "Tincturen"; in alchemy, these are the spiritual essences or "souls" of substances that have the power to transform or "tinge" matter.</note> have been mixed into a mass or lump / and thus left enclosed and firmly sealed together in the mother’s **womb** <term>Behrmutter: literally "bearing-mother," an archaic term for the uterus.</term> to nourish one another / and to do the work that they have to do with and among one another; it follows / that we should next consider within ourselves what may be hidden and contained in these mixtures and congealing of the **generative** seeds / or seeds suitable for procreation / which are thus enclosed in the womb of their mother. To this I say / that therein, in the mixture of the seeds, are hidden the secret tinctures / and the hidden soul with all its effects. They are all enclosed in their seeds that have been mingled together.

<column-break/>

<page-num>223</page-num>
<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc.</header>
<sig>P 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

2. And so that we may speak even more clearly and in greater detail / it must be / by virtue of these secret and hidden tinctures / and the active life and soul enclosed therein / that everything must necessarily lie hidden in the mixed seeds which is afterwards brought to appearance or into the light. It must be contained within them in the manner of potential and power: first the body / the flesh and bones; the number and order / the placement / the shapes of the parts; with the size and proportion of the matter: and the soul with its tincture and life / and with all its formations and effects; and finally with its belonging spirit / and also with all its effects; and all other such things / which are afterwards found in the outer man / after he is brought forth into the outer world.

3. In this way we see what lies hidden in those mixed seeds / and what comes forth from those seeds / when they are mixed and preserved in the proper manner / namely a body composed of flesh and blood / with all its belonging parts; also a living soul / which moves with life through each and every limb of the body; together with a living spirit / which moves and works in the soul as its ruler. Thus body / soul / and spirit come from the mixing of the seeds in the womb: for they are actually hidden and contained in the mixed seeds / and proceed from them / as if from their material cause / from which they take their origin. And the origin of the seeds themselves comes / as you know / from their parents / in the course of common nature / with the addition of the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Geist der Welt"; also known as the *Spiritus Mundi*, a theoretical universal substance or energy believed by alchemists to permeate all things.</note> / and the heavenly influences / which pregnant the seeds / after they are mixed / with a generative power.

4. According to this, let us search further into what the hidden thing in the mixed seeds might be / which is actually the formative or creative cause of the mixed seeds into a body of flesh with its...

parts; and into a living soul and spirit? For the mixed seeds are only the matter; but we find through experience that a formative power and ability is hidden within them; and that this is the original and immediate formative cause of these united seeds into a specific body, soul, and spirit. If one were to ask further, who then planted this formative and active power into the united and mixed seeds, so as to bring forth a visible body, a living soul, and an active spirit, so that it might work in soul and body? Here, all **Human Philosophy** with its wisdom lies prostrate; and is indeed blind. What then is to be done? Well then: if you search further for who the foremost Worker of this formative power and shape-giving strength is, which is hidden within them, then you must lead me to God Himself, who is the Creator of this formative power and might, and who planted the same into the seeds during the First Creation of the world, as we **Christian Philosophers** <note>original: "Christliche Philosophi"</note> read in *Genesis 1, verses 11 and 12*. There it is said of the plants that they had the **vegetative** <note>original: "Vegetabilische"</note> life, or their own shape-giving form or preservation and growth, or the **principle** <note>original: "Principium"</note> of **vegetation** <term>Vegetation: In this alchemical context, it refers to the natural power of growth and reproduction inherent in all living things, often compared to the way a "stone" or "tincture" grows in the laboratory.</term> in their own seed. And in the 21st and 22nd verses, that all creatures should be fruitful and each should multiply after its kind; for the animal life was planted into their seeds. And so it is with man also: when man begets a man, the shape-giving power is actually planted in the seed: which in *Genesis 2, verse 24*—though not exactly in those explicit words, yet not unclearly—is indicated: The man shall cleave unto his wife: For the generating and shape-giving power is in the seed; and it is the Divine blessing within it, which was given to him in the first creation, *Genesis 1, verse 28*. Adam knew this after his fall, and actually did it; just as it is expressed in a very chaste manner in *Genesis 4, verse 1*: And

<meta>catchword: Adam</meta>

Adam knew his wife, and she was pregnant; which sufficiently proves that the **efficient cause** <term>Wirck-Ursache: A philosophical term referring to the primary source or agent that brings something into being.</term> actually lies hidden in the seed.

5. This fruitful and increasing and multiplying power in the seed of living creatures was expressly confirmed by God Himself after the **Great Flood** <note>original: "Sündfluth"</note>, so that it should remain in the seed, *Genesis 8, verse 17*: And bring out with you (from the ark) every living creature etc. that they may breed abundantly on the earth, and be fruitful and multiply. And again we read concerning man in *Genesis 9, verse 1*: And God blessed Noah and his sons, and said to them: Be fruitful and multiply and fill the earth. Thus we have now found that God Himself is the highest Worker of this propagating and multiplying power of the forming and generating ability; who initially, through an everlasting law of nature in the first creation, planted and fixed it into the mixed seeds.

6. Now that we have seen the first cause of this hidden multiplying power, let us proceed to examine the next foremost and **instrumental cause** <term>werckzeugliche Ursach: The means or "tool" through which a higher power acts to produce an effect.</term> of the same under God, the universal Creator, Father, and Cause of all causes. The office of this great instrumental cause is to maintain and impregnate the formative or shape-giving power in the seed. And this is commanded by God to the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Geiste der Welt"; often referred to in alchemy as the *Anima Mundi*, the vital spirit that connects the divine will to physical matter.</note>, whom He has for that purpose also appropriately qualified and made capable. Accordingly, by him—as God's foremost instrument in the governance of all natural generations—the generating power has been poured out into all plants and animals or living creatures, but most of all it is to be wondered at in the children of men.

...multiplied influences of the **World Spirit** <note>original: "Welt-Geist"; in alchemical thought, this is the "Anima Mundi" or a subtle substance that bridges the gap between the spiritual and material realms</note> and is maintained and preserved through the external starry heavens of this world; so you bring me down even further, finally and thirdly to investigate what the immediate instrumental causes of the same are. Here, however, there is no difficulty in recognizing which are the immediate and lower instruments, once the mixed seeds from both parents come together in union. So that, whether you look upward or downward, you will always find that the mixed seeds of both persons are the matter of the body, soul, and spirit; and not only that, but also the immediate cause of the formative power within them. The nearest cause of this, however, is the **Spirit of the World** with its **Astral outpourings** <note>original: "Astrolischen Ausflüssen"; refers to the belief that stars emit "influences" that shape earthly life</note>, which are filled with form-giving spirits: and then, closest above Him, the Creator himself. And if you wish to go from the top downward, you bring me again from the Creator of all things to the Spirit of this world, and thus through Him to the natural parents, as working causes under Him, just as He is under the Creator of all formations and effects, which are worked out and brought forth from the seeds themselves.

8. They stand in such a way in subordination under one another, not in contradiction one against the other. They are not outside one another; and yet each is far distinguished from the other; and yet without the slightest confusion and disorder. And why should man separate these causes, which are thus united by the Creator, and are bound together in subjection and sub-ordering of the one under the other?

9. Since, therefore, all these causes are bound together in proper sub-ordering by the wise Creator and Former of all things; so the **Philosopher** <note>original: "Philosophus"</note>, who searches for God in the course of **natural nature** <note>original: "natürliche Natur"; refers to the physical world governed by natural laws as established by God</note>, says with truth and propriety: **Man and the Sun beget man** <note>original: "Homo & Sol generant hominem"; a famous phrase from Aristotle often used in alchemy to show that human life requires both biological parents and the cosmic energy of the sun</note>, that is, man and the sun produce another human being, who is like him-

<sig>P 4</sig>

-self. This is asserted, I say, by those **Philosophers** who search for God in the course of **natural nature**; which is the nature that belongs to this visible creation; and is called by them the "natural nature"; that is, that nature which arises or proceeds from the **Spirit of the World**, in an ordinary course and line of nature, as we say: this is what they understand by the "natural man."

10. This **natural Philosopher** views man as the lowest instrument: and yet as an instrument, because the form- or shape-giving power is in his seed. And alongside this, he joins to him the **Sun**, as it is the great planet in the hand of the **Spirit of the World**, and includes within it all the remaining planets and powers of nature. And because man and the sun are here joined together, it is bright and clear that it must be so understood; and that he actually and necessarily establishes this his union with the sun, or with the Spirit of the World, as the foundation, so that man, as an instrument in His hand, is driven or worked by Him as it pleases Him; or he considers them both as united instruments in the hand of God, so that man is the lower and weaker, and the Spirit of the World in the Sun has the upper hand and is higher and more powerful.

11. For you must not think that this wise **Philosopher** who speaks thus is either an atheist or a heathen. For he does not exclude God, the high and exalted Spirit of Eternity, as if He were not the First and highest cause of all things; even though he only mentions the most prominent instrument in the hand of the Creator. Perhaps the Philosopher has within himself higher and deeper, yes, even nobler thoughts of God the Creator and the Spirit of Eternity than you yourself. However, in seeking the origin of all things in natural nature, and finding the **Spirit of the World** in God—and under God—to be the first and highest instrument, which immediately follows Him in the world as...

<summary>This page describes a hierarchy of creation where the "Spirit of the World" and the Sun act as celestial intermediaries between God (the First Cause) and human parents (the immediate cause) in the formation of new life. It emphasizes that the biological "seed" contains a formative power that is guided by these higher cosmic influences.</summary>

# Chapter VI.
## The Fifth Day's Work
### On the First Formation and Animation of the Fruit.

1. Now that the form- and shape-giving power / which exists in the seeds / has been revealed / one might further ask: to whose special direction does it actually belong to order those formations and effects? To which the answer serves / that such will clearly be revealed in the manner and way of the incarnation of the child; and therefore we have now come to speak in an orderly fashion of the incarnation of man / and of the process of the same in the <term>womb</term> <note>original: "Behrmutter"; literally "bearing-mother"</note>. The external <term>astrologer</term> <note>original: "Astrologus"</note> of this visible world would perhaps do it more precisely / and be able to express it more clearly with his own technical terms / than I shall do: Nevertheless, I will not skip over it entirely / but treat the work of the incarnation of the child in the mother's womb / specifically for the sake of those important circumstances / which lie hidden therein. Which are these three / namely:
> I. The formation of the external body / along with its parts and members.
> II. The formation of the soul / along with its life.
> III. The formation of the spirit / which moves in the life.

2. And first, indeed, we wish to begin with the house of the child in the mother's womb / namely with the body / wherein the soul and the spirit live. We wish, therefore, to consider the nature of this body / and the matter / out of which it is made. Here, the Spirit of the World first gives the command to the planet <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: "Saturno"; in medical astrology, Saturn was often associated with the first month of pregnancy and the consolidation of matter</note> under Him to take the blood of the mother / and to mix the same with the congealed seeds or natures: and then the spirit of the elements / or their more spiritual part / also mixes itself into the blood; and thereby a fleshy body is formed and joined together by elemental influences. For they mix and mingle themselves so long with—

This happens in the third month: and according to whether this planet / or the property and power of nature / is directed well or poorly at such a time / the condition or the essence of the blood is much changed.

# The VII. Chapter.
## The Sixth Day's Work
### The second vivification or breathing-in of spirit by the spirit of this world.

1. In the life of this outer soul, there lies hidden, according to its potential, a life-giving spirit; which must be the agent working therein and moving itself, to order and govern the life that is implanted into the heart. Since the spirit of the great world <note>The "spirit of the great world" refers to the *Spiritus Mundi* or macrocosmic spirit, which in Hermetic philosophy acts as the intermediary between the celestial and physical realms.</note> knows this well enough, it takes the living seeds, which now actually have a life in themselves, into its own power: to work further in them, and through its art to draw out a spirit from the life that lies hidden within.

2. And it does this by mixing its spiritual property, which is full of spirit, with those mixed seeds of life; and after the life of the soul is fully impregnated with the working property of the spirit of this world, a life is generated there—namely, in the life of the natural soul, a life-giving spirit is generated.

3. But when I say that the spirit of the world is such a spirit that is "full of spirit," I mean nothing more than that this spirit, being impregnated within itself with living power and reality, moves and works incessantly in its own working torment <note>The original word "Quall" (modern *Qual*) in the context of writers like Jacob Boehme often carries a dual meaning: both "torment" or "agitation" and "source-fountain" (from *Quelle*), referring to the dynamic tension of life.</note>. Therefore, when it qualifies the life with its living and active power, and mixes its own life with the soul-life, an active spirit is born in the life, to be the living governor and ruler of the same.

4. Now we have a fortunate trinity; here is a trinity in unity and union with one another in the mother’s womb; one body, one soul, and one spirit inseparably united in and through one another. Here is the flesh-body, the outer cabinet; here is the precious jewel and gem, the soul hidden in the body, and yet distinct from the body; 
<meta>catchword: distinguished</meta>

---

<page-num>237</page-num>
<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures &c.</header>
distinguished; and here is the most precious pearl, the spirit, which is hidden in the life of the soul; which is not the life itself, but distinct from it: although it lives in the same, and moves and works within it.

5. Just as the living soul is placed in the heart, so the spirit of life is placed in the head. Since the head and brain, together with the internal and external senses, are placed in the highest part of the body, the star-spirit <note>original: "Stern-Geist." This refers to the Sidereal Spirit, the astral body that mediates between the eternal soul and the physical flesh.</note> sets this active spirit of life therein, as the most convenient place for its working and movement. Therefore the spirit is placed in the brain as in its own proper seat, so that from there the spirits shall flow off to all parts of the body, as from a well and spring through all its instruments. For since it has its seat here, it is spread out through its own working virtue and powerful operation, and, by means of the nerves, is led through the whole body, as they (the nerves) are prepared as its channels and conduits. Hence the spirit of sensation and motion in all living creatures proceeds from the brain as from its origin.

6. This spirit is now called by various names: such as the <term>animal spirit</term> <note>original: "Animalische... Geist." In early modern medicine, "animal spirits" were not "spirits" in the religious sense, but a subtle fluid or gas that traveled through the nerves to facilitate movement and thought.</note>, creaturely, living and animal spirit, the sensual spirit, and the moving spirit. It is called the animal spirit because it is one and the same in all living creatures or animals. It is called the sensual spirit because it is the seeing spirit in the eye, the hearing spirit in the ear, and so forth. It is called the moving spirit because it is the moving power in the movement of the muscles <note>original: "Fleisch-Mäusen" (literally "flesh-mice"), an archaic German term for muscles, perhaps due to their shape under the skin.</note>, which move from internal desire; just as these are excited by an external object; and thus, through the outer senses, communicated to the inner common sense of the fantasy and imagination.

7. In all irrational creatures or animals, their movements follow immediately in this manner: The moving spirit pulls the muscles or

8. Now, if the child were to be born here from the body of its mother, having only a body with its proper parts and mechanical limbs and faculties, together with a soul implanted in the heart and an active spirit in the brain, it would therefore be born a completely irrational animal, and a purely dumb beast. For up to this point, there is no difference between a rational and an irrational animal—between a human and a beast—in their origin. For which reason the spirit of the world does not wish to bring its work only thus far and leave it stuck there, but rather to proceed with it until its full restoration and completion.

9. For because it sees that the work has begun, and has proceeded thus far successfully in its hand, it intends to proceed with it to the highest perfection of nature: and so we wish likewise to follow on its heels. And if anyone should ask me why the spirit of the world should not now restrain its activity? Or wherein lies the necessity of this its progress from irrationality to rationality? Then I would immediately ask such a person whether he thinks that the spirit of this world is not a rational spirit, and that it does not know what or wherein it works? Oh no: It has not forgotten the lesson given to it in **Genesis 1:24 & 25** <note>original: "Gen. 1 v 24 & 25"</note>. For God said to the spirit of this world: **Let the earth bring forth living creatures according to their kind, cattle and creeping things, and the beasts of the earth according to their kind.** <note>original: "die Erde bring hervor die lebendigen Creaturen nach ihrer Arth / Vieh und Gewürme / und die Thiere der Erden nach ihrer Arth."</note> Beside which scriptural passage, this spirit of the world also knows, through long-lasting experience, that every thing aims for and brings forth its own kind, such as a dog a dog, a lion a...

<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc.</header>
<sig>Q 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: Be</meta>

...lion, etc. and thus it must likewise be in the rational kind.

10. When it therefore works in irrational creatures and, with its life-spirit, qualifies and effects their mixed seeds, it knows well that there is nothing more for it to work therein; and consequently it must not go further once it has come so far. There is nothing further for it to do there but to bring forth an animal in its kind, according to its seed: for it cannot work beyond the line of natural nature. And therefore, when it knows that the matter in which it works is a human seed, and that a rational spirit lies hidden in those mixed seeds, it does not cease, but works on until it finally brings forth a rational animal from it. And thus man generates and brings forth his own kind and true image just as other animals do, which are not capable of reason. But how the rational spirit of man is distinguished from the soul and spirit of the beast, and how it comes to and attains its rationality, must be the content of our next following investigation.

# The VIII. Chapter.
## The Seventh Day's Work.
### Of the Origin of Human Reason.

1. The spirit of the great world needs no instruction from man to teach it that the rational spirit, according to its power and faculty, lies actually hidden in the mixed seed of the parents; and that nothing else is lacking there than that it qualify, awaken, or bring it forth with its influences <note>The "influences" (*Influentzen*) refer to the astrological or macrocosmic forces believed to act upon earthly matter.</note>, and thus give it an actual birth, and make the child into a rational creature. It has not forgotten the command of God to Noah in **Genesis 9:1** <note>original: "Gen. 9 v 1"</note>, when He blessed Noah and his sons, and said to them:

### Be fruitful and multiply / and fill the earth. Wherein this appears to be included.

2. The spirit of this world was present and heard it / when this command and blessing was given by God to Noah and his children. Note / the blessing consisted in their fruitfulness and multiplication. Noah / his sons and the spirit of the world understood the sense and meaning of these words well enough. What kind of fruitfulness and multiplication is it then / that they understood? It is clear / that they thought they were commanded by God to fill the earth with their kind: they well understood / that by virtue of this they were not sent or commanded to fill the earth with irrational creatures / but to bring forth those who were rational and like unto themselves / and thus to bring forth out of themselves rational creatures / in their own image and likeness; for in any other way / according to any proper speech / they could not have generated in their kind or fathered children.

3. They knew well / and thus the spirit of this world also knew / that the root / source and origin of this fruitfulness and multiplication was in themselves / namely in their own natural seed / planted therein by God himself: They knew it was His Divine will / that it should be internally and essentially in their own inherent tinctures <note>In the philosophy of Jacob Böhme and his followers, a "tincture" is the spiritual essence or vital energy that gives life and quality to a physical substance.</note> and spiritual seeds / inwardly within themselves. They knew well / that God had not commanded them an impossible work / or that they should do that which was not in their power and ability to perform.

4. And in this way, we have come to the rational spirit of the child, as it is sown and brought forth into nature through the assistance of the Spirit of this world, in union with the spiritual principles that lie hidden within the mixed seeds.

# The Fourth Little Treatise on Union

## Chapter X.
## The Ninth Day’s Work:
### On the Birth of the Child.

1. Now we have come to the complete end of the ninth month, to the precise and proper time for the full birth of the child from the mother’s womb into this outer world. At this birth, a great gathering occurs; for present here are not only the earthly parents, along with their relatives and neighbors, but also the heavenly ones, who can claim just as much ownership of the child as the earthly ones. For the seeds could not have generated or produced a child without the influences of the heavens, just as the heavens could not have done so without the proper mixing and preservation of the seeds. Thus, it must be granted to both the heavenly and the earthly powers, as they stand in a close union of parental kinship with the newborn child.

2. Here, at this birth, the spirit of this world and all the planets come together, and the constellations stand in their order within their "star-wheel" <note>original: "Stern-Rade," referring to the rotating wheel of the zodiac or the celestial spheres</note>. And here, the spirit of the world calculates the state and condition of the newborn child. At this very time, they all see—within their struggling wheel or sphere—which among them manages to become the child’s <term>Ascendent</term> <note>The astrological sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth, believed to govern personality and fate</note>; as well as his goal and end, how long he shall live, and what notable good or bad fortune might encounter him externally from the ruler of this world, namely its spirit. And this is also observed by the "artists" <note>In this context, "artists" refers to practitioners of astrology and the natural sciences</note> according to the manifold positions of all seven properties of natural Nature, seen in the seven High Regents <note>The seven classical planets: Sun, Moon, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn</note> of this world, directly under their great spirit. However, those who desire to know more of this are directed to those whose profession it is, especially to the more learned and understanding among them.

3. Accordingly, let us consider what the end and

---

<page-num>249</page-num>
<header>Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc.</header>

gathering of all this solemn assembly might be. Let us take this newborn child into our arms and consider what we see, and reflect upon what is so worthy of great wonder therein. We see that the child has come forth as a perfect birth from the <term>womb</term> <note>original: "Behrmutter," literally "bearing-mother"</note>: he has come into this world with a body of flesh, whose parts and members are all set in their proper place and position; furthermore, he comes forth with a living soul planted into the heart, which makes him a living creature; and so that he may finally be made complete, he also brings with him a rational spirit, which is placed in the brain, together with a mind, will, and senses, through which he is made a rational creature. This is the highest point of the child’s perfection as far as the outer life under the government of the spirit of the great world is concerned.

4. Therefore, the perfection—indeed, even the highest perfection—of the outer man depends on the spirit of rationality. Through this, he is more excellent than all the beasts of the field, more subtle and cunning than any of them, a greater discerner of good and evil, wiser and more understanding in the out-birth of nature, and more skillful in taming, governing, and subduing the other creatures; and thus to rule over the fish in the sea, over the birds of the air, and over the beasts of the earth, as a mighty lord under the spirit of this world, in the same way as this spirit is under God. Through this rational spirit, he stands above all species of animals in this visible world: through this, he is more closely related and dear to the great spirit of this world, because he is brought forth after his image. And considering that he thus carries more of his image upon himself than all other creatures, he cares for the rational man and provides for him more than all others. Also, through the reason of the arts and sciences, the outer rational man is capable and has an understanding mind, which no other animal or creature in the visible creation has besides him: through these...
