this <note>referring to "reason," which was the subject of the preceding sentence on the previous page</note> can explore and discover the hidden wonders and mysteries of internal nature, the multitude of which is exceedingly great. Through this, I say, he is capable of all the liberal arts and academic sciences, and is furthermore also fit to learn the principal or original languages. Through this, he can look upward toward the heavens and grasp the movement and order of the stars: and thus he becomes a **mathematician**, an **astronomer**, and an **astrologer**. Through this, he is capable of searching into the secrets of the earth and discovering veins of gold and silver; and thus, when he applies art to labor, he becomes a **chemist** <note>original: "Chymiste"; in the 17th century, this term encompassed both chemistry and alchemy</note>. He looks into the nature of plants, animals, and minerals: and so he shall be a **naturalist**, a **surgeon**, and an **apothecary**. Namely, according to how this spirit of reason is variously impregnated by his great Father, in union with the heavenly influences through the planetary wheel of natural nature. Indeed, through this subtlety of reason in his mind, he can even understand things that are separated and free from matter: and thus becomes a **metaphysician**, that is, one who explores things that are above nature. Through this, he seeks to grasp what angels and spirits are, and grasps both according to his own kind: He can attain a great degree of **knowledge** regarding eternal and invisible things, by means of and through the help of his ideas and images, and his abstractions `(*)` and compositions `(†)`. Also, with the help of his interpreters, he can penetrate the sense and meaning of the letter of the Scripture, and through his degrees at the universities, make himself into a theologian or a prophet, even if the Divine life and the nature of God have not yet been born within him. Indeed, through this rational power, which is implanted in his mind by the spirit of the world, he may not only recognize all things outside of himself, but may furthermore—through the reflection of the rays of his reason into himself—attain to a knowledge of himself, and know everything about himself that is written here.

7. For we must know that within the spirit of reason is contained a principle of conscientiousness and self-knowledge. And through this, he may recognize that which he actually recognizes, and understand that which he understands. And through this, he has a power to will and not to will, to choose and to reject.

# The Fourth Little Treatise on Union

[page 252]

The dignity of the **external man**, who is also the **man of reason**, along with his superiority over all other creatures in this visible world, depends upon his rational faculty: just as this faculty itself depends upon the **Spirit of this world** and the **astral influences** of the heavens. Thus, man is born in the image of this spirit and comes into the world as a very small and subtle creature who, by the power of his inquisitive reason, penetrates into all the wonders that are under the sun. Through this reason, he interacts with his fellow creatures, who are of the same kind and nature as he is; they deal with one another through its help, wherein the subtle cunning of reason, with its searching wit, is made chiefly visible. For there, no one can either buy or sell unless he has the **mark of the world-spirit**. <note>This is an allusion to the "Mark of the Beast" from the Book of Revelation, here applied philosophically to signify that one cannot participate in worldly commerce or society without the "currency" of worldly reason.</note>

9. In this way, you see why the external man, the man of reason, is so closely related, so dear, and so precious to the spirit of the stars and elements. This is because he is a rational spirit, just as the world-spirit is, and is generated and born in its likeness; indeed, he is born a subject under its government. Therefore, his birth and end also depend upon its dominion.

10. But here I fear that an objection will be raised against what has been written and asserted thus far: Why do you say that reason is generated and born in the image of the spirit of this world, and not rather in the **Image of God**? What foundation and ground can this strange claim have, when you wish to take away this most noble image from the man of reason? To which I answer, that it is no strange claim or presumption of a singularity or novelty, but the pure truth. For there can hardly be a more dangerous error in the world than to assert that human reason carries within itself the most noble Image of God, or the living impression of the Divine Being, according to which man was created in his paradisiacal state of in-

<margin>R</margin>
<meta>catchword: nocence</meta>

***

[page 253]

->### Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc.<-

-nocence. Let us remember that **God’s Image** is a heavenly image that comes immediately from God Himself; or a Divine mark that is imprinted into the human essences. On the other hand, the image of reason, when compared with the former, is an earthly image; it is made for this earth of ours and comes not immediately from God, but from the Spirit of the World. It has only imprinted a human mark into the animal essences in the external man, so as to distinguish him from the irrational cattle. For God’s heavenly image in Adam consisted just as little in "art" and reason as it does in the angels. In heaven, one has no need of art or reason; likewise, there is neither art nor reason in God’s Image. Rather, after Adam had lost the Divine Image within him, the Spirit of this world gave Adam art and reason, so that they might serve him in place of the Image of God which was lost.

11. Thus, art and reason are now the **image of the Fall**, given to the external man of reason by the spirit of this visible world, which is a very active, rational spirit. Accordingly, the spirit of reason in man is just as truly a sign or mark of our fallen state from God as these visible plants of the earth and these coarse and mortal bodies are the signs of our fall from our first paradisiacal life. Alas! The Image of God is something quite different; it is given to the **inner man** immediately by God, who is an eternal, immortal man, the **hidden man**, born and concealed within the external humanity.

12. The Image of God, therefore, which belongs to the inner man, consists in a Divine and Heavenly life that proceeds immediately from the fullness of His Divinity. Through this, the spirit of the inner man attains to becoming one life, one nature, and one spirit with God, in the same way that the external man, through the life of...

of reason, usually becomes one life, one nature, and one spirit with the spirit of this world.

<margin>Clusters of red dots marking the paragraph</margin>
13. In this way, we see clearly and distinctly that, although the **external man** may indeed be made into a rational human being by the stars and constellations, this still cannot cause him to be in the likeness and **Image of the Divine Being**, which is above reason. The introduction of this Divine Image is, beyond all contradiction, above the power of the **Spirit of the World**, which works through external heavenly influences. This worldly spirit exerts itself to the utmost to bring reason into the soul <note>original: "Gemühte" — a term often used in German mysticism to describe the seat of the emotions, will, and thoughts, broader than the modern "mind."</note>, will, and senses. Therefore, the rational spirit of man—because it is influenced or brought about by the spirit of the stars and elements—can desire nothing other than the kingdom of this world. The reason of his soul and will moves and drives him to strive for glory, wealth, and beauty in this visible creation; or to seek art and knowledge, and to find the magnificence and excellence of earthly wonders in this external nature. From this rational soul of the external humanity, therefore, proceed lies and folly, falsehood and deceit.

<margin>Clusters of red dots marking the paragraph</margin>
14. And what will it matter, even if reason investigates the **essences** <note>In this context, "essence" refers to the fundamental nature or "inner juice" of a thing that gives it its properties.</note> or beings and qualities of the stars and elements, and can fathom the wonders of external nature up in the heights—as in the region of the star-elements—and the ground of all things in the depths below? What use and service will it be to one who is alienated from the **Principle of Divine Life** and Nature within himself? Despite all that knowledge, it may be no better than an **animal life**. For even reason itself, in the soul and will of the external man, cannot be distinguished from that of an animal if he is subjected to the earthly and worldly life through the dominion of the stellar powers. And to speak in clear and distinct words: the natural man, even in following the highest **Form of Religion**,

<meta>catchword: Form/</meta>

***

# Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc. 255

<margin>Clusters of red dots marking the paragraph</margin>
[religious] Form, can never attain to that which is true religion in the sight of God. He may indeed be an honorable, virtuous man, but he is no holy and divine man. Indeed, even in the greatest exaltation of rationality, and in its clearest proofs and demonstrations, he may nevertheless stand before God as nothing other than a cunning and deceitful animal.

<margin>Clusters of red dots marking the paragraph</margin>
15. For see and know that this external visible world has a rational life within it, which is revealed only in the children of men. Know likewise that this external rational life has no higher understanding than the **essence** and the being from which it originates <note>original: "urständet" — to have one's origin or 'original standing' in something.</note>; and thus, it can reach no further than the thing in which it dwells. Since the spirit of reason in man proceeds from the active power of the Spirit of this World—through the stars and elements—and dwells therein, it cannot know nor recognize what is outside of them. It cannot know what is in the invisible worlds, and can have no communion with them, or with those who lie beyond them in the immeasurable spaces of eternity. It cannot become spiritual and like the **Godhead**. This is reserved solely for the **inner man**, who dwells within the outer.

<margin>Clusters of red dots marking the paragraph</margin>
16. Therefore, let human reason not exalt itself so highly nor boast, nor puff itself up in its own concepts and imaginings. For you should know that it was never the **Image of God**; nor is it the Image of God now, nor will it ever be the Image of God. For it is an image that proceeds solely from the Spirit of this World. All its sharpness, wit, and subtlety come not from the Spirit of Eternity or from the eternal world—through the immediate proceeding of the **Divine Word** to bring forth His own likeness—but rather from the Spirit of this World and from those external **planetary powers**. And for that reason, it naturally tends to incline the soul and the will away from the Eternal Unity and from the eternal Godhead, and toward the Spirit of this World.

# 256 The Fourth Little Treatise on Union

...to pursue the world; considering that it cannot rise above its origin: for which reason it is not to be trusted, nor relied upon.

<margin>17. +</margin> The conclusion of all that has been said concerning the nature and origin of the **external man**—as he consists of a body, soul, and spirit—is this: The <term>Rational Man</term> <note>original: "Vernunfft-Mensch" — refers to the human being as guided solely by earthly logic and sensory intellect.</note> or the external man cannot inherit the Kingdom of God. No matter how highly he may be ranked or gifted with a life of rationality in his active mind and understanding, he is nonetheless incapable of eternity, nor is he prepared for it. Rather, it is the **inward man**, with his body, soul, and spirit, to whom this lot belongs and can alone belong; for he is the true son of the **Divine Nature** in its first production or bringing forth. He is from and of eternity, and is thereby prepared once again for the same.

<margin>18. *</margin> Accordingly, allow me now to address those of you who seek the noble <term>Philosopher’s Stone</term> <note>The "Stein der Weisen" here is used as a metaphor for spiritual transformation and the perfection of the soul, common in Christian alchemical thought.</note>. I wish that in the forming of your "stone," you possessed as much knowledge to give it a body, soul, and spirit as the spirit of this world has in giving a body, soul, and spirit to the mingled seeds. Then you would rejoice over the birth of your Stone, just as the worldly spirit does over every child of human nature that it brings forth into this visible creation.

<margin>19. +</margin> We have now sufficiently considered the birth and **Nativity** (†), the perfection and excellence, and especially the rationality of the external mortal man. We have shown how all his glory and perfection lie in the fact that he is born into the lineage of the children of men as a rational creature. Here, then, is a great gathering and assembly. Do you not see their great joy and exultation? Let us, therefore, go forward and investigate its grounds and causes.

(†) <note>original: "Nativität" — refers to the "nativity" or birth chart, which is the state or position of the stars in the outer heavens at the time and hour of birth.</note>

## Chapter XI.
### The Tenth Day’s Work:
->Of the Joy of both the Heavenly and Earthly Parents over his Birth.<-

<margin>1.</margin> The joy of parents at the birth of a child into this visible world arises from the following causes: (1) Because everyone in the line of natural nature desires to see the fruit of their labor and to behold the work of their hands. Therefore, they rejoice over the birth because it is an external sign and token of the fertility of them both: a sign that neither the mother’s womb was barren, nor the father's body dead. (2) Because a creature is brought into the world in their own image and after their likeness of nature; properly proportioned or shaped in all its parts, and gifted with a living soul and spirit from the soul and spirit of the **Great World** <note>original: "grossen Welt" — likely referring to the Macrocosm or the universe as a living whole.</note>. (3) Because they have a branch and an offspring from their own bowels, from their own **essences**, and from their mingled seeds of life, through their joint action and unanimous consent in the line of natural generation. To this offspring, they can leave their goods, to propagate their names on the earth and build them a house; so that, although they themselves are mortal, they may nonetheless attain a kind of immortality through this, as long as their memory is preserved in their seed after them, and their descendants rise up in their place.

<margin>2.</margin> In and over this, there is indeed great joy; yet it is almost weighed down and dampened because the parent does not know whether he will leave his labor to a wise or a foolish child; whether to a sober and modest son, or a profligate one. For this was also the concern of the spirit of Solomon, according to **Ecclesiastes 2:18-19**. When he thought of the one who should be his successor, it caused him to break out in these words...

3. But besides these reasons, which have already been mentioned and are common or known to all, there remains a further ground and cause for joy over the birth of the external, visible man, which is a special and uncommon one. And such is this: that because most people do not direct their eye higher than the power and capacity of nature; others, being somewhat more God-fearing and devout, rejoice over the birth of the child as a blessing from God. Therefore, the parents say that the children, through God’s cooperating mercy and blessing, are from themselves; and the wise <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophi" — referring to natural philosophers or scientists of the era who studied the mechanics of the physical world.</note> say that they come from the spirit of this world as God's instruments. But all must confess that the Divine blessing is everything in all of this, and that the fertility of the womb or the body originally comes from God. Which is certainly a deeper ground for joy than any of the preceding ones. But even this is not yet everything.

4. For finally, and for the fourth and last part, the enlightened Christian will rejoice over the birth of the visible child in the consideration that a miracle of God is brought into the world. Such a person cannot do otherwise than marvel at the Divine wisdom, power, and goodness in this production; which in itself is so truly astonishing that it far surpasses the understanding of even the greatest philosophers to fully fathom and comprehend. He will marvel within himself, and marvel again to see such a wonderful creature brought into the world, as David says: You have possessed my reins. You have covered me

For which reason this little treatise is especially written; so that this eternal man may be distinguished from that other, whose birth and existence is only in time. 7. Now it remains for us to also search a little for the final causes <note>The "final cause" is a philosophical term referring to the ultimate purpose or "end goal" for which something exists.</note> of the outer Heavenly parents, and to consider why the Spirit of this World, along with the planets and constellations, rejoices so greatly over or at the birth of every rational child. And these causes are as follows:

8. The First Cause may be because, when a rational creature or human is born, the Spirit of the World looks upon them as a child brought forth in its own likeness, and as one who carries its image in a much more vivid way than the plants of the earth or irrational creatures do. And it considers that this is a child generated out of its own inward parts, and a son of the stars, who has been generated through them in the work of natural procreation. For since it did not lie in the power of the natural parents to have brought a birth into the world in a natural way without it, the Spirit claims a right and ownership to the newborn child just as much as the natural parents do. And we shall see it make its claim in such or a similar manner: „Greetings to you, my dear child, born of my essences, conceived through my stellar influences, and brought forth through my natural powers and capacities, which have illuminated, penetrated, and qualified—or worked and prepared—the hidden seeds of your earthly parents with their rays. Your fleshly body is curdled together from my elements; and all your parts are set, formed, and fashioned in their proper place by my planets. Your soul—namely, your living soul—from whom else is it but from me? I, under the Spirit of Eternity, have made you into a living creature; I have endowed and quickened you with the life of this outer world, my kingdom; and now you live and draw breath in me. Therefore you are mine: and you shall be mine for all the appoint-

<meta>catchword: ed</meta>

,,ed days of your natural life. But further, because you are made into a living soul in my principle, you are thereby made equal to me, your Father, who am a living worker. Otherwise, I could not impregnate the sun, as I do, with a life-spirit to make all things living. Because you are now thus made into a rational spirit, you resemble me even more. This ensures that you bear my image with a testimony. For I am also a rational spirit, with a will and a mind of my own. My government in the world shall bear witness to this, along with all emperors, kings, and high authorities under me; besides my various arts, sciences, and vocations within the sphere of my dominions, for the preservation of all my subjects. And if I had not impregnated both your mind and will with the power of my rational capacity, you could never have become a rational creature, as you now actually are, because I have sown the seeds of reason into you. Nor could I have claimed you for myself as my firstborn son, or made any claim on that account, nor could I have been your true father. But I have done all this for you: therefore you are my son [or my daughter] and I will rejoice over you.“ Accordingly, the wise <term>Philosopher</term> <note>Referring to practitioners of "Natural Philosophy," the precursor to modern science which blended physics, astrology, and alchemy.</note> concludes that the visible child is a true son [or daughter] born as much of the stars as of the natural parents; and that it is generated and produced by them both through transmission <note>original: "ex Traduce" — refers to Traducianism, a doctrine suggesting that the soul is transmitted from the parents to the child through the natural process of generation, rather than being a new creation by God.</note> or through procreation, and thus belongs to both in common union.

For because the Spirit of this world is a rational spirit, it understands very well that its kingdom would soon come to an end through the death of one person after another, unless they...

10. A third cause and reason why the Spirit of this World rejoices over the birth of every visible child is because its kingdom is not only propagated thereby, but is propagated with such subjects as are bound to render it obedience. For every visible, rational creature is born into and for subjection, and stands under the government of the Spirit of the Stars: which, when it sees that a new subject is born to it with every such birth, and its kingdom is thereby filled—not with rebels and traitors, but with good and faithful subjects—is full of joy over this. Therefore, we should constantly remember that the Spirit of the Stars and Elements <note>In this mystical worldview, the "Spirit of the Stars and Elements" refers to the natural forces of the universe that govern physical life and the human body.</note> is the Chief Regent over all the children of men under God, insofar as they are rational and visible creatures: and that, wherever they may be, they are given to it by God, one like the other, to rule over them as its subjects; whose government is truly monarchical.

11. There has always been a great dispute as to whether the monarchical form of government exists by Divine Right or not. However, monarchical government is undoubtedly the best in the whole world according to the Light of Nature <note>The "Light of Nature" (Lumen Naturae) is a term used by Paracelsus and Jacob Böhme to describe the innate knowledge or wisdom found within the natural world, as opposed to direct divine revelation.</note>. For if you look up toward the Eternal World, there is only one single God who reigns there, namely the Spirit of Eternity. If you look down toward the dark world, called Hell; then there is, I say, in that same kingdom of darkness, also only one highest Chief Regent under God, namely the Spirit of fiery wrath <note>German: *feuriger Grimm*. This refers to the harsh, judging, or consuming aspect of the divine nature often associated with the "first principle" in Boehmian theosophy.</note>. If you look toward the light eternity, there is likewise only one highest Sovereign under God, namely the Spirit of His Eternal Love; in which kingdom all the wonders of God have become self-standing and essential. And finally, if you look at this lowest world, this our visible globe, there is also here only one highest Head and Regent

## The I. Chapter.
### Introduction and what gave the Author cause for these meditations.

<margin>1.</margin>In the year 1671, when I was beset by great temp-

## Chapter II.

->On the Union of Spiritual Tinctures.<-

1. There is a pure and Divine desire in the tincture to be nourished and maintained by those who are accustomed to give it: and to be cherished and warmed by that mother-womb of life and essence of love from which it was separated.

2. And a similar desire exists also in the transmitter or giver of the tincture / to nourish the seed of life in his spiritual child / to keep it warm and to refresh it. And by both of these desires of the one toward the other / the union of the tinctures is promoted.

3. This union may now be promoted with impurity and mixing; or else it may be performed in all purity / free from the mixing of nature / and from every kind of lower inclination toward one another.

<column-break/>

# 267
# Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures etc.
<meta>catchword: none</meta>

4. From these two inward attractions or yearnings of the tincture / because they are in two persons / there must necessarily follow a drawing together of the persons / in whom the seed of spiritual life truly lives / for mutual preservation and nourishment.

5. From this longing attraction or drawing together of the persons follows this spiritual relationship of the father and child. The father is the giver / sustainer / and multiplier of the love-seed; the child is the person who receives it: and must remain so until they can nourish and make themselves alive.

6. From the drawing together of the persons follows the union of the persons: and in this union consists the relational bond between father and son.

7. This union of the tinctures and persons enjoyed in such a way / is a pure union; and surpasses the union of the flesh incomparably far: it is not fleshly / but spiritual; not of man / but of God; and can be enjoyed only in God / and for God's sake.

8. Worldly unions are from and through the will of men / and for their own sakes: but the union of the tinctures is heavenly and from heaven / and happens purely through the will of God himself. For the tincture is God's gift; which is given freely and for nothing into those whom it pleases Him to give it / and how and when it pleases Him: and therefore it must be holy / as God is holy; also pure and heavenly / just as He is / without the slightest blemish of the flesh or contamination of the earth.

9. The true and supernatural ground of the spiritual union of persons is the mutual inflow and outflow of the tincture from person to person. The giving of the tincture to another is only a half-union: but the return flow back again makes a full and perfect union.

10. The ground of this union of two persons...

14. And thus the various faculties and instruments of the body may be used to serve the righteousness and Heavenly-Health of the tincture : just as they were previously used to serve sin in the flesh. <note>original: "Rom. 6 v 13"</note> Romans 6:13.

## Chapter III.

### Of the Advantages / or also Disadvantages / that arise from the Fellowship of Tinctures.

1. From this fellowship of the tincture now toward the union of persons / not only internally and invisibly / but also externally and visibly / by means of the bodily instruments (such as through the laying on of hands or the like) / one will be able to have great enjoyment and advantage from the other / through the communal influence of the **Holy Flame** of the Spirit from the **Central-Fire** in the heart: From this will arise Spiritual and Heavenly Discourses / with prayer and thanksgivings / also openings of the inner and Eternal Worlds; according to the quality or proportion of the Divine Gift in each one / as it is hidden in the tincture <note>The "tincture" is viewed here as a concentrated spiritual essence or "soul-medicine" that carries divine qualities.</note>.

2. And thus they will / in this communal enjoyment of one another / in the Heavenly Tincture of pure love / sometimes discourse among themselves of the nature of this union; and of the communion of the saints / just as in heaven; so also in and upon the earth; and of the joy of the Holy Spirit in giving and receiving this Divine Tincture from- and to vessels prepared and made fit for it. At other times they will entertain one another with the different degrees of this Spiritual Union and fellowship: also of the various enjoyments / and benefits / as well as dangers / that are common to them. Again / in these alternations they will sometimes strengthen and lift one another up in their different works and lots / and according to the degree of the Holy Tincture of Divine Wisdom in them: and will especially ask and answer one another concerning the union of natures / persons and spirits; also taking care / that they do not confuse one with the other. Again at other times they will marvel at the Divine Goodness and in their Communal Union in Christ

### Chapter IV.
#### Of the Union of Spirits / and what great prudence is necessary to be used therein.

1. Just as Eve gave Adam the apple / and yet did not recognize the effect of the apple until it was eaten; so may it likewise happen here in this case. The apple here is the union of spirits / that is / in the state of mixture and imperfection; the cunning serpent uses the spirit of the weaker one to seek the union of spirits; and the stronger one is soon moved to consent to it. The weakest in spirit presents that beauty of the apple / the beauty and sweetness of the union of spirits; they seek the same earnestly; and yet do not know the dangerous effect of it. For the cunning of the serpent is just as little noticed in such a temptation of one / who tempts the other with this golden apple of the union of the spirit / as Eve noticed or understood it / when she tempted Adam. The actual eating of the apple is the real surrender of their spirits, the one to the other / because or as long as they are still in the state of mixture <note>A state where the divine and the worldly/corrupt elements are still mingled and not yet separated by purification.</note>; through their own free will / and not through the will of God: in such a way as to become one spirit out of two / in the mixture of good and evil; and through this they lose / by their voluntary action and deed / their paradisiacal crown. For in this way the formation of the <term>Philosopher’s Stone</term> is forfeited: and <term>Sophia</term> <note>Divine Wisdom, often viewed in this tradition as the spiritual "bride" of the soul.</note>, the bride of the souls / is poured out.

2. Thereupon I saw further / that by resisting this temptation of the serpent in the spirit of Eve, or / in the lower <term>Essence</term> of the soul / and by not

### Chapter V.

->Regarding Personal Union; and regarding the<-
->necessary warnings therein, together with<-
->the nature, as well as the manner and way<-
->of its cessation.<-

1. Personal Union depends on the union of hearts, and this depends on the <term>Tincture</term> <note>In spiritual alchemy, the Tincture is the essential, transformative life-force or soul-energy that flows between beings</note>, and the same depends on the will of the free Worker, who is the Mover. Which free Agent or Worker is the Divine Spirit, who works in and through the human will, as long as it continues to press forward to deny itself entirely for the sake of God’s and Christ’s will.
2. Spiritual union (in the highest sense) depends on the union of the Angels, this on the Divine Nature, and the same on the Divine Essence, as the origin and root of the Eternal Unity of life, in the Angelic world, and the world-round or Sphere of Eternity.
3. From the Divine Essence flows the Divine Nature: and from the Divine Nature flows the Tincture.
4. Which Tincture, although it is itself unstained, nevertheless may enter into such vessels, and flow through them

---

# 275 Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc.

which are still stained. And there is thus no small danger in how this heavenly gift is to be guarded, so that one may not lose it!
5. The cessation or lack of flow of the Tincture from one to another hinders the Personal Union: and this cannot be enjoyed where the same does not flow. Therefore, when the Tincture flows from one person to the other and mutually through each other, the personal union must remain, regardless of all external distance of dwelling: but if the same does not flow, then the Personal Union must cease, regardless of all external proximity and intimacy.
6. Although in the common flow and counter-flow of the Tincture for personal unions, there is actually an advantage in external proximity and the conveniences of a more intimate interaction. Which, therefore, are not to be neglected or despised, where one can have such conveniently and without falling into greater danger.
7. Nevertheless, there is no greater danger than entering into a personal union with someone through a betrothal to one another; because it stands in the power of God alone to make the Tincture flow, and not in the power of the Christian who possesses it.
8. For which reason, one should certainly not make or enter into any such vows <note>original: "Gelübde"</note> that we do not have the power to keep, but which God has the power to break: and if they have been made or entered into, one should have repentance and sorrow over them and leave them. The creature should take care that it does not tempt God! We must not trust in our own strength beyond our ability: the Tincture is God's, not our own. No one can bind themselves to that which stands in the communion of the Tincture, and consequently depends on God alone. Therefore, one should not bind oneself to such personal unions through holy or solemn vows.
9. Personal unions that stand in spirituality (whose spirituality depends on common connections and the flowing of the Tincture...

<margin>*</margin>just as the flowings of the <term>Tincture</term> <note>In this mystical context, the Tincture is the vital, transformative life-force or spiritual energy that flows from the Divine through the soul</note> depend upon the will of God, who moves within them; when these unions are made by Christians in their ignorance, they are not in and of themselves binding. And all vows of Personal Union must be determined by the will of God; otherwise, they are evil, sinful, and unauthorized.

<margin>*</margin>10. For just as God previously called Abraham out of his natural fatherland and away from all his friends and relatives, so He may even now call some to leave father, mother, wife, children, house, and everything that belongs to this world; yes, even their spiritual parents and obligations, and all those lovely enjoyments of the circulation of the Tincture from heart to heart.

<margin>*</margin>11. And again, just as God tested Abraham with the sacrifice of his only son, so He may now test some in the same way. For here, the Heavenly Tincture with its out-flowings is the soul's true Isaac; it is its lily and its unique pearl; it is the heir of the promise; it is the life of its life, the paradise of its soul, and the heart of its heart. Nothing is closer to it, nothing dearer than this.

<margin>*</margin>And to truly give up such a union and such a fellowship as this—which consists in the communal outflow and inflow of the Tincture—will touch the very life of the spiritual selfhood <note>original: "Selbheit," referring to the ego or the individual's sense of self-will that must be surrendered to God</note>.

<margin>*</margin>12. Nevertheless, it must happen, and one must obey the will of God. All those who are thus tempted and tested must be undaunted in binding the sacrifice and laying their hand to the knife of separation, no matter how firmly the other person may try to hold on. There must be a breaking of all will, and a breaking of all bonds or covenants, and of all spiritual kinships, obligations, and alliances, as soon as it becomes clear that there is a danger of falling into spiritual adultery <note>A term used by mystics to describe placing a created being or a spiritual feeling above the pure love of God</note>, the consequence of which would be most deadly.

# 277 Of Natures, Essences, Tinctures, etc.

### Regarding those two small words on Page 207, verse 3: "from the vine-shoot" <note>original Latin: "ex Traduce"</note>; it was considered necessary to note this here for the simple-minded.

This term "vine-shoot" <note>original: "Tradux"</note> actually signifies a shoot of a vine that is laid under the earth from a grapevine and thus propagated into another plant. So, "from the vine-shoot" <note>original: "ex Traduce"</note> means "through propagation." Now, because it is maintained in Romans 5:12 that sin entered the world through one man and has spread to all and into all, much dispute has arisen in the reason of both the learned and the unlearned concerning whether the soul—and consequently sin within the soul—is also propagated along with it or not? And then, how does such a thing happen? Is it through "imputation" <note>The legalistic idea that sin is "credited" to a person's account</note> alone, or through "essential propagation" <note>The idea that the substance of the soul and its corruption are physically/spiritually passed down from parents</note>? To this the answer is that it truly happens through both imputation and through actual propagation. For to say and maintain that God imputes Adam's sin to his descendants, if they were not truly partakers of the guilt of sin, would certainly require no small amount of art to excuse God from injustice. Our God does not need this; no, He does not need our sins to declare Him righteous. No, by no means. Rather, let it remain according to Romans 3:4, that God is true, but all men are liars. 

2. But when we say that sin entered through actual or essential and true propagation—as it indeed did and still does—then the scholars <note>original: "Schuhl-lehrer," literally school-teachers, referring here to academic theologians</note> want to disturb us with their questions, which are more inquisitive than useful: whether it enters through the soul, or through the body and through the flesh alone, etc.? And even the most subtle among them answer this through a hidden contradiction, or not much better, when they deny that it is transmitted or propagated through the soul, and yet say that sin is transmitted through the "working power" <note>(†) This is nothing else than the soul’s own <term>Separator</term>, that is, the cause of its creaturely movement and life.</note> of the seed.

->transmitted<-

<sig>S f</sig>

<vocab>Tradux (vine-shoot/propagation), Tincture (life-force), Soul-Separator (the internal organizing power of life), Abraham, Isaac, Imputation (legal reckoning of sin), Essential Propagation (physical/spiritual inheritance)</vocab>

<summary>This page concludes the discussion on the spiritual necessity of dissolving human unions if they interfere with God's will, using the imagery of Abraham's sacrifice. It then moves into a theological explanation of "traducianism," arguing that both the soul and original sin are propagated from parents to children through a vital spiritual "working power."</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the main text of the Fourth Treatise, focusing on the transmission of the soul and original sin through procreation, and begins the alphabetical index (Register) for the work. It continues the theological argument that while God is the ultimate source of life, the corruption of the "Image of God" in Adam leads to the propagation of a fallen nature.</meta>

# 278 The Fourth Little Treatise on Union

guided. But what is this active power in the seed other than the soul? Which is called by the Philosopher <note>Likely referring to Aristotle, whose definitions of the soul as the "active principle" of life were standard in early modern theology.</note>, whom they all follow, an active movement, a principle, and <term>cause and origin</term> <note>original Greek: αἰτία καὶ ἀρχὴ</note>. If, however, someone should answer and say that in such a way God would be the author of sin, the answer is that such a thing does not follow. For certainly man is, and may in truth be said to be, the begetter of a human being: and what is that other than to beget another equal to himself? God gave this power to man in the beginning, in Genesis 1:28. And one finds nowhere that He ever took it away from him again: for we read that Adam begot a son in his own likeness, in and after his own image. Now, he had lost the image of God; why then is it repeated: *in his own likeness, in his own image*? Dear reader, what else is indicated here than the propagation of the whole man, corrupted as he himself was? The contrast between these two expressions proves it in Genesis 5:1: *On the day that God created man, He made him after the likeness of God*; that same likeness was the beauty and purity of the soul. And that is followed in verse 3: *Adam begot a son in his own likeness, in and after his own image*, that is, according to the original defilement and impurity of the soul. For *that which is born of the flesh is flesh: and that which is born of the Spirit is spirit*, John 3:6. Psalm 51:7 <note>In many English Bibles, this is Psalm 51:5: "Behold, I was shapen in iniquity; and in sin did my mother conceive me."</note>. *Behold, I am begotten of sinful seed, and my mother conceived me in sin.* See Robert Gell in his *Remains*, Volume 2, pages 2 and 3. <note>Robert Gell (1595–1665) was an English clergyman whose "Remains" were published posthumously, containing mystical and scholarly commentaries on Scripture.</note>

->T H E   E N D.<-

# Front Cover

The volume is bound in a sturdy, aged material—most likely <term>vellum<note>A high-quality parchment made from animal skin, frequently used for durable book bindings in the early modern period.</note></term> or leather. The surface has acquired a deep, yellowish-brown patina over the centuries, with visible mottling, staining, and wear consistent with frequent handling.

While no titles or names are written on the exterior, there are faint rectangular impressions in the center of the cover. These marks may indicate where a paper label was once attached or where decorative patterns were pressed into the material, though any such details have long since faded or been worn away. Along the right edge, the stitching that binds the cover to the internal pages remains visible, showing the physical construction of the manuscript.
